Outcome and exact scope\n\nThis report jointly rederives the enlarged common-square architecture and\nall its original interpolation coefficients. The paid zero-sum column is\nzeta, whereas the actual skew divergence is\n\n    d=zeta+(p-s)(v-z).\n\nSeparating z from v changes the architecture, but does not grant free\nsign-feasible transport. The outgoing optimum vector depends on zeta,\nnot on d alone. Both the incoming total/clock moments and the complete\nfirst-row total/clock moments are derived below for GENERAL z.\n\nLaw I uses a clock-zero endpoint common column independent of v, a\nstrictly positive incoming optimum load nu=(1/64)e_0 at the INITIAL\nquery, and an explicitly scaled positive source/last/tail metric. Its full\npolynomial is PSD below the target and all optimum signs pass. Its\nfirst-row total has the required sign. Nevertheless its endpoint clock\ndefect is negative uniformly in every enclosing m>=1536, for every\nskew completion preserving the selected actions. In the ideal endpoint\nmetric, EVERY compatible endpoint incoming load cancels exactly from\nthis defect. The positive load in this law therefore changes individual\nterms without repairing their net obstruction.\n\nLaw II changes terminal probabilities to the actual last queried index\nand adds a specified metric mass1/16 at the penultimate query. It keeps\nthe independent clock-zero common column and the positive incoming\nload1/64 at index0. This changes the failing moment: the full first-row\nTWO-moment screen now passes, as do full PSD and all optimum signs.\nHowever a different invariant, the last queried row tested against the\nPENULTIMATE clock, is strictly less than -1/16. It forces a negative\noriginal finite multiplier from the last query to an earlier-than-\npenultimate query. Every balanced correction annihilating e and t\npreserves this obstruction.\n\nThese are exclusions of TWO fully specified laws, including every\nremaining balanced skew in each law. They are not an impossibility\nresult for the general architecture or for the full dual cone. No\nformal residual vector or negative coefficient is presented as an\ninterpolable primal trajectory. No full-class upper bound on W, and\ntherefore no resolution of the original problem, has been obtained.\n\nThis is ONE joint proof draft. All new calculations are unverified;\nnone is adopted as a separately accepted premise. The only accepted\nfacts used are1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 at the scopes specified\nbelow. The older reports and the main advisory supplied directions only.\n\n## 2. Problem, accepted interfaces, and physical word\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n and smoothness L>0,\nW_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite Euclidean\ndimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,\nand x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.\nThe original question is whether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where\nW_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, and the original all-split\nbenchmark has\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the signed residual orientation and\nfull terminal-square certificate interpretation. Only incoming edges\nat the ACTUAL unqueried terminal may be deleted; no queried-prefix\ncoordinate or constraint is removed. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies\nthe word below, its mass formula, and B_n/D<47/50. Only CONDITIONAL on\nthe still-missing full-class W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for every m>=1536 does\nit imply the799/800 separation. That missing hypothesis is not proved.\n\nFix arbitrary integer m>=1536 and keep its parameters in EVERY child:\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1,\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nThe indices l,r are the last and penultimate QUERIED samples, different\nfrom the actual terminal n. We use the following short local scalar\naliases throughout this report. Their public glossary names carry85:\np=p85, s=s85, qtar=q85, T=T85, U=U85, J=J85, P=P85, H=H85,\nX=X85, r=r85. These explicitly scoped aliases do not change any\nexisting project-glossary definition. The matrix for the chronological\ncoordinate change is Ltri, distinct from scalar T; a superscript T\nmeans transpose. In Section3 only, Len is an integer suffix length.\n\nThe physical last three steps are (b,1+beta,b). Put hgap=1+beta=2-eps.\nElementary bounds from m>=1536 are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<3/2.\n\nThe accepted mass formula, also obtained by substitution in\n1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j, is\n\n    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).\n\nConsequently\n\n    2^m<1+S<c^m, c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-20).             (2.1)\n\nLet e be the all-ones column on the n queries and e_i its coordinate\ncolumns. Define clocks\n\n    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, T=t_l=1/p-b,\n    Delta=T-1=1/p-b-1, t_r=T-hgap.\n\nThey are strictly increasing, t_0=1 and t_1=1+b. In particular\n\n    3/(4p)<Delta<1/p, Delta-b>1/(2p),\n    0<hgap/Delta<4p<1/128.                            (2.2)\n\n## 3. Tail quantities and the bounds actually needed\n\nWrite sbit(L) for the binary digit sum. For0<=L<=n put\n\n    rtail_L=product_(j=n-L)^(n-1)(h_j-1)\n           =beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)),\n    s=rtail_n=beta^n/c^m,\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j),\n    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nThe suffix formula follows because h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j), and\nsum_(j=1)^L v2(j)=L-sbit(L). With L=l-i one has\n\n    u_i=rtail_L+rtail_(L+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,\n    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a(1+beta), u_(l-2)=ba beta,\n    U>7/3.                                          (3.1)\n\nFor the last inequality the last three terms exceed4/3,5/8,7/18,\nwhose sum169/72 exceeds7/3. Every rho_i is strictly positive.\nThe following source bound is especially useful:\n\n    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, rho_0<1/10, s<p.                (3.2)\n\nFor a symbolic proof,2^m-1>16m^2 for m>=16 by induction, starting at16.\nAlso beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2, so beta^(4m)<1/2.\nThus beta^n<2^(-4m), while b/a<4 and p>c^(-m) yield\nu_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64. The other inequalities follow.\nNo arithmetic, recurrence, or trajectory was evaluated by a program.\n\nTelescoping the terminal update gives\n\n    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S,                  (3.3)\n\nwhere Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_ij=h_j for j<i, and zero otherwise.\nFor Law I let tbar=sum rho_i t_i and set\n\n    alpha=(1-p)rho, V=t^T(k-alpha)=(1-p)(1/p-tbar).\n\nThe necessary uniform bounds are\n\n    32<V<512m^3, p V^2<1/1024.                       (3.4)\n\nHere is the full derivation, to avoid importing the previous unverified\nreport. The binary product and its digit-weighted sum give\n\n    Ztail=sum_(L=0)^n rtail_L\n         =product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    U=2Ztail-1-s,\n    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)\n                    2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)),\n    Mtail=sum_(L=0)^n L rtail_L.\n\nChoose j with m/2<2^j<=m. Bernoulli's inequality gives beta^(2^j)>=3/4,\nand c+beta^(2^j)<7/2; hence Mtail/Ztail>3m/28. Also ns<1. Therefore\n\n    sum_i rho_i(l-i)>(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2>3m/56-1/2.\n\nThe suffix after query i has mass at least b+(l-i). As1-p>1/2,\nthe formula for V makes V>32 for m>=1536.\nFor the upper bound, Len=L+1 final steps lie in H_d where d is the least\ninteger with2^d>Len. Thus their mass is smaller than c^d<(2^d)^2<=4Len^2.\nSince u_i<2 beta^L and U>2,\n\n    V<4 sum_(L>=0)(L+1)^2 beta^L\n      =4(1+beta)/(1-beta)^3<512m^3.\n\nThe series identity follows by multiplying by(1-beta)^3.\nFinally2^m>2^30 m^6 for m>=128: the base compares2^128 with2^72,\nand the induction step follows from\n(1+1/m)^6<=sum_(j>=0)(6/m)^j<=64/61<2.\nUsing p<2^(-m) gives pV^2<2^(-12)<1/1024.\n\nLaw II instead chooses alpha=(1-p)e_l. It has\n\n    V=b(1-p), alpha_0=0, v_0=u_0, v_l=a+p,\n    0<V<3/2, pV^2<1/1024.                            (3.5)\n\nIn BOTH laws alpha_star=p, v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s>0,\neta=F/E, e^T v=E, and\n\n    R=Ltri^T v,\n    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j)-alpha_i>0.            (3.6)\n\nIndeed R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i.\nMoreover0<v_0<u_0 in Law I, v_0=u_0 in Law II, and R_0<2p in both.\nFor Law I use alpha_0=((1-p)/U)u_0, with (1-p)/U<3/7.\n\n## 4. FULL original-space identity with an independent common column\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep x_0,g_0,...,g_n\narbitrary and independent, substituting ONLY the actual GD recursion.\nThe accepted residual is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nFor i<n set y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Then\n\n    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g,\n    g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                                (4.1)\n\nLtri is invertible with diagonal1. More explicitly, with\npi_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j),\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n             +sum_(j<i)h_j product_(j<j'<i)(1-h_j')y_j.\n\nThus this change spans ALL original queried-gradient directions.\nThe actual terminal gradient remains independently free.\n\nChoose any z with e^T z=E, positive weights w_i, and zero-sum zeta.\nLet Ww=sum_i w_i and define\n\n    K=diag(w)-ww^T/Ww,\n    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T,\n    d=zeta+F(v-z),\n    A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).                     (4.2)\n\nThe complete signed multiplier array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,l},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct i,j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i.           (4.3)\n\nDiagonal multipliers vanish. In scalar entries,\n\n    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(r'>i)(K_(r',j)+A_(r',j))\n                          +R_i v_j+eta Zout_i z_j,\n    Zout=Ltri^T z.                                  (4.4)\n\nThe formula defines every original ordered pair; none is hidden behind\nan existence assertion. The finite prefix outgoing total at i is N_ii,\nits incoming total is N_ii-2alpha_i, and the actual terminal adds incoming\n2alpha_i. Hence finite-node divergence is zero, terminal divergence2,\nand optimum divergence-2. All sampled values cancel in\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nFor prefix nodes including star,\nQ_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>; the remaining prefix quadratic is\n-g^T N y. Using(4.1) it is\n-<x_0,(Pe-d)^T y>+y^T P y. The terminal expansion, with -2f_n\nincluded, is\n||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2-2sum alpha_i delta_i.\nThose delta terms cancel the prefix value terms. Now\n\n    Pe=Ev+Fz,\n    2s v-Pe+d=-2Fz+zeta,\n    eta E^2=p^2-s^2.\n\nSubstitution proves the COMPLETE identity\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n         +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n         +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.           (4.5)\n\nLet ybar_w=sum w_i y_i/Ww. As e^T zeta=0, completion gives\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(zeta_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                      (4.6)\n\nThis pays the initial charge ONCE. It retains every detail, the common\nsquare and its derivative, and the actual terminal square.\nFull PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J. For necessity take scalar x_0=1,\ny_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i), where\nc0=1+[sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i)]/E, and g_n=-s+v^T y.\nEvery displayed square vanishes. Reconstruct the queried gradients by\n(4.1). This is a formal-space test, not a primal interpolation claim.\nIn particular g_n need NOT equal p when z and v differ.\n\nFor the actual common queried-gradient direction g_i=G, y_i=x_0-t_i G.\nPut V=t^T v, Z=t^T z, theta=t^T zeta, X=t^T Kt. The full prefix remainder\nis exactly\n\n    (q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>\n                      +(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2.          (4.7)\n\nThe real common derivative theta has not been discarded.\n\n## 5. General optimum moments, complete first-row moments, literal skew\n\nFrom Pe+d=2p v+zeta, the outgoing optimum vector is\n\n    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta.                              (5.1)\n\nThe incoming vector is\n\n    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha.                       (5.2)\n\nSince e^T A=-d^T and t^T At=0, its EXACT moments are\n\n    M0=e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    M1=t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.              (5.3)\n\nA nonnegative nu necessarily has M0>=0 and M0<=M1<=T M0.\nConversely the isolated two-moment problem has the nonnegative endpoint\nsolution\n\n    nu_target=((T M0-M1)/Delta)e_0\n                          +((M1-M0)/Delta)e_l.      (5.4)\n\nThis is not sufficient for the finite multiplier signs.\nFor ANY specified compatible nu_target let\n\n    rvec=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target,\n    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean*e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,\n    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,\n    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,\n    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.               (5.5)\n\nThe two moment identities imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.\nThus e^T fvec=0 and cvec^T fvec=0. Multiplication shows Ae=d and\nAt=rvec, with both terms skew. This is a literal entrywise completion.\nEvery other completion is A+C with C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0.\nSuch C leaves the polynomial, mu,nu and all moment tests below unchanged.\n\nFor the first row define\n\n    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,\n    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,\n    G0=P0-Delta*S0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-T)N_0j.\n\nThe first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T. Write\n\n    Lcol=Pe-d=2s v+2F z-zeta.\n\nThen N_0j=b Lcol_j-a(P_0j+A_0j), and N_00=b Lcol_0-aP_00.\nAs mu_0=2pR_0-a zeta_0, the complete general first-row sum is\n\n    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bF z_0.          (5.6)\n\nUsing(P+A)t=2Pt-2alpha-nu yields the complete clock identity\n\n    P0=b(2sV+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0\n                                  +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0,\n    (Pt)_0=(Kt)_0+Vv_0+eta Zz_0.                    (5.7)\n\nThese formulas explicitly include BOTH optimum directions.\nIf all finite lambda_(0,j)>=0, equivalently N_0j<=0, then necessarily\n\n    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0.                      (5.8)\n\nIndeed b<=t_j-1<=Delta for all j>0. When S0=0 all off-diagonals must\nvanish. These are exact invariant screens, not sufficient row signs.\n\nThere is another invariant at the actual last query l. Its row in\nLtri^T is e_l^T, so N_ll=P_ll, (Nt)_l=2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l.\nTesting its earlier entries against t_r, the penultimate clock, gives\n\n    Hlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-t_r)N_lj\n         =2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l-t_r mu_l-hgap*P_ll.  (5.9)\n\nNonpositive N_lj require Hlast>=0, since t_j<=t_r for j<l.\nA negative Hlast forces N_lj>0 at some j<r, not merely at r.\n\n## 6. The shared explicit common column, incoming load, and paid imbalance\n\nBoth laws fix the genuinely independent common column\n\n    z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, e^T z=E, t^T z=0.          (6.1)\n\nIt differs from v: for example every odd interior v_i is negative and\nnonzero, whereas z_i=0 there. The independence is not a scalar rescaling.\nSet the NONZERO incoming load\n\n    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload*e_0.              (6.2)\n\nFor each law's own alpha,V define\n\n    theta0=2(1-p)-2pV=2p alpha^T t,\n    theta=theta0+ellload,\n    Xreq=theta0/p-V^2+ellload.                       (6.3)\n\nSelect positive base weights what_i as specified for that law and put\n\n    tbase=sum what_i t_i/sum what,\n    Vbase=sum what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,\n    scale=Xreq/Vbase, w_i=scale*what_i,\n    K=diag(w)-ww^T/sum w,\n    zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.                             (6.4)\n\nThis fixes every scale from the actual word before row-sign work.\nIt gives t^T Kt=Xreq, e^T zeta=0 and t^T zeta=theta. Moreover\n\n    J=theta^2/(4Xreq).                              (6.5)\n\nIndeed(Kt)_i=w_i(t_i-tbar_w), whose squared inverse-weight sum is\nXreq. Weighted Cauchy--Schwarz shows the same law minimizes this charge\nat fixed theta for its metric. By(5.3), M0=M1=ellload. Thus(6.2) is\nexactly compatible. Define d by(4.2), A by(5.5), then EVERY scalar\nmultiplier by(4.3)--(4.4). The literal law sets C=0; our obstructions\nwill cover every extra skew C annihilating e and t.\n\n### 6.1 Positive metric, full PSD, all optimum signs\n\nFor both laws1<theta0<2. In Law I use V>32 and pV^2<1/1024 to get\npV<1/32768 and then theta0>1. In Law II use V<3/2 and(2.1).\nConsequently\n\n    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p>0,\n    Xreq<2/p+ellload, 1<theta<3, theta/Xreq>p.        (6.6)\n\nThe last follows because theta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+pV^2>0.\nEvery base weight is positive because p^2 rho_i>0, and the two unit\nendpoint masses make Vbase>0. Hence all metric weights are positive.\nThe full charge satisfies\n\n    J < [theta0*p/4]*(1+ellload/theta0)^2*(1024/1023)\n      < (4225/8184)p,\n    p^2+J <(17/32)p<qtar.                            (6.7)\n\nFor the second line use p<1/128 and\n4225/8184<67/128, equivalent to540800<548328.\nThus full PSD holds strictly below the requested target for BOTH laws.\nNo interpolation signs are inferred from PSD alone.\n\nThe terminal multipliers are nonnegative, and nu is exactly ellload at\nindex0 and zero elsewhere. All outgoing mu_i are STRICTLY positive.\nTo prove this, zeta_i has the sign of t_i-tbar_w, crossing only once\nfrom negative to positive, and has zero sum. Every proper suffix sum\nis positive: if i is before the crossing, use the negative preceding\nprefix and zero total; if i is after it, every remaining term is\npositive. If zeta_i<0 then sum_(j>i)zeta_j>=-zeta_i, whence\n\n    (Ltri^T zeta)_i=zeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j\n                                     >=(h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0.\n\nIf zeta_i>=0 the same expression is positive, including i=l because\nzeta_l>0. Equation(5.1) proves all mu_i>0.\n\n## 7. Exact endpoint-clock reduction and incoming-load cancellation\n\nFor (6.1) put Y=-(Kt)_0=w_0(tbar_w-1)>0. Then zeta_0=-theta Y/Xreq.\nWrite\n\n    K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK, Y=Xreq/Delta+eY.\n\nSubstituting(5.6)--(5.7), z_0=ET/Delta and Z=0 gives\n\n    G0=Gendpoint+Epert,\n    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq,\n    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[Xreq-T theta-(p^2-s^2)T^2]\n       +a nu_0+2bs(V-ET)\n       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-2aV]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0. (7.1)\n\nThis follows by expanding the metric part as\n a(2Y-Delta K_00)+(a-Delta)zeta_0-btheta,\nand using R_0=bE-a v_0 in the remaining terms.\nFor the endpoint reduction with zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt and ANY\ncompatible endpoint load, not just(6.2), write its total M0\nand moment M1, so theta=theta0+M0 and\nXreq=theta0/p-V^2+M1. Endpoint support means\nM1-T M0=-Delta nu_0. Consequently\n\n    Xreq-T theta=btheta0-V^2-Delta nu_0,\n    (a/Delta)(Xreq-Ttheta)+a nu_0\n                                  =(a/Delta)(btheta0-V^2). (7.2)\n\nThus EVERY endpoint incoming load cancels out of Gendpoint exactly.\nFor our fixed nonzero load, therefore,\n\n    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[btheta0-V^2-(p^2-s^2)T^2]\n       +2bs(V-ET)\n       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-2aV]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0. (7.3)\n\nA positive load is not intrinsically useless: it changes other rows,\nthe charge, and the metric perturbation. Equation(7.2) states only this\nprecise endpoint cancellation and does not exclude distributed metrics.\n\n## 8. Law I: tail terminal probabilities, source/last metric, positive load\n\nFix alpha=(1-p)rho and the complete choices(6.1)--(6.4) with\n\n    omega=p^2, what=e_0+e_l+omega*rho.               (8.1)\n\nEquations(4.3)--(4.4),(5.5) specify ALL multipliers. Full PSD and every\noptimum sign already follow jointly from Section6.\nFor exact metric estimates put xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta in[0,1],\nrbar=sum rho_i xclock_i, and define\n\n    gbase=Vbase/Delta^2,\n    fbase=(1+omega rho_0)(1+omega rbar)/(2+omega).\n\nRetaining the two unit endpoints in the variance minimum gives\ngbase>=1/2; centering at1/2 gives gbase<=1/2+omega/4.\nMoreover1/2-omega/4<=fbase<=1/2+omega and\n\n    K_00/scale=\n       [1+omega+omega^2 rho_0(1-rho_0)]/(2+omega)\n                        in[1/2,1/2+omega/2].\n\nSince Y=(Xreq/Delta)fbase/gbase, these imply\n\n    |eY|<=4Xreq*omega/Delta,\n    |eK|<=2Xreq*omega/Delta^2.                       (8.2)\n\nUse a<1/2, Xreq/Delta<5, theta<3 and0<Delta-a<Delta. The two\nterms of Epert are bounded by25omega and12omega respectively. Thus\n\n    |Epert|<40omega. (8.6)\n\nThis contradicts the required G0>=0 in(5.8). For EVERY extra skew\nC with Ce=Ct=0, some original finite lambda_(0,j),1<=j<l, is negative.\nThe j=l term has zero coefficient in G0 and cannot create this failure.\nThe nonzero incoming load did not repair the whole-law obstruction.\n\n## 9. General positive-height selector inequality and full W remain OPEN; no decisive failure, so two-anchor mechanism NOT opened.", "evidence": "# Geometry-adaptive single-anchor terminal mixtures\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED — max4 invocation83, project gd-exact-profile-20260926.\nCurrent steering ab8fdd0dc7b752a2; plan14ef9bbc976ba162.\nThis is one joint mathematical draft. Verdict and exact scope\n\nThe assigned selector has NOT been refuted, and its uniform all-function\ninequality has NOT been proved. Only Mechanism1 was opened. There is no third law.\n\nThe following analytical screens are completed jointly in this draft:\n\n* The known root-sign-reversing quadratic passes. On that precise prefix,\n  the last anchor is proved to be the unique optimal single anchor AND\n  to attain the TRUE full-prefix functional. The functional is below1/(4D).\n* The canonical Huber contact passes with equality1/D. EVERY original\n  anchor attains this value, so the deterministic index tie rule chooses0,\n  not the last anchor.\n* A genuinely multidirectional, separable Huber/quadratic family passes\n  uniformly for all initial coordinate amplitudes and every allowed m.\n  Its actual gradient vectors span two dimensions when both amplitudes\n  are nonzero; no equality of formal gradient variables is imposed.\n* Every legal prefix with ALL heights delta_i=0 passes with the stronger\n  bound below1/D, in arbitrary dimension. The original rows force these\n  anchors to be projections onto ONE convex set. This closes the whole\n  zero-height case, not just a preselected cloud of points or a scalar\n  projection example.\n* The same selector passes for EVERY legal prefix with\n  max_i delta_i<=||x_0||^2/(12D*sqrt(m)), by an explicit bound on\n  the sum of all physical suffix products. This is a quantitative\n  positive-height extension, not a full all-prefix theorem.\n\nAn exact finite correlation-cover inequality states the remaining\ninterface. It has not been proved for arbitrary positive-height prefixes.\nThe zero-height argument does not transfer merely by dropping height\nterms; those terms change the normal-cone inequalities themselves.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the full original interpolation model\nand maximal prefix extension. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the\nsame-enclosing-m scalar benchmark comparison. No unverified prior\nfinding or external theorem is used as a mathematical premise. In\nparticular the known quadratic is rederived here as part of this joint\nargument, rather than importing its previous unverified proof.\n\n## 2. Original model, hierarchy, and normalization\n\nNormalize L=1, an actual selected minimizer x_star=0, and f_star=0.\nAll norms and inner products are Euclidean. The original updates are\n\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i,     g_i=grad f(x_i),     f_i=f(x_i).\n\nFor all original ordered pairs, including the optimum, retain\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.\n\nThis orientation agrees with accepted1252b8823bb5a290. All statements\nabout a full certificate would have to retain independent physical\nformal gradients; no new PSD certificate is asserted in this report.\nThe score is W=2R, namely2f_n/||x_0||^2 about the specified minimizer.\n\nFix an integer m>=1536 ONCE. Throughout every child uses\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j).\n\nLet h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum H_m, D=D_m=1+2S, R=n-1, and\n\n    A=h_R=1+a, J=2^(m-1)-1,\n    t=a*c^(m-1), T=h_J=1+t.\n\nHere T is the actual whole-root step; A is the actual last leaf.\nThe complete prefix set is I={star,0,...,R}; the actual terminal is n.\nNo tail, leaf, child, query, or original row is removed.\n\nWe will use only the following deliberately loose parameter bounds:\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 1/3<a<1/2, 1<A<3/2, T>18,\n    D>36, S/T<15/2, D/T<16, D<2*c^m.\n\nHere are their symbolic justifications. Since sqrt(2)>7/5 and\nsqrt(2)<10/7, and epsilon<=1/6144,\n12/5<c<(17/7)*(6145/6144)<5/2. Also beta>99/100, so\na>(99/100)/(5/2)>1/3, while a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<1/2.\nFor v=a/(c-2), one has0<v<1 because\nc(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta. Literal concatenation gives\n\n    1+S_j=(1-v)*2^j+v*c^j.                             (2.1)\n\nThis identity follows by substituting into\n1+S_(j+1)=2*(1+S_j)+a*c^j and checking j=0; no recurrence\nwas executed. For j>=1, (2.1) is strictly less than c^j.\nConsequently D=2*(1+S_m)-1<2*c^m, and\nS/T<c^m/(a*c^(m-1))=c/a<15/2. Moreover\nT>1+c^(m-1)/3>18, and D/T=1/T+2S/T<16.\nEvery step exceeds1, so S>n and D>2^(m+1)-1>36.\nThese bounds apply to EVERY declared m with the SAME parameters.\n\n## 3. Exact complete-prefix functional and the explicit selector\n\nPut\n\n    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n    y_star=delta_star=0.\n\nThe original rows Q_(star,i)<=0 imply delta_i>=0.\nFor a probability alpha on I define\n\n    Psi(alpha)=2*sum_i alpha_i*delta_i\n                         +||x_n-sum_i alpha_i*y_i||^2.\n\nAccepted1252 constructs the maximal legal prefix extension F from\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in I)(<y_i,v>-delta_i),\n    F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)).\n\nWe jointly derive the finite variational step used here:\n\n    2F(x_n)=min_(alpha probability on ALL I) Psi(alpha). (3.1)\n\nFor any alpha, H dominates its alpha-average quadratic; completing\nthe square in v gives2F(x_n)<=Psi(alpha). A minimizing alpha exists\nby compactness. Put V=x_n-sum alpha_i*y_i at this minimum.\nThe partial derivatives of Psi/2 are delta_i-<V,y_i>. Probability\nexchanges show they have a common value d on positive coordinates\nand are at least d elsewhere. Thus\nmax_i(<y_i,V>-delta_i)=-d equals its alpha-average. Evaluating\n<x_n,V>-H(V) now gives Psi(alpha)/2. This proves the reverse\ninequality and (3.1), with the full vector geometry retained.\n\nFor each actual finite anchor define\n\n    d_i=||y_i||^2, b_i=<x_n,y_i>-delta_i.\n\nIf d_i>0, set theta_i=clip_[0,1](b_i/d_i); if d_i=0 set theta_i=0.\nPut\n\n    Psi_i=||x_n-theta_i*y_i||^2+2theta_i*delta_i,\n    M_1=min_(0<=i<=R) Psi_i.\n\nThe selector is the least original index attaining this finite minimum.\nIt is exactly the stated rule; choosing a particular anchor later is\nonly a valid upper bound on M_1 unless optimality is actually proved.\nIf d_i=0, b_i=-delta_i<=0, so the zero convention is minimizing.\nFor d>0 define the scalar gain\n\n    G(b,d)=0                  if b<=0,\n           b^2/d              if0<b<d,\n           2b-d               if b>=d.\n\nAt b=0,d the adjacent formulas agree. Set G(b,0)=0 for the only\nlegal case b<=0. Completing the scalar square yields exactly\n\n    M_1=||x_n||^2-max_i G(b_i,d_i).                    (3.2)\n\nThere is no unevaluated one-variable optimization left. The chain\nfor every legal actual terminal is\n\n    2f_n <=2F(x_n) <=M_1.                             (3.3)\n\nThe assigned quantitative assertion is\n\n    M_1 <=17||x_0||^2/(16D)                           (3.4)\n\nfor EVERY legal original H_m prefix in EVERY finite dimension.\nA family passing (3.4) is not a proof of this assertion.\n\n## 4. Exact root-sign-reversing quadratic screen\n\nChoose the ONE global scalar function\n\n    kappa=2/T, f(x)=kappa*x^2/2, x_0=1.\n\nIt is convex differentiable and1-smooth because0<kappa<1; its\nunique minimizer is0 and the true initial radius is1. All physical\nupdates are exactly\n\n    x_i=product_(s<i)(1-kappa*h_s), g_i=kappa*x_i,\n    x_n=P=product_(s<n)(1-kappa*h_s).\n\nProducts here are symbolic identities, not computed trajectories.\nFor every ordered pair, including the terminal and optimum,\n\n    Q_ij=-kappa*(1-kappa)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0.           (4.1)\n\nThe root multiplier is -1. Every other step is at most1+t/c;\nwith z_i=kappa*h_i,\n\n    0<z_i<=2*(1+t/c)/(1+t)<9/10      (i!=J).        (4.2)\n\nFor the last comparison use c>12/5 and t>17: after multiplying\npositive denominators it is enough that\n2+(5/6)t<(9/10)(1+t), which holds for t>33/2.\nEvery non-root multiplier is therefore in(0,1). All x_i before\nand at the root query are positive. All subsequent x_i are negative\nand strictly increase toward0. In particular P<0, and x_R is the\nnegative prefix value closest to0.\n\nBoth physical immediate-child roots have\nz_i=2*(1+t/c)/(1+t)>2/c>4/5. Their multipliers are each less\nthan1/5; all other non-root factors have magnitude less than1.\nConsequently\n\n    P^2<1/625, D*kappa*P^2<32/625<1/16.             (4.3)\n\nThus this function's ACTUAL score is below1/(16D).\n\nLet b=1-kappa*A. The bounds above give1/2<b<1 and\nx_R=P/b. Every prefix anchor has\n\n    y_i=(1-kappa)*x_i,\n    delta_i=kappa*(1-kappa)*x_i^2/2.\n\nFor R the optimal scalar weight is\n\n    theta_R=(b-kappa/2)/(1-kappa)\n            =[1-kappa*(A+1/2)]/(1-kappa),\n    0<theta_R<1.                                    (4.4)\n\nIts positivity follows from kappa*(A+1/2)<4/T<1; the upper\nbound uses A+1/2>1. Direct substitution gives\n\n    Psi_R=P^2-[P-(kappa/2)*x_R]^2\n         =[kappa*b-kappa^2/4]*x_R^2\n         <kappa*x_R^2<4*kappa*P^2<128/(625D)<1/(4D).\n                                                               (4.5)\n\nWe now evaluate the TRUE full-prefix functional, rather than assuming\nthis anchor is best. Set v_*=kappa*x_R/2<0. At every original anchor,\n\n    <y_i,v_*>-delta_i\n      =[kappa*(1-kappa)/2]*x_i*(x_R-x_i)<=0.          (4.6)\n\nFor positive x_i, the second factor is negative. For every negative\nprefix value, x_i<=x_R, the second factor is nonnegative. Equality\nin (4.6) occurs only at i=R; star also has value0. Thus the maximum\nin H(v_*) is0. Furthermore x_n=v_*+theta_R*y_R by (4.4), and\n0<theta_R<1. Evaluation of the defining conjugate at v_* gives\n\n    2F(x_n)>=2P*v_*-v_*^2=Psi_R.\n\nTogether with (3.1) and the valid star/R probability this proves\n\n    2F(x_n)=M_1=Psi_R<1/(4D).                        (4.7)\n\nThe last anchor is also the UNIQUE best finite anchor here. Indeed\nthe gap between any alpha-average and the maximum at v_* is a sum\nof nonnegative terms, strictly positive for positive mass at any\nfinite i!=R by (4.6). Equality in the square-completion bound also\nforces residual v_*. A star-only choice would have residual P!=v_*.\nHence any exact minimizing probability has positive mass at R and\nno other finite node. In particular no other single-anchor law ties R.\nThis argument is specific to this legal quadratic; it is not a general\nlast-anchor theorem.\n\nFor comparison to the previous round, the physical occupation average\nhas y_bar=(1-kappa)*(1-P)/(kappa*S)>0 by summing the actual GD\nupdates, whereas x_n=P<0. Its optimum scalar mix with star is zero,\ngiving surrogate P^2. Equation(4.7) displays exactly why the present\nadaptive support repairs that earlier failure. No conclusion about\nall other prefixes follows.\n\n## 5. Exact Huber contact and its deterministic selector\n\nPut k=1/D and define the ONE global scalar Huber function\n\n    f_H(x)=x^2/2                       if |x|<=k,\n           k*|x|-k^2/2                 if |x|>=k,\n    x_0=1.\n\nIts derivative clip(x,-k,k) is continuous, monotone and1-Lipschitz.\nThe unique minimizer is0. At original cumulative mass\ns_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, all original prefix and terminal points satisfy\n\n    x_i=1-k*s_i>k, g_i=k,\n    f_i=k*x_i-k^2/2,\n    y_i=x_i-k>0, delta_i=k*y_i.\n\nIndeed s_i<=S and1-k*S=(S+1)/D>k. All updates use the original\nsteps; finite-to-finite Q rows vanish. Rows to and from star are\nQ_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-k*y_i<=0. The terminal obeys the same\nidentities. The closest initial radius is1.\n\nLet z=x_n-k=S/D>0. For every actual prefix index i,\n\n    y_i>=y_R=x_n+a*k>z,\n    theta_i=z/y_i in(0,1),\n    Psi_i=2k*x_n-k^2=1/D.                            (5.1)\n\nThe equality follows from the scalar minimizer x_n-theta_i*y_i=k.\nFor the full probability simplex, delta_i=k*y_i including star, and\nits barycenter y ranges over[0,y_0]. Thus (3.1) minimizes\n2k*y+(x_n-y)^2 over that interval; its minimum is at y=z and equals\n1/D. Equivalently the actual Huber value already attains this upper\nbound. Hence\n\n    2f_H(x_n)=2F(x_n)=M_1=1/D.                        (5.2)\n\nEVERY prefix anchor is best. Under the stipulated least-index tie\nbreak, the selected index is0. This is an explicit reason not to\nidentify the rule with a fixed last-anchor law.\n\n## 6. A genuine two-dimensional positive-height screen\n\nTake arbitrary u>0 and v!=0. Define a scalar Huber function with\nthreshold k_u=u/D by the formula of Section5, replacing k by k_u.\nOn R^2 use the ONE static function\n\n    f(z,w)=f_(H,u)(z)+(kappa/2)*w^2,\n    x_0=(u,v), kappa=2/T.\n\nIts Hessian bounds, or directly its coordinate gradients, show convexity\nand1-Lipschitz gradient. Its unique minimizer is(0,0), so the exact\ninitial radius squared is u^2+v^2. Every original iterate is\n\n    z_i=u*(1-s_i/D),\n    w_i=v*product_(t<i)(1-kappa*h_t),\n    g_i=(u/D,kappa*w_i).\n\nThe first coordinate remains on the positive affine Huber ray through\nthe actual terminal. The second has exactly the one root sign change\nfrom Section4. All original updates hold coordinatewise. Every Q row\nis the sum of the Huber row calculated in Section5 and the nonpositive\nquadratic row in(4.1), including optimum and actual terminal rows.\nThis proves legality of the entire original transcript, not only of\nits collapsed endpoint data.\n\nIt is genuinely multidirectional. The first two gradients have\nnonzero determinant\n\n    det(g_0,g_1)=-(u/D)*kappa^2*A*v !=0,\n\nbecause the first physical step is A. Therefore the trajectory is\nnot a scalar common-gradient embedding.\n\nEvaluate the allowed R segment at theta=1, without claiming its\noptimum is attained there and without choosing different coordinate\nweights. Its Huber contribution is exactly\n\n    u^2/D+A^2*u^2/D^2.\n\nTo check, at R the first-coordinate y is z_n+a*k_u and\nx_n-y=-a*k_u; hence2delta_R+|x_n-y|^2 differs from the true\nHuber score u^2/D by (a+1)^2*k_u^2=A^2*u^2/D^2.\nThe quadratic contribution is\n\n    [kappa*(1-kappa)+a^2*kappa^2]*v^2*(P/b)^2\n       <kappa*v^2*(P/b)^2\n       <128*v^2/(625D),                              (6.1)\n\nusing a<1 and (4.3). Because A^2<9/4 and D>36,\n\n    M_1 <=Psi_R(theta=1)\n        <(17/16)*u^2/D+(128/625)*v^2/D\n        <17*(u^2+v^2)/(16D).                         (6.2)\n\nThe same upper bound includes the zero-amplitude endpoints by the\nalready treated scalar cases. No optimizer or executed product is\nneeded. The TRUE full-prefix functional obeys, on this same prefix,\n\n    u^2/D+kappa*v^2*P^2 <=2F(x_n)\n       <=M_1<17*(u^2+v^2)/(16D).                     (6.3)\n\nThe lower inequality uses this actual globally defined function;\nthe upper inequality uses(3.3). We do not assert exact equality for\nthe two-dimensional full functional. These bounds suffice to show\nthat this rotating-gradient test is not a selector failure or full-W\nfailure. They do not cover arbitrary two-dimensional functions.\n\n## 7. The entire zero-height legal-prefix class\n\nHere start from ANY legal original prefix with delta_i=0 for EVERY\nfinite i. Do not assume that a given cloud of convex-hull points is\na GD trajectory. Retain all original ordered rows. Define the ONE\ncompact convex set\n\n    K=conv{0,y_0,...,y_R}.\n\nSince f_i=||g_i||^2/2 and x_i=y_i+g_i, direct cancellation gives\n\n    Q_ij=<g_i,y_j-y_i>                               (7.1)\n\nfor every ordered pair, including star. Thus all original rows imply\n<g_i,z-y_i><=0 for all z in K. As y_i belongs to K and x_i=y_i+g_i,\nfor every z in K one has\n\n    ||x_i-z||^2\n      =||g_i||^2+||z-y_i||^2-2<g_i,z-y_i>\n      >=||g_i||^2.\n\nThe unique nearest point in K to x_i is therefore y_i. Consequently\n\n    y_i=P_K(x_i), g_i=x_i-P_K(x_i)                   (7.2)\n\nat EVERY original queried index, with no free reassignment of anchors.\n\nFor clarity this also constructs a legal static global function:\n\n    f_K(x)=dist(x,K)^2/2.\n\nA nearest point exists by compactness, and uniqueness follows from\nstrict convexity of squared norm along a segment of K. Projection\noptimality gives <x-P_K(x),z-P_K(x)><=0 for z in K. Applying this\nat x and x' and adding proves\n\n    <P_K(x)-P_K(x'),x-x'> >=||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2.\n\nHence g_K(x)=x-P_K(x) is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the distance values\nusing P_K(x) and P_K(x') as trial points shows\nf_K(x+d)<=f_K(x)+<g_K(x),d>+||d||^2/2 and, by reversing the\ncomparison, f_K(x+d)>=f_K(x)+<g_K(x+d),d>-||d||^2/2.\nContinuity of g_K proves differentiability with gradient g_K.\nFor convexity, a convex combination of the projections is a valid\npoint in K; convexity of squared norm then proves the convexity\ninequality for f_K. Thus f_K is finite convex differentiable and\n1-smooth, with its complete minimum set exactly K. It interpolates\nall queried points, gradients, and values by(7.2), and follows the\noriginal GD trajectory. The designated minimum0 belongs to K and\nis explicitly valid. If desired choose the closest point P_K(x_0)\nas the reference minimum; the bound below holds for that minimum too.\n\nIn this case(3.1) additionally evaluates the TRUE maximal prefix value:\n\n    2F(x_n)=min_(y in K)||x_n-y||^2=dist(x_n,K)^2.    (7.3)\n\nFor each original step, its already computed projection y_i is a\nvalid trial point for the NEXT distance. Therefore\n\n    dist(x_(i+1),K)\n      <=||x_i-h_i*g_i-y_i||\n      =(h_i-1)*dist(x_i,K),                         (7.4)\n\nbecause every h_i>1. Only the original geometry yields this bound.\nAt the actual last anchor, theta=1 gives\n\n    M_1<=||x_n-y_R||^2\n       =a^2*dist(x_R,K)^2\n       <=[product_(i<n)(h_i-1)]^2*dist(x_0,K)^2.     (7.5)\n\nThe product has a closed symbolic value for the physical hierarchy.\nA level ell step1+a*c^ell occurs2^(m-1-ell) times. The multiplicity\nsum is n and the level-weighted sum is n-m (both follow by the\nliteral binary concatenation, or induction on these finite sums).\nConsequently\n\n    product_(i<n)(h_i-1)\n       =a^n*c^(n-m)=beta^n*c^(-m).                  (7.6)\n\nNo trajectory, recurrence, or product was numerically evaluated.\nUsing D<2*c^m and0<beta<1,\n\n    D*beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)<2*c^(-m)<1.\n\nThus for every such prefix and every specified minimum0,\n\n    M_1<=beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)*dist(x_0,K)^2\n        <=beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)*||x_0||^2\n        <||x_0||^2/D                                (7.7)\n\nwhen x_0!=0; if the distance is zero the non-strict zero bound\nalready suffices. Since any supplied legal prefix interpolant has\nterminal value at most F(x_n), this proves the assigned inequality\nfor the ENTIRE zero-height legal-prefix class, including all of its\nmultidirectional configurations. A nonzero initial radius for f_K\nis dist(x_0,K); if x_0 belongs to K, all queried gradients and the\nactual terminal error are zero, a harmless degenerate case.\n\nAn unconstrained convex-hull configuration can have a large gap\nbetween distance to its hull and distance to all rays[0,y_i]. It\ncannot refute the assigned selector through delta_i=0: the GD and\nnormal conditions(7.1)--(7.4) force the last ray already to be close\nenough at the required quantitative scale. This distinction uses\nall the original rows and the actual last leaf.\n\n## 7b. A quantitative small-positive-height extension\n\nThe preceding geometry need not be assumed when the heights are small.\nFor ANY legal original prefix put\n\n    Delta=max_(0<=i<=R) delta_i, r_i=h_i-1>0.\n\nDirect expansion of the original row, using\nx_(i+1)=y_i-r_i*g_i and y_(i+1)=x_(i+1)-g_(i+1),\n\n    Q_(i+1,i)=delta_(i+1)-delta_i\n                +r_i*<g_i,g_(i+1)>+||g_(i+1)||^2<=0.\n\nConsequently\n\n    r_i^2*||g_i||^2+2delta_i-2delta_(i+1)-||g_(i+1)||^2\n       =||r_i*g_i+g_(i+1)||^2-2Q_(i+1,i)>=0,\n\nand hence\n\n    ||g_(i+1)||^2<=r_i^2*||g_i||^2+2Delta          (i<R).\n                                                               (7b.1)\n\nBoth optimum rows at0 imply||g_0||^2<=<g_0,x_0>, so\n||g_0||<=||x_0||. Applying(7b.1) successively, by finite induction\nas an inequality rather than an executed recurrence, and evaluating\nthe same R segment at theta=1, gives\n\n    M_1 <=2delta_R+a^2||g_R||^2\n        <=p_m*||x_0||^2+2Delta*e_m,                 (7b.2)\n\nwhere the two explicit physical word functionals are\n\n    p_j=product_(i in H_j)(h_i-1)^2\n       =beta^(2(2^j-1))*c^(-2j),\n    e_j=sum_(i in H_j) product_(t in H_j,t>i)(h_t-1)^2,\n    p_0=1, e_0=0.\n\nHere indices inside a child are its original chronological order;\nthe SAME enclosing m parameters are used. We now bound e_m\nquantitatively, rather than leaving its size to a recurrence oracle.\nConcatenation as left child, root, right child gives the identity\n\n    e_(j+1)=M_j*e_j+p_j,\n    M_j=1+a^2*c^(2j)*p_j\n       =1+a^2*beta^(2(2^j-1)).                     (7b.3)\n\nThe left-child suffix factors cross the actual root and whole right\nchild; the root contribution is p_j; the right-child contribution\nis e_j. This proves(7b.3) with every physical suffix retained.\nAs all M_j>=1, expansion of this identity as a finite sum yields\n\n    e_m <=[product_(j=0)^(m-1) M_j]*sum_(j=0)^(m-1)p_j.\n                                                               (7b.4)\n\nLet q=ceil(log_2(4m)); q is only an analytic splitting index, not a\nnew schedule parameter. Then4m<=2^q<8m. For j<q,\nM_j<5/4<sqrt(2), and hence the product of at most q such factors\nis less than2^(q/2)<3sqrt(m).\nFor j=q+r with r>=0, use log(beta)<=-1/(4m) and\n2^j-1>=2^(j-1) to obtain\n\n    beta^(2(2^j-1))<=exp(-2^j/(4m))<=exp(-2^r).\n\nSince2^r>=r+1, the sum over r>=0 is at most\nsum_(r>=0)exp(-(r+1))=1/(exp(1)-1)<1.\nThe logarithm of the product of all remaining M_j is thus less\nthan a^2<1/4, using log(1+s)<=s for s>=0. This product is less\nthan exp(1/4)<4/3. The last inequality follows by comparing the\nexponential series term by term with the geometric series at1/4.\nThese bounds also apply if the finite tail is empty.\nConsequently product M_j<4sqrt(m). Furthermore\np_j<=c^(-2j)<=4^(-j), so sum p_j<4/3. Equation(7b.4) gives\n\n    e_m<(16/3)*sqrt(m)<6sqrt(m).                    (7b.5)\n\nThe constants are valid for EVERY m>=1536. No arithmetic, product,\nor recurrence was evaluated by a program. The product decay already\nproved above gives D*p_m<2c^(-m)<1/16 for these m. Therefore\n\n    M_1 <=p_m*||x_0||^2+2Delta*e_m,\n    M_1 <||x_0||^2/(16D)+12sqrt(m)*Delta            if Delta>0.\n                                                               (7b.6)\n\nFor Delta=0 the first inequality directly gives the stronger bound.\nIn particular the assigned target inequality holds for EVERY legal\noriginal prefix satisfying the explicit positive-height condition\n\n    max_i delta_i <=||x_0||^2/(12D*sqrt(m)).         (7b.7)\n\nThis is a uniform quantitative sufficient condition, not a conjecture\nabout all prefixes. It uses an available point on the SAME single-\nanchor segment and does not introduce any new terminal probability\nlaw. It does not claim that a failed (7b.7) forces selector failure;\nHuber is an example with larger heights which passes by Section5.\nNo new minimizer or function is needed: (7b.1)--(7b.7) apply to the\nsupplied legal static prefix and its original chosen minimizer.\n\n\n## 8. Exact remaining positive-height interface and failed transfers\n\nLet chi=17/(16D) and E=||x_n||^2-chi*||x_0||^2.\nIf E<=0 the optimum-only choice theta=0 proves(3.4). If E>0,\n(3.2) proves that(3.4) is equivalent to the existence of an actual\nfinite anchor with d_i>0 such that\n\n    b_i>=sqrt(E*d_i)              if d_i>=E,\n    b_i>=(E+d_i)/2                if0<d_i<E.         (8.1)\n\nTo verify the cases, solve G(b_i,d_i)>=E on its two increasing\npositive branches; at d_i=E they both require b_i>=E.\nThe threshold requires b_i>0, so the zero-gain branch cannot help.\nEquation(8.1) is an exact finite geometry condition, not its proof.\nIts unproved quantifier is: every positive-height legal original\nH_m prefix satisfies one of these original-anchor inequalities.\n\nTwo direct proof routes were screened within Mechanism1.\n\nFirst, a stronger sufficient last-anchor estimate would be\n\n    2f_R-(1-a^2)||g_R||^2 <=chi*||x_0||^2.           (8.2)\n\nIts left side is exactly Psi_R(theta=1), so(8.2) would imply(3.4).\nNo bound(8.2) for arbitrary legal prefixes is established here.\nFailure of the previous backward-only multiplier support does not\nrefute(8.2): its negative direction was not interpolable. Conversely,\nthe family calculations above cannot establish(8.2) outside their\nhypotheses. We retain (8.1), rather than replacing the selector by R.\n\nThe original optimum rows give a useful exact last-anchor formula.\nWrite y=y_R, g=g_R, delta=delta_R, d=||y||^2, and\n\n    t_R=a*<g,y>+delta.\n\nFrom Q_(R,star)<=0 one has <g,y>>=delta>=0, so t_R>=0.\nSince x_n=y-a*g, the segment numerator is b=d-t_R. Thus if d>0,\n\n    theta_R=max(0,1-t_R/d),\n    Psi_R=2delta+a^2||g||^2-t_R^2/d    when0<=t_R<d,\n    Psi_R=||x_n||^2                   when t_R>=d.  (8.3)\n\nAt equality the formulas agree. If d=0, the optimum rows imply\ndelta=0 and the stated zero-anchor convention applies. These are\nexact scalar evaluations; bounding their minimum together with\nall OTHER anchors remains necessary. They do not leave an unknown\none-dimensional optimizer and do not supply the missing global rate.\n\nSecond, the full normal-cone contraction closes the zero-height\ncase but does not extend unchanged. For general heights, expanding\nALL the original rows gives instead\n\n    Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i><=0,\n    <g_i,y_j-y_i><=delta_j-delta_i.                  (8.4)\n\nThe right side is not zero and has no fixed sign across all pairs.\nTherefore y_i need not be the projection of x_i onto the convex\nhull K. Discarding delta_i>=0 from Psi while continuing to use\n(7.2) or(7.4) is invalid. As a concrete legal check, in the Huber\ncontact all finite y_i are positive and K=[0,y_0], whereas for a\nlater query with x_i in[0,y_0] the projection is x_i itself and the\nactual y_i=x_i-k differs. The height differences in(8.4) account\nfor this. Such an i exists, for example i=R: s_R=S-A>1 gives\n0<x_R<y_0. All values here use the original Huber trajectory.\n\nThus no counterexample to the selector has emerged, but the attempted\nprojection transfer has a concrete failed hypothesis. The unresolved\ncase requires controlling positive heights and the changed geometry\njointly at scale1/D. It is not a support-representation issue or an\nunevaluated scalar minimization.\n\n## 9. Literature, memory, and resource record\n\nQuery-memory recovered TASK.md, current guidanceab8fdd0dc7b752a2,\nprevious quadratic finding1b246cab08578ca1 and its precise failure\nscope, and the relevant accepted facts. The first broad combined\nretrieval was truncated; the needed local report sections and both\naccepted proofs were subsequently read in bounded passages. No\ntruncated finding was used as a correctness premise. Original full W threshold and adaptive one-anchor cover remain OPEN.", "evidence": "# Signed height transport and the two convex height extensions\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED — max4 invocation84, gd-exact-profile-20260926.\nSteering: TASK.md and master_guidance b2215cdc6c21a4c3.\nPlan: 322dae312e1b1c16. Interim finding: 52de0461cdbd8db0.\nAll new arguments in this document form ONE draft. There was no authorized\nformal gate, no fact_submit, and no new accepted fact. No new assertion\nbelow is a truth-store building block.\n\n## 1. Verdict and scope\n\nThe signed-height identity in the assignment is correct as an algebraic\nidentity. It retains a nonpositive original-row sum and all adjacent error\nsquares, but is not by itself an upper certificate.\n\nMechanism1 specifies a nonnegative original-row law collecting the signed\nheight mass at the ACTUAL last queried leaf. It uses remote leaf-to-leaf\nand remote checkpoint-to-leaf rows outside E80, has the exact full value\nflow, and keeps every physical step. Nevertheless its full quadratic has\nexplicit negative directions at every q, in particular q=17/(16D).\nThese directions are NOT legal transcripts. They refute this literal\ncoefficient law, not the adaptive selector, not the entire signed-height\nmethod, and not the full interpolation cone. Two necessary original-space\npayment inequalities identify the missing source and short-leaf reserves.\n\nOnly after that decisive law failure was Mechanism2 opened. The proposed\nfinite maximum of affine height supports does satisfy phi(0)=min phi=0,\nphi(y_i)=delta_i, and y_i=prox_phi(x_i). Its smooth envelope interpolates\nthe ENTIRE legal prefix on one static function. But it is generally\nstrictly BELOW the maximal legal terminal extension. A same-m, same-word\nlegal quadratic prefix gives explicit strictly different terminal values,\ntrue minimum sets, and globally optimized level thresholds. This refutes\nthe proposed zero-error transfer from the lower height envelope to a\nfull terminal upper. It does not refute the one-anchor inequality.\n\nThe correct upper height extension is the convex roof of the finite\nheight data, not their affine-support maximum. The report proves the\ninclusions between their level sets, gives a paid anchor-alignment bound,\nand evaluates its finite one-anchor threshold minimization. The still\nmissing all-prefix bound on that payment is theorem-level. No epigraph\nidentity or unevaluated threshold is presented as a rate.\n\nOriginal target: OPEN. Full W<=17/(16D): OPEN.\nAdaptive single-anchor cover: OPEN, NOT REFUTED.\nExactly TWO mechanisms were opened; no two-anchor selector was opened.\n\n## 2. Original problem, accepted inputs, and notation\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the\nsupremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\nEuclidean dimension, every convex differentiable L-smooth function with\nan attained minimum, and every x_0 different from the selected minimizer.\nThe updates are x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.\nThe original target is to decide whether\n\n    lim_(n->infinity) W_n^star/U_n^ZJ = 1,\n    W_n^star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h).\n\nThe ORIGINAL benchmark has C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1,\n\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nEvery integer split and the original all-word quantifier remain.\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the exact original interpolation and\nmaximal prefix extension. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies B_n/D<47/50\non the hierarchy below. Its 799/800 separation is CONDITIONAL on the\nfull-function W upper, which has not been established here.\n\nFix any enclosing integer m>=1536, once and for all. Put\n\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, R=n-1, S=sum_(i<n)h_i,\n    D=1+2S, A=h_R=1+a, q=17/(16D).\n\nEvery child uses this SAME m and these SAME parameters. The actual\nlast leaf is R, the actual unqueried terminal is n. All h_i>1 and\n0<a<1/(1+sqrt(2))<1/2. In particular A<3/2. No products or trajectories\nappearing below were evaluated by a mathematical program.\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Set g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i),\nx_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Retain the ORIGINAL ordered rows\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.\n\nThe queried set is I={star,0,...,R}. Put\n\n    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n    y_star=delta_star=0.\n\nDirect expansion, for every ordered pair in I, gives\n\n    Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>.               (2.1)\n\nThe two optimum rows give\n\n    delta_i>=0, delta_i<=<g_i,y_i>.                  (2.2)\n\nAll vectors are real and finite dimensional. A full-space quadratic test\nbelow means a test on independent formal x_0,g_0,...,g_n after the exact\nGD substitution; such a test need not satisfy interpolation. All local\nsymbols introduced below are local to this report, not glossary overrides.\n\n## 3. Mechanism1: signed heights and exact adjacent squares\n\nDefine positive physical excesses and their suffix weights by\n\n    r_i=h_i-1,\n    omega_i=product_(t=i+1)^R r_t^2, omega_R=1,\n    p=product_(t=0)^R r_t^2,\n    c_0=omega_0,\n    c_i=omega_i-omega_(i-1)=omega_i*(1-r_i^2), 1<=i<=R.\n\nEmpty products equal1. The c_i are SIGNED and telescope to sum c_i=1.\nFor i>=1 with a long step h_i>2, c_i<0. At both level0 leaves and level1\nsteps c_i>0: their excesses are a and beta. At levels at least2 the\nexcess is at least beta*c=lambda*(1-epsilon^2)>2, since epsilon<1/8,\nlambda>12/5, and (12/5)*(63/64)>2. These signs use the original physical\nword, not a reset child schedule. Also c_R=1-a^2>0.\n\nFor i<R the update gives y_(i+1)=y_i-r_i*g_i-g_(i+1). Equation (2.1)\nthen gives the EXACT adjacent row\n\n    Q_(i+1,i)=delta_(i+1)-delta_i\n                   +r_i<g_i,g_(i+1)>+||g_(i+1)||^2.\n\nConsequently\n\n    ||g_(i+1)||^2\n      =r_i^2||g_i||^2+2(delta_i-delta_(i+1))\n          -||r_i*g_i+g_(i+1)||^2+2Q_(i+1,i).        (3.1)\n\nMultiply (3.1) by omega_i and sum over every original i<R.\nThe gradient terms telescope because omega_i*r_i^2=omega_(i-1)\nfor i>=1, omega_0*r_0^2=p, and omega_(R-1)=a^2. Adding 2delta_R gives\n\n    E_R:=2delta_R+a^2||g_R||^2\n       =p||g_0||^2+2sum_(i=0)^R c_i*delta_i\n         -sum_(i<R)omega_i||r_i*g_i+g_(i+1)||^2\n         +2sum_(i<R)omega_i Q_(i+1,i).             (3.2)\n\nThis derivation retains signed height changes. No delta_i-delta_(i+1)\nwas replaced by a maximum height, and no error square was dropped.\nThe last original step gives x_n-y_R=-a*g_R, so E_R is exactly the\nlast-anchor segment cost at weight1. It is only a sufficient upper\nsurrogate for the adaptive selector.\n\n## 4. A concrete remote-row law and its full-space refutation\n\nLet Pplus={i: c_i>0}, Pminus={j: c_j<0}. Define the complete height\ntransport t, with unlisted entries zero, by\n\n    t_(i,R)=c_i,          i in Pplus, i!=R,\n    t_(R,j)=-c_j,        j in Pminus,\n    t_(R,star)=1.                                       (4.1)\n\nEvery t is nonnegative. Its net finite flow at i is c_i. At a positive\nnon-R node the outgoing mass is c_i; at a negative node the incoming\nmass is -c_i. At R, outgoing minus incoming is\n1+sum_(-c_j)-sum_(i in Pplus,i!=R)c_i=c_R, using sum c_i=1.\nThe star has net flow -1.\n\nThis law compares every positive height to the actual last leaf using\nQ_(i,R), and the last leaf to every negative long-bridge height using\nQ_(R,j). It includes both whole physical children and their real bridge.\nIt leaves the E80 support: Q_(0,R) is a remote leaf-to-leaf row, and\nQ_(1,R) is a remote checkpoint-to-leaf row, since c_1>0 and R-1>1.\nThus its failure is not inferred merely from the old E80 obstruction.\n\nThe proposed ORIGINAL prefix multipliers are twice t, plus 2omega_i\non every row (i+1,i), i<R. Add the sole terminal row lambda_(n,R)=2.\nAll overlaps are added, all other entries are zero, and no incoming\nterminal edge is used. Prefix flow is0 at i<R and2 at R: at0 it is\n2c_0-2omega_0=0; at an interior i it is\n2c_i+2omega_(i-1)-2omega_i=0; at R it is2c_R+2omega_(R-1)=2.\nAfter adding the terminal row every finite node has the required flow.\n\nSubtract (3.2) and add these prefix rows. The complete prefix remainder is\n\n    K_t(q)=q||x_0||^2-E_R\n           +2sum_(i<R)omega_i Q_(i+1,i)+2sum_ij t_ij Q_ij\n      =q||x_0||^2-p||g_0||^2\n           +sum_(i<R)omega_i||r_i*g_i+g_(i+1)||^2\n           +2sum_ij t_ij<g_i,y_j-y_i>.               (4.2)\n\nAll heights, equivalently all sample values, cancel exactly. Its FULL\noriginal certificate polynomial is\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,R)\n         +2sum_(i<R)omega_i Q_(i+1,i)+2sum_ij t_ij Q_ij\n       =||g_n+a*g_R||^2+K_t(q).                     (4.3)\n\nFor a valid upper certificate, (4.3) must be PSD on every original\nformal vector assignment. Nonnegative coefficients alone do not suffice.\n\n### 4.1 Free initial direction\n\nTake any nonzero vector v and set x_0=0, g_0=v, g_i=0 for every i>0,\nincluding n. Then y_0=-v and y_i=-A*v for every finite i>=1.\nThe only nonzero adjacent square is omega_0*a^2||v||^2=p||v||^2,\nwhich cancels the source product term. The only nonzero transport\nquadratic is its source0 row to R:\n\n    2t_(0,R)<v,y_R-y_0>=-2a*omega_0||v||^2<0.\n\nBoth q||x_0||^2 and the terminal square vanish. Therefore the entire\npolynomial (4.3) is strictly negative, for EVERY q. In particular it\nfails at the full requested scale17/(16D) for every enclosing m>=1536.\n\nThis is a rank-one ORIGINAL-SPACE discriminator, not a legal prefix.\nIndeed x_0=0 and g_0!=0 already contradict the two optimum rows.\nNo lower bound on W or failure of a true legal-prefix inequality follows.\n\n### 4.2 An interior short-leaf direction\n\nThe failure is not confined to how c_0 was defined. Choose the actual\ninterior leaf j=2, and set x_0=0, g_j=v!=0, all other gradients zero.\nSince m>=1536, 0<j<R and the neighboring physical indices all exist.\nHere y_j=-v, all preceding y_i=0, and all later y_i=-A*v. The adjacent\nsquares have total\n\n    (omega_(j-1)+a^2*omega_j)||v||^2\n                       =2a^2*omega_j||v||^2.\n\nOnly the outgoing transport from j contributes. As\nc_j=omega_j*(1-a^2), equation (4.2) is exactly\n\n    K_t(q)=2omega_j*a*(a^2+a-1)||v||^2<0,             (4.4)\n\nbecause 0<a<1/2 implies a^2+a<3/4. Again the terminal square vanishes.\nThis is a second full-space, noninterpolable discriminator. A change\nonly to the initial transport cannot repair this literal collector.\n\n### 4.3 Necessary payments for a future different transport\n\nFor any other nonnegative height transport T with finite net flow c,\n(4.2) still holds with T in place of t. This is an identity, not a\nfeasibility assertion about T. On the slice with only g_0=v nonzero,\nkeep x_0 free and put\n\n    u=T_(0,star), z=sum_(j=1)^R T_(0,j).\n\nAll other sources have zero gradient. Exact substitution gives\n\n    K_T(q)=q||x_0||^2-2u<x_0,v>+2(u-a*z)||v||^2.\n\nFor q>0 this slice is nonnegative for all x_0,v if and only if\n\n    2(u-a*z) >= u^2/q.                               (4.5)\n\nComplete the square q||x_0-(u/q)v||^2 to obtain both directions.\nThus a free initial optimum payment cannot be omitted, and its mixed\nx_0/gradient term costs u^2/q. The collector has u=0,z=omega_0>0.\n\nLikewise, at any interior leaf j, put\nu_j=T_(j,star), L_j=u_j+sum_(k<j)T_(j,k),\nZ_j=sum_(k>j)T_(j,k). On the slice with only g_j=v nonzero and x_0 free,\n\n    K_T(q)=q||x_0||^2-2u_j<x_0,v>\n          +2(omega_j*a^2+L_j-a*Z_j)||v||^2.\n\nA necessary condition is therefore\n\n    2(omega_j*a^2+L_j-a*Z_j) >= u_j^2/q.             (4.6)\n\nNeither (4.5) nor (4.6) suffices for full PSD; mixed-gradient directions\nremain. These are explicit audits of this signed-height payment\nmechanism, not a third proposed law or an unknown PSD problem claimed\nas solved. Reverse remote payments or direct optimum mass would change\nthe law and must pay the indicated source terms.\n\n## 5. Mechanism2: the affine-support height function is genuinely proximal\n\nAfter Section4's decisive first-law failure, define from ALL original\nlegal prefix rows\n\n    phi(y)=max{0, max_(0<=i<=R)[delta_i+<g_i,y-y_i>]}.\n                                                               (5.1)\n\nIt is finite, continuous, convex and nonnegative. By (2.1), every affine\npiece at y_j is at most delta_j, while piece j equals delta_j. Equation\n(2.2) handles the zero piece. Thus phi(y_j)=delta_j. The active affine\npiece also gives, for every z,\n\n    phi(z)>=phi(y_j)+<g_j,z-y_j>,\n\nso g_j belongs to the subdifferential of phi at y_j. At zero, the\nfinite pieces have values delta_i-<g_i,y_i><=0 by Q_(i,star)<=0.\nHence phi(0)=0=min phi. Both optimum-row orientations were used.\n\nWrite prox_phi(x) for the unique minimizer of phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2.\nIt exists because phi>=0 and the quadratic is coercive, and is unique\nby strict convexity. With x_i=y_i+g_i, the subgradient inequality gives\n\n    phi(z)+||x_i-z||^2/2\n       >=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2+||z-y_i||^2/2.\n\nEquality holds at z=y_i. Therefore\n\n    prox_phi(x_i)=y_i,\n    f_phi(x_i):=min_z[phi(z)+||x_i-z||^2/2]=f_i.       (5.2)\n\nFor completeness, f_phi is one static convex differentiable 1-smooth\nfunction on the SAME finite-dimensional space. Let u=prox_phi(x),\nu'=prox_phi(x'), v=x-u, v'=x'-u'. To prove v in partial phi(u), fix z\nand compare the minimizing u to u+s(z-u), 0<s<=1. Convexity bounds\nphi(u+s(z-u)) by phi(u)+s(phi(z)-phi(u)). Expanding the quadratic\nin this comparison, dividing by s, and sending s down to zero gives\nphi(z)>=phi(u)+<v,z-u>. Add this subgradient inequality at u and u'\nto get\n\n    <u-u',v-v'>>=0,\n    <x-x',v-v'>>=||v-v'||^2.\n\nThus ||v-v'||<=||x-x'||. For d=x'-x, trial points u,u' in the two\nenvelope minima give\n<v',d>-||d||^2/2 <= f_phi(x')-f_phi(x)\n                         <= <v,d>+||d||^2/2.\nThe Lipschitz estimate shows both bounds equal <v,d> up to a term\nbounded in absolute value by 3||d||^2/2. Hence grad f_phi(x)=v,\nand that gradient is 1-Lipschitz.\nConvexity follows by using a convex combination of u,u' as a trial point\nand convexity of phi and the squared norm. Equation (5.2) and its gradient\nversion show that every original update follows the exact supplied prefix.\nThe entire function is fixed before the run.\n\nIts COMPLETE minimum set is\n\n    C_low(0)={y: <g_i,y><=<g_i,y_i>-delta_i for all i},     (5.3)\n\nwhich contains0. Indeed the envelope is nonnegative, and its value is\nzero precisely when its minimizing point equals x and phi(x)=0.\nThe true initial distance is dist(x_0,C_low(0)). This can be smaller than\n||x_0||, so the new interpolant's closest minimum must not be inherited\nsilently from a different original function. The selected minimum0 is\nnevertheless always explicitly valid.\n\nThese identities make phi a correct height representation of the\nQUERIED proximal pairs. They do not make f_phi a maximal extension.\n\n## 6. The upper height model and a paid level-set comparison\n\nAccepted1252 gives the maximal legal prefix extension F by\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in I)(<y_i,v>-delta_i),\n    F(x)=sup_v[<x,v>-H(v)].\n\nFor every probability alpha on ALL I, completing the square gives\n\n    2F(x)<=2sum_i alpha_i delta_i+||x-sum_i alpha_i y_i||^2.\n\nThe minimum over alpha is attained on its compact simplex. At a\nminimizer let V=x-sum alpha_i y_i. The derivatives of half the displayed\nquadratic are delta_i-<V,y_i>. Exchanges of probability imply that they\nare equal on positive alpha_i and no smaller elsewhere. Therefore the\nmaximum affine term in H(V) equals its alpha-average, and evaluating\nthe conjugate at V gives equality. This proves jointly\n\n    2F(x)=min_(alpha probability on I)\n                   [2sum alpha_i delta_i+||x-sum alpha_i y_i||^2]. (6.1)\n\nDefine the extended-valued CONVEX ROOF\n\n    Psi(y)=min{sum_i alpha_i delta_i:\n                alpha_i>=0, sum alpha_i=1, sum alpha_i*y_i=y},\n                                                               (6.2)\n\nand set Psi=+infinity outside conv{y_i:i in I}. Each nonempty feasible\nfiber is compact. The minimum is attained; taking convex combinations\nof feasible probabilities proves convexity. Its epigraph is the closed\nfinite-dimensional polyhedron generated by the finite height points\nand the upward vertical ray; alternatively compactness of probabilities\nproves lower semicontinuity directly for bounded heights. It is proper.\nJensen's inequality for phi gives phi<=Psi. At every y_i, the supporting\npiece i bounds Psi from below by delta_i, while the point mass bounds it\nfrom above by delta_i. Thus Psi(y_i)=delta_i, and the same affine piece\nshows g_i in partial Psi(y_i). Also Psi(0)=0=min Psi.\n\nEquation (6.1) is exactly\n\n    F(x)=min_y[Psi(y)+||x-y||^2/2].                   (6.3)\n\nThis identifies the upper model without importing a conjugacy theorem.\nThe finite affine maximum phi and the convex roof Psi interpolate the\nsame proximal data but are different convex functions. In particular\n\n    f_phi(x)<=F(x)                                   (6.4)\n\nhas the LOWER-bound orientation for a proposed full terminal upper.\nThe fact that f_phi is legal only shows it is one possible extension.\n\nFor each t>=0 define three sets, including star as the point0:\n\n    C_low(t)={y:phi(y)<=t},\n    C_roof(t)={sum alpha_i*y_i:alpha a probability,\n                             sum alpha_i*delta_i<=t},\n    C_ray(t)=union_(0<=i<=R){theta*y_i:0<=theta<=u_i(t)},\n    u_i(t)=min(1,t/delta_i) if delta_i>0, and1 otherwise.\n\nThen C_roof(t) is the sublevel set of Psi, and\n\n    C_ray(t) subset C_roof(t) subset C_low(t).                       (6.5)\n\nThe first inclusion uses only the probability on star and i; the second\nuses phi<=Psi. All three sets are closed and nonempty; C_roof(t) and C_ray(t) are\ncompact. A Euclidean nearest point to each exists. For C_low(t) this follows\nby restricting a distance-minimizing sequence to a bounded closed ball.\n\nDefine the actual one-anchor selector\n\n    M_1=min_(i,0<=theta<=1)\n                       [2theta*delta_i+||x_n-theta*y_i||^2].\n\nThe three threshold optimizations have exact values\n\n    min_(t>=0)[2t+dist(x_n,C_low(t))^2]=2f_phi(x_n),\n    min_(t>=0)[2t+dist(x_n,C_roof(t))^2]=2F(x_n),\n    min_(t>=0)[2t+dist(x_n,C_ray(t))^2]=M_1.               (6.6)\n\nFor the first two identities, any level gives a trial point with height\nat most t; conversely take the height of an attained envelope minimizer.\nFor C_ray(t), every member is theta*y_i with theta*delta_i<=t, so its\nobjective is at least M_1. Conversely choose a minimizing pair i,theta\nand t=theta*delta_i. This proves equality and attainment.\n\nThe one-anchor threshold optimization is explicitly finite. Set\n\n    d_i=||y_i||^2, b_i=<x_n,y_i>-delta_i,\n    theta_i=clip_[0,1](b_i/d_i) if d_i>0, and0 otherwise,\n    t_i=theta_i*delta_i.\n\nThen the minimum in the third line of (6.6) is attained at one of these\nn listed thresholds, and equals min_i[2t_i+||x_n-theta_i*y_i||^2].\nIf d_i=0, delta_i>=0 makes theta_i=0 minimizing. For d_i>0 this follows\nby completing the scalar quadratic square. Thus no unevaluated threshold\nor new feasibility oracle is offered as a selector.\n\nHere is a VALID paid transfer from the lower level set. For any t>=0,\nlet z_t be its nearest point to x_n and put\n\n    d_t=||x_n-z_t||, e_t=dist(z_t,C_ray(t)).\n\nA nearest point in C_ray(t) and the triangle inequality give\n\n    M_1 <= 2t+(d_t+e_t)^2.                           (6.7)\n\nThe missing alignment payment is 2d_t*e_t+e_t^2. Omitting it reverses\nthe role of the inclusions (6.5). Equivalently, on any original radial\nsegment, convexity gives the nonnegative chord-height error\n\n    eta_i(theta)=theta*delta_i-phi(theta*y_i)>=0.\n\nThe segment cost is EXACTLY\n2phi(theta*y_i)+||x_n-theta*y_i||^2+2eta_i(theta).\nAn affine-support or level-set replacement must pay that error as well\nas any displacement to its chosen point. The next section evaluates\nboth effects explicitly rather than assuming them negligible.\n\nNo bound on e_t or eta_i at the required all-prefix scale has been\nproved. The convex\nroof description does not authorize a two-anchor selector experiment.\n\n## 7. Every physical factor satisfies\n0<kappa*h_i<1/2. Thus the original trajectory is exactly the symbolic\nidentity\n\n    x_i=product_(t<i)(1-kappa*h_t), g_i=kappa*x_i,\n\nand 1=x_0>x_1>...>x_R>x_n>0. No recurrence was numerically replayed.\nAll original ordered pairs, including star and terminal, have\n\n    Q_ij=-kappa*(1-kappa)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0. Then x_n=bX and\n\n    y_i=(1-kappa)*x_i,\n    delta_i=kappa*(1-kappa)*x_i^2/2,\n    delta_R=kappa*X*Y/2.\n\nSince n>=3 and each omitted step exceeds1, S>A+2. (7.2)\n\nAll slopes are positive, so the COMPLETE zero set is (-infinity,Y/2].\nThe true closest initial radius of f_phi is therefore 1-Y/2, strictly\nbetween0 and1. (7.4)\n\nThus even the original static legal function has terminal value larger\nthan that of the supplied affine-height envelope, with the SAME valid\nreference minimum0. The changed true closest radius is stated separately;\nno normalized comparison between two different closest minima is implied.\n\n### 7.2 The maximal extension and exact one-anchor optimum\n\nFor accepted1252's defining H take v_*=kappa*X/2.
