Guidance5db5ffb3f0b50444.\nOne invocation; at most two analytical mechanisms; no formal submissions.\nAll new mathematical arguments form ONE joint candidate. No new fact_id.\nOriginal unrestricted minimax target OPEN.\n\n## Verdict\n\nMechanism1 derives the EXACT one-step hexagonal image for every R>=0 and\nh>1, including both kinds of short original steps. It gives explicit same-m\nwhole-H_j bounds and an exact complete-word scalar loss. At R=S_j+1, the\nscalar composition is S_j/2+1. Combining this with the sharp separate\nEuclidean and terminal-objective conversions gives1/(S_j+1), whose ratio to\n1/(1+2S_j) tends to2. It exceeds17/(16(1+2S_j)) at every j>=1.\nThis is a failure of the scalar relaxation, NOT an actual terminal example\nviolating the triangle bound. One actual fixed-edge trajectory attains every\none-step scalar image on this path, but it does NOT attain the independently\nmaximized terminal objective. The lost orientation is identified explicitly.\nUnbounded initial radius does not force a fixed face; an actual first-leaf\nvertex-to-edge switch at arbitrarily large radius is displayed.\n\nOnly after this decisive failure, Mechanism2 tests the explicit remaining-mass\npotential U_d(x)=max_(g in D)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2), d>=1. It supplies a storage\ncontaining longitudinal position, mass and transverse energy. A direct\none-step inequality is proved for every h<=3/2, and its precise extension\ncondition is given. Long bridges cannot be handled one step at a time.\nMost concretely, the previous strict vertex-switch counterexample is now\npaid over its ENTIRE actual H_3=(H_2,1+beta*c,H_2): for every d>=1 its\nend/start U ratio is less than7/64. An explicit signed-charge inequality\nuses the real initial reserve and the actual outgoing state. This includes\nboth complete children, the true bridge, and the last leaf of that block.\nIt is an all-m calibration family, NOT an all-start/all-depth theorem.\n\nThe universal U_d block inequality remains unproved. Neither a finite image\ncomposition nor the signed-charge telescope is presented as a solution.\nThere is no full triangle bound, full-function W upper bound, or arbitrary-N\nsimplex claim. No original problem quantifier has been changed.\n\n## 1. Frozen word, function, and original scope\n\nFix an integer m>=1536 once and for all. Define\n\n  epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n  c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n  n=2^m-1, h_i=(H_m)_i, S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1)h_i, D_m=1+2S.\n\nThe same m and parameters are used in every child. In particular the final\nphysical step is1+a. No child is treated as a separately parameterized word.\n\nTake the three fixed real vertices\n\n  v_1=(1,0), v_2=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), v_3=(-1/2,-sqrt(3)/2),\n  D=conv{v_1,v_2,v_3},\n  F(x)=max_(g in D)[<x,g>-||g||^2/2], x in R^2,\n  T_h(x)=x-h P_D(x), x_(i+1)=T_(h_i)(x_i),\n  Psi(x)=2F(x), E_m(x_0)=Psi(x_n)/||x_0||^2,\n  V_triangle(m)=sup_(x_0!=0) E_m(x_0).\n\nAll inner products and norms are Euclidean. P_D denotes Euclidean projection\nonto this one fixed triangle. The original W_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex\nL-smooth functions with a minimizer, under steps h_i/L. Hence W=2R, and\nV_triangle is a restricted-family quantity at the specified word. A triangle\nupper bound, even if proved, would not be an upper bound on W.\n\nAccepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 gives the all-split benchmark\nratio B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50. Its799/800 consequence requires the UNPROVED full\nfunction-class bound W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m). No such implication is\ninvoked here. Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the ordered interpolation\nconvention and the need to retain the actual terminal; the legality needed\nhere is also derived directly in Section2. Previous unverified reports are\nawareness only, not premises of this joint candidate.\n\n## 2. Static legality and exact original interpolation rows\n\nThe vertices satisfy ||v_i||=1, sum_i v_i=0, <v_i,v_j>=-1/2 for i!=j, and\nsum_i v_i v_i^T=(3/2)I. Thus\n\n  D={x:<x,v_i>>=-1/2 for all i},\n  ball(0,1/2) subset D subset ball(0,1).\n\nCompleting the square in F identifies its unique maximizer as g=P_D(x).\nThe defining necessary and sufficient projection inequality is\n\n  <x-g,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                         (2.1)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance along a segment toward\nz; sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2. Adding (2.1) at two\ninputs x,y gives ||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2 <= <P_D(x)-P_D(y),x-y>, and then the\nCauchy--Schwarz inequality proves1-Lipschitzness. The maximizing inequalities\nbound F(x+t)-F(x)-<P_D(x),t> between0 and ||t||^2. This proves that F is\ndifferentiable with gradient P_D. F is finite and convex because D is compact\nand F is a maximum of affine functions of x.\n\nF(0)=0 and F>=0. For x!=0 choose0<r<min(1,1/(2||x||)); then rx belongs to\nD, and F(x)>=(r-r^2/2)||x||^2>0. The complete minimum set is{0}. Consequently\n||x_0|| is the actual distance to the closest minimizer.\n\nFor every original query and terminal i=0,...,n, put g_i=P_D(x_i),f_i=F(x_i),\nand include x_*=g_*=f_*=0. For EVERY ordered pair,\n\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n      =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.                            (2.2)\n\nThe inequality is (2.1) at j with z=g_i. Thus incoming/outgoing terminal,\nremote, reversed, and optimum rows all hold for these actual samples.\nMultiplying F by L and using steps h_i/L preserves the points and W score.\n\n## 3. Ordered gaps, metric, values, and face guards\n\nDefine labeled coordinates q_i=(2/3)<x,v_i>. Their sum is0, x=sum_i q_i v_i,\nand ||x||^2=(3/2)sum_i q_i^2. At the current point relabel only for notation\nso that q_1>=q_2>=q_3. A relabeling means a triangle symmetry, not a change\nof the physical function or a replacement of the actual state.\n\nSet\n\n  u=q_1-q_2>=0, v=q_2-q_3>=0,\n  (q_1,q_2,q_3)=((2u+v)/3,(v-u)/3,-(u+2v)/3),\n  A=(2u+v)/3, B=(u+2v)/3,\n  Q(u,v)=||x||^2=u^2+uv+v^2.                            (3.1)\n\nThus the assigned sorted triple(A,B-A,-B) is recovered, and A/2<=B<=2A.\nThe two gaps contain all the actual planar state up to a triangle symmetry.\n\nThe projection regions in this order are\n\n  C: u+2v<=1;\n  V: u>=1;\n  E: 0<=u<=1 and u+2v>=1.                              (3.2)\n\nTheir boundaries overlap and the formulas agree there. With r_i=(2/3)<g,v_i>\nfor g=P_D(x), the respective projection coordinates are\n\n  C: r=q;\n  V: r=(2/3,-1/3,-1/3);\n  E: r=(1/6+u/2,1/6-u/2,-1/3).                         (3.3)\n\nFor C, membership in D is exactly q_i>=-1/3. For V, the projection normal\ninequalities at v_1 are q_1-q_2>=1 and q_1-q_3>=1; ordering reduces these\nto u>=1. For E, the point in (3.3) lies on the edge opposite v_3 precisely\nwhen u<=1. Its residual q-r is a nonnegative multiple of(1,1,-2) precisely\nwhen u+2v>=1; this is the outward edge normal, proving (2.1). These cases\ncover every u,v>=0. This derives (3.2)--(3.3) without an external theorem.\n\nThe actual objective is\n\n  Psi_C=Q(u,v),\n  Psi_V=2u+v-1,\n  Psi_E=(3u^2+2u+4v-1)/4.                              (3.4)\n\nFor the vertex chart set s=<x,v_1>=u+v/2 and let t be the signed coordinate\nperpendicular to v_1. Then t^2=3v^2/4, Q=s^2+t^2, Psi_V=2s-1.\nFor the edge chart let n_E=-v_3, choose a unit edge tangent t_E, and write\nz=<x,n_E>=u/2+v, y=<x,t_E>. Then y^2=3u^2/4, Q=z^2+y^2 and\nPsi_E=z-1/4+y^2. In particular both charts retain their full transverse energy.\n\n## 4. Every exact physical update and every reordering\n\nAll original steps have h>1. Put k=h-1>0. In the following table an ordering\nsuch as213 gives the identities of the old coordinates in new descending\norder. The displayed output is the NEW ordered gap pair(U,V).\n\nCORE, u+2v<=1:\n\n  order321: (U,V)=(k v,k u).                             (4.1)\n\nThis follows from q'=(1-h)q=-kq; it includes ties.\n\nVERTEX, u>=1:\n\n  h<=u:       order123, (U,V)=(u-h,v);\n  u<=h<=u+v:  order213, (U,V)=(h-u,u+v-h);\n  h>=u+v:     order231, (U,V)=(v,h-u-v).                 (4.2)\n\nIndeed the labeled adjacent differences after the actual step are u-h and v.\nThe sign of u-h and then of u+v-h gives exactly these three orders. The last\ntwo old coordinates remain ordered because their gradient coordinates agree.\n\nEDGE, 0<=u<=1 and u+2v>=1: define w=v-h(1-u)/2. Then\n\n  w>=k u:     order213, (U,V)=(k u,w-k u);\n  0<=w<=k u:  order231, (U,V)=(w,k u-w);\n  w<=0:       order321, (U,V)=(-w,k u).                 (4.3)\n\nHere the labeled adjacent differences are -k u and w. Thus the old second\ncoordinate is never below the old first; the signs of w and w-k u give the\ncomplete list. No order-persistence assumption enters (4.1)--(4.3).\n\nFor each output use precisely these next-query guards:\n\n  next C iff U+2V<=1;\n  next V iff U>=1;\n  next E iff U<=1 and U+2V>=1.                          (4.4)\n\nInside a row of (4.1)--(4.3), every boundary in (4.4) is affine in u,v.\nTogether with u,v>=0, the current face boundaries u+2v=1,u=1 and order\nboundaries h=u,h=u+v,w=0,w=k u, this retains all guards. The A,B update is\nA'=(2U+V)/3,B'=(U+2V)/3, so the table is also an explicit map in A,B.\n\nThe formulas agree on shared boundaries. At u+2v=1, w=-k v and the edge\nrow becomes(k v,k u), the core formula. At u=1, w=v and (4.3) becomes the\napplicable vertex row. The adjacent rows agree when w=0,w=k u,h=u or h=u+v.\nNo additional branch is inserted by allowing closed inequalities.\n\nThe exact new squared norm is\n\n  core:   k^2 Q(u,v);\n  vertex: (u-h)^2+(u-h)v+v^2;\n  edge:   k^2 u^2-k u w+w^2.                            (4.5)\n\nThese are permutation-invariant norm expressions for the raw differences.\nIn particular the edge cross term has its displayed MINUS sign.\n\n\n## 5. Exact hexagonal image at every radius and every original step\n\nDefine G(x)=max_i|<x,v_i>| and C_R={x:G(x)<=R}. In the ordered chamber,\n\n  G=max(2u+v,u+2v)/2,\n  C_R={u,v>=0:2u+v<=2R, u+2v<=2R}.\n\nLet k=h-1>0 and M_h(R)=max_(x in C_R)G(T_h x). The exact formula is:\n\nIf h>=2,\n\n  M_h(R)=kR,                                      0<=R<=1;\n  M_h(R)=max{k,R-h/2,(R+h-3/2)/2},                 R>=1.       (5.1)\n\nIf 1<h<2,\n\n  M_h(R)=kR,                                      0<=R<=1/2;\n  M_h(R)=max{k min(R,1),R-h/2,h(2R-1)/4},          1/2<=R<=3/2;\n  M_h(R)=max{k,R-h/2,(R+h-3/2)/2},                 R>=3/2.     (5.2)\n\nEndpoints agree. In particular the actual leaf has h=1+a<3/2, the level1\nbridge has h=1+beta<2, and every higher bridge has h>=2. No leaf is replaced\nby an artificial h=2 step. These formulas are nondecreasing in R.\n\nProof. In the core the image is -kx; its maximum G is k min(R,1).\nFor R<=1/2 the entire hexagon lies in D, so this already proves (5.2)'s\nfirst part. For R>=1/2, in the edge chart the labeled post-step dots are\n\n  s'_1=v/2+(1-3h/4)u-h/4,\n  s'_2=v/2+(3h/4-1/2)u-h/4,\n  s'_3=-(u+2v)/2+h/2.                              (5.3)\n\nThe feasible interval and ceiling for its ordered coordinates are\n\n  0<=u<=U_R:=min(1,(4R-1)/3),\n  (1-u)/2<=v<=min(2R-2u,R-u/2).                    (5.4)\n\nThe ceiling changes at u=2R/3. The negative parts of s'_1 and s'_2 are\nat most k min(R,1), since their values on the lower edge boundary are\n-k(1+3u)/4 and k(3u-1)/4 and u<=U_R. Also s'_1<=s'_2. The third coordinate\ncontributes at most max(k/2,R-h/2); k/2 is covered by the core term.\nAt the upper ceiling, s'_2 equals\n\n  R/2-h/4+3ku/4,                 u<=2R/3;\n  R-h/4+3(h-2)u/4,              u>=2R/3.           (5.5)\n\nFor h<2 these slopes have opposite signs. The maximum is at u=2R/3\nwhen R<=3/2 and at u=1 when R>=3/2, giving respectively h(2R-1)/4 and\n(R+h-3/2)/2. For h>=2 the maximum is at U_R. For R<=1 it is k(R-1/2),\ndominated by kR; for 1<=R<=3/2 it is R+h/2-3/2, dominated by k; for\nR>=3/2 it is (R+h-3/2)/2.\n\nThere is no vertex region for R<1. For R>=1 its post-step dots are\n\n  (2u+v)/2-h, (v-u+h)/2, -(u+2v)/2+h/2, u>=1.\n\nThe first and third absolute values are bounded by max(k,R-h/2).\nThe only further candidate, the positive second dot, has maximum\nR+h/2-3/2 for 1<=R<=3/2 and (R+h-3/2)/2 for R>=3/2. The former is at\nmost k when h>=2. When h<2 it is at most h(2R-1)/4, because their\ndifference is (2-h)(3/4-R/2)>=0. Negative second dots are covered by the\nfirst-coordinate positive bound. This proves all claimed upper bounds.\n\nFor attainment, the core term is attained by x=min(R,1)v_1. A maximizing\nterm R-h/2 is nonnegative and is attained at the edge-axis point with\nu=0,v=R. In the middle part of (5.2), the edge term is attained at\nu=v=2R/3, which satisfies (5.4). In the outer part, the mixed term is\nattained at u=1,v=R-1/2. For (5.1), if this outer term is strictly larger\nthan k then R>h-1/2>=3/2, so this last point is allowed. If it ties k,\nthe core point suffices. All proposed maximizing cases are thus realized.\n\n## 6. Paid norm and objective conversions\n\nFor every actual x,\n\n  G(x)^2<=||x||^2<=4G(x)^2/3.                      (6.1)\n\nFor example, in the chamber u>=v, the difference\n4G^2/3-Q=(u-v)(u+2v)/3 is nonnegative; in the other half chamber\ninterchange u,v. The lower bound follows because each v_i is a unit\nvector. Both constants are attained: on a vertex axis and at u=v.\n\nThe always valid objective estimate is Psi(x)<=||x||^2<=4G(x)^2/3.\nFor every R>=3/2 the SHARP objective maximum on C_R is\n\n  max_(G(x)<=R)Psi(x)=2R-1.                        (6.2)\n\nIndeed in a vertex chart Psi<=2R-1. In an edge chart Psi=z-1/4+y^2\nwith z<=R and y^2<=3/4, so Psi<=R+1/2<=2R-1. In the core Psi<=1,\nalso below 2R-1. The point Rv_1 attains equality. Notice that the\nedge-axis point -Rv_1 instead has Psi=R-1/4. This distinction survives\neven for arbitrarily large R; a gauge radius is not an objective value.\n\n## 7. Explicit same-m complete-H_j envelope estimates\n\nWrite E_j(R) for the chronological composition of the EXACT M_h maps\nover H_j, with E_0(R)=R. It is only a scalar enclosing radius. From\nmonotonicity, every actual run satisfies G(T_(H_j)x)<=E_j(G(x)), where\nT_(H_j) denotes the complete actual composition on the one fixed F.\nIt does not follow that E_j is the true image of the whole block.\n\nPut S_j=sum H_j and eta=a/(c-2). Directly from the literal word,\n\n  1+S_j=(1-eta)2^j+eta c^j,    0<eta<1.            (7.1)\n\nFor clarity, A_j=1+S_j obeys A_(j+1)=2A_j+a c^j; substitution proves\n(7.1), starting at A_0=1. We use the same enclosing m throughout.\nUseful coarse rational bounds are\n\n  2/5<a<5/12, 99/100<beta<1, 12/5<c<5/2,\n  12/5<beta*c<5/2, 1-eta<8epsilon.                 (7.2)\n\nHere lambda>241/100 and lambda<29/12 follow by squaring their positive\nsqrt(2) comparisons. Because epsilon<=1/6144, c<49/20 and beta>99/100,\nso a>99/245>2/5; a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<5/12. Also beta*c=lambda(1-epsilon^2)\n>(241/100)(999/1000)>12/5 and is below lambda<5/2. For the last inequality,\nc(c-2)<1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2 and\n(1-8epsilon)(1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2)<1-epsilon give eta>1-8epsilon.\n\nThe exact ruler prefix products are\n\n  P_ell=product_(r=1)^ell(h_(r-1)-1)\n       =beta^ell c^(-sbit(ell)),                   (7.3)\n\nwhere sbit(ell) is the number of binary ones, and P_0=1. The ruler entry\nis beta*c^(nu_2(r)-1); counting each power-of-two divisor of the integers\n1,...,ell gives sum nu_2(r)=ell-sbit(ell), proving (7.3).\nIn particular P_ell<=a<1 for every nonempty prefix and\nP_block,j:=product_(h in H_j)(h-1)=beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j).\n\nThe following are quantitative complete-word statements, not unevaluated\nmax-plus expressions:\n\n  E_j(R)=P_block,j R,                                  0<=R<=1;\n  E_j(R)<=max(R,a c^(j-1)),                       R>=0,j>=1;\n  E_j(R)<=max(R-S_j/2,C_hex,j),                       R>=0,j>=1;\n  C_hex,j=(3a c^(j-1)-S_(j-1))/2.                    (7.4)\n\nFor the first line, formulas (5.1)--(5.2) give M_h(r)=(h-1)r for\n0<=r<=1 for EVERY original step. For the two short steps this follows\nfrom k>=a>2/5: on 1/2<=r<=1 both r-h/2 and h(2r-1)/4 are at most kr.\nEvery original prefix product is at most1, so no intermediate scalar\nradius leaves this interval. This proves the whole-block equality.\n\nThe other two lines follow from the pointwise bounds\n\n  M_h(R)<=max(R,h-1),\n  M_h(R)<=max(3(h-1)/2,R-h/2),                    (7.5)\n\nvalid for every h>1 and R>=0. To check the second, the mixed outer branch\nis at most3k/2 when R<=2h-3/2 and otherwise at most R-h/2. The short\nmiddle branch is at most the outer mixed branch, since their difference\nis k(R-3/2)/2<=0; the core term is at most k. The first bound follows\ndirectly from (5.1)--(5.2): the mixed outer branch is an average of R and\nk-1/2, and the short middle branch is at most R. The largest k in H_j\nis a c^(j-1), proving the second line of (7.4).\n\nComposing the second max in (7.5) by distributivity gives the upper bound\nmax{R-S_j/2, max_i[3(h_i-1)/2-(mass strictly after i)/2]}.\nAn occurrence of a level-l bridge is followed by its complete H_l right\nchild, so its latter expression is at most (3a c^l-S_l)/2. Equality in\nthis purely affine-max comparison is possible at the last occurrence of\nthat level, whose suffix is exactly H_l. There is no reset of parameters.\nDefine T_l=(3a c^l-S_l)/2. Formula (7.1) gives\n\n  T_l=[1+(3a-eta)c^l-(1-eta)2^l]/2.\n\nThese T_l strictly increase. Indeed 3a-eta=a[3-1/(c-2)]>a/2>1/5,\nand hence (3a-eta)(c-1)>7/25>1-eta by (7.2). Also c^l>=2^l.\nSubtracting adjacent displayed formulas proves T_(l+1)>T_l. The maximum\nis consequently T_(j-1)=C_hex,j, proving the final explicit upper bound.\nIt includes every branch, but C_hex,j grows; it is not a sharp convergence\ntheorem and is not substituted for the desired bound.\n\n## 8. Exact complete-word failure of the scalar relaxation\n\nFor every j>=1 choose the scalar initial radius R_0=S_j+1. At every actual\nposition, let A_i be the original mass already executed. Then\n\n  R_i=S_j+1-A_i/2,\n  M_(h_i)(R_i)=R_i-h_i/2,\n  E_j(S_j+1)=S_j/2+1.                             (8.1)\n\nThis is a symbolic proof for every j, not an executed recurrence. Here is\nwhy EVERY branch choice is valid. Let Hmax=1+a c^(j-1). From (7.1),\n\n  S_j> [c/(c-2)](Hmax-1)-1 >5Hmax-6>4Hmax-5.\n\nThus R_i>=S_j/2+1>2Hmax-3/2>=2h_i-3/2. Also R_i>3/2 since S_j>1.\nIn the outer formulas (5.1)--(5.2), R-h/2 dominates the mixed branch\nexactly when R>=2h-3/2. It also dominates k there, since h>1. This proves\nthe induction in (8.1), including the real last leaf.\n\nCombining the separately sharp conversions (6.1)--(6.2) with this exact\nscalar path gives the certificate value\n\n  [2E_j(R_0)-1]/R_0^2=1/(S_j+1).                  (8.2)\n\nIts ratio to the baseline1/(1+2S_j) is\n(1+2S_j)/(1+S_j)=2-1/(1+S_j). It exceeds17/16 because S_j>1>1/15.\nFor j=m it tends to2 as m tends to infinity, since S_m>=2^m-1.\nThis is a precise, unavoidable loss for the relaxation that keeps only the\nscalar radius, uses these exact one-step images, and applies the sharp\nseparate initial-norm and terminal-objective conversions. Any weaker\none-step bounds or weaker separate conversions cannot repair that loss.\nIt does NOT exclude a bound retaining an objective or directional state.\n\nThe incompatibility is concrete. Start the ACTUAL fixed F at\nx_0=-(S_j+1)v_1. Every point is on the same physical edge-normal ray with\nz_i=S_j+1-A_i/2>1/2 and y_i=0. Its actual gauge is exactly R_i, so ONE\nlegal trajectory attains all the scalar image radii in (8.1). But its\nactual terminal value is S_j/2+3/4, not S_j+1. Its terminal point is on\nan edge axis, whereas the maximizer of (6.2) is on a vertex axis. The\ninitial Euclidean bound is already exact on this edge ray. No orientation\nswitch can be inserted at the terminal of this static trajectory.\n\nAlternatively, the actual start +(S_j+1)v_1 stays in the same vertex\nthrough the terminal and finishes at v_1 with Psi=1. Neither of these\nlegal starts realizes (8.2) as its normalized objective. No full-word\ntriangle counterexample is claimed.\n\n## 9. Actual fixed-face criteria, and why large radius is insufficient\n\nFor a particular vertex, order the other coordinates so its upper gap is\nu_0 and the remaining gap is v_0>=0. The condition\n\n  u_0>=S_m+1                                      (9.1)\n\nis necessary and sufficient for that SAME vertex chart to hold at all\nqueries AND the terminal. Under this condition the gap at any prefix is\nu_0-A_i>=1 and the other gap is unchanged. Conversely, if the chart holds\nthrough the terminal, its affine update gives u_n=u_0-S_m>=1.\n\nFor a fixed physical edge, write x=z n_E+y t_E. The condition\n\n  z_0>=1/2+S_m/2, |y_0|<=sqrt(3)/2                 (9.2)\n\nis necessary and sufficient for that edge chart to be valid throughout,\nincluding terminal. Necessity uses z_n=z_0-S_m/2>=1/2 and initial edge\nmembership. Sufficiency follows from z_i=z_0-A_i/2 and\n|y_i|=P_i|y_0|<=|y_0| using (7.3). Boundary choices are harmless.\n\nBoth fixed-face classes have normalized payoff at most1/D_m. Indeed\nD_m P_block,m^2<1 because (7.1) gives D_m<2c^m and P_block,m=beta^n c^(-m).\nIn a vertex chart the difference between ||x_0||^2 and D_m Psi(x_n)\nis (s_0-D_m)^2+t_0^2. In an edge chart it is\n(z_0-D_m/2)^2+(1-D_m P_block,m^2)y_0^2. The equalities at x_0=D_m v_1 and\nx_0=-(D_m/2)v_1 are legal by (9.1)--(9.2). Thus the classes actually\nattain1/D_m. This is not extended to the face-changing domain.\n\nThere is no scalar large-radius criterion for fixed faces. Choose\n0<delta<a, u_0=1+delta and v_0=T with T arbitrarily large. This is a\nstrict vertex query. The first actual leaf1+a gives ordered gaps\nU=a-delta in (0,1), V=T-a+delta. For T large enough U+2V>1, so the next\nquery is strictly on an edge. Meanwhile ||x_0||^2=u_0^2+u_0 T+T^2 tends\nto infinity. This family rules out assuming that all large starts have\none face. Criteria (9.1)--(9.2), not radius alone, identify the histories\nto which the fixed-face sharpening applies.\n\n## 10. Mechanism2: a remaining-mass potential with longitudinal storage\n\nFor d>=1 define the explicit function\n\n  U_d(x)=max_(g in D){2<x,g>-d||g||^2}\n        =d Psi(x/d)\n        =||x||^2/d-dist(x,dD)^2/d.                 (10.1)\n\nCompleting the square proves every identity. In particular U_1=Psi and\n0<=U_d(x)<=||x||^2/d. Formula (3.4), applied at x/d, evaluates U_d without\nan optimization oracle: in the scaled core it is Q/d; in a scaled vertex\nchart it is 2s-d; in a scaled edge chart it is z-d/4+y^2/d. The scaled\nguards are respectively u+2v<=d, u>=d, and u<=d<=u+2v. Thus both the\nlongitudinal coordinate and the transverse square remain explicit.\n\nThe concrete universal law tested in this mechanism is\n\n  U_d(T_(H_j)x)<=U_(d+2S_j)(x), all x, d>=1, j<=m.  (10.2)\n\nIt is a proposed all-block inequality, NOT a proved theorem. At j=m,d=1\nit would imply the baseline triangle estimate. It is stronger than that\nestimate because of the extra subtracted distance in (10.1), and because\nit quantifies over all trailing masses and all child levels. We do not\ndescribe proving (10.2) as a minor remaining technical task.\n\n## 11. Direct short-step payment and an exact long-step obstruction\n\nThere is a useful direct theorem for one step on ANY nonempty compact\nconvex body D. Let d>=1,h>0, g=P_D(x), q=P_D((x-hg)/d). Then\n\n  U_(d+2h)(x)-U_d(x-hg)\n     >= B(d,h)||q-g||^2,\n  B(d,h)=h[(2-h)(d+2h-1)-h]/(d+2h-1)^2.           (11.1)\n\nProof. Put theta=h/(d+2h-1) in (0,1] and z=theta*g+(1-theta)*q in D.\nWrite x=g+w with <w,q-g><=0, the original projection inequality. Evaluate\nthe maximum U_(d+2h) at z and put e=q-g. Subtracting U_d(x-hg), whose\nmaximizer is q, gives\n\n  -2theta<w,e> + [d-(d+2h)(1-theta)^2]||e||^2.\n\nThe coefficient of <g,e> has canceled because\ntheta(d+2h-1)=h. The first term is nonnegative, and the square coefficient\nequals B(d,h) after expansion. This proves (11.1) directly, including\nboundary projections and without any smoothness or external black box.\n\nFor 0<h<=3/2 and d>=1 one has B(d,h)>=0: the numerator bracket is at\nleast (2-h)2h-h=h(3-2h). Therefore every actual short leaf satisfies\n\n  U_d(T_(1+a)x)<=U_(d+2(1+a))(x).                  (11.2)\n\nFor 3/2<h<2 the same proof works if\nd>=h/(2-h)-2h+1. This condition is stated, not assumed for every actual\nlevel1 step. In particular it must be checked against the real suffix mass.\n\nThe proposed ONE-STEP U payment is false at every long bridge h>2, even\non actual reachable inputs. Then\n\n  U_d(T_h x)=(h-1)^2||x||^2/d\n             >||x||^2/(d+2h)=U_(d+2h)(x).         (11.3)\n\nAt any specified original bridge this x is reachable by taking an\nappropriately small nonzero initial point along the same axis: the finite\nprefix products are all nonzero, so the initial radius can be chosen to\nkeep the entire prefix in the core and attain the specified tiny bridge\ninput. No arbitrary cloud of unrelated states is used in this test.\n\nThis failure does NOT refute (10.2), which groups both real children with\ntheir bridge. Nor does the short-step result prove that block law by\niteration. The exact interface is the compensation from BOTH complete\nchild runs, not an unearned monotonicity across the bridge.\n\n## 12. Joint derivation of forecasts and the block-charge interface\n\nFor the actual seven projection charts f define orthogonal projectors and\noffsets P_f,b_f by\n\n  core: P_f=I,b_f=0;\n  edge: P_f=t_E t_E^T,b_f=n_E/2;\n  vertex: P_f=0,b_f=v_j.\n\nTheir ranges are orthogonal to their offsets. In the actual chart,\nP_D(x)=P_f x+b_f and Psi(x)=||P_f x||^2+2<b_f,x>-||b_f||^2.\nThese are exactly the cell formulas derived in Sections2--4.\n\nAt each original position i, including the terminal, set\n\n  s_i=sum_(t=i)^(n-1)h_t,\n  d_i=1+2s_i,\n  p_i=product_(t=i)^(n-1)(h_t-1),\n  L_i^f(x)=p_i^2||P_f x||^2+2<b_f,x>-d_i||b_f||^2. Hence\n\n  L_i^f(x_i)=L_(i+1)^f(x_(i+1))                   (12.2)\n\nwhen f is the actual present chart; no future chart persistence is used.\nChoose the smallest face containing the projection at each actual point,\nand let sigma_i be its chart. (3.2)\n\nThe explicit formula starts at1 and satisfies the recurrence because\n2*eta+a=c*eta. We also need, at every small child as well,\n\n    S_j/d_j>7/5, j>=1.                               (3.3)\n\nThe base is S_1/d_1=ell/beta>7/5, since a>2/5 and beta<1.\nIf L_j=S_j/d_j>7/5, the literal mass identity gives\n\n    S_(j+1)/d_(j+1)=(2*L_j+1+1/d_j)/c\n                     >(19/5)/(17/7)=133/85>7/5.\n\nThis is a symbolic induction at the fixed enclosing m; no recurrence\nis executed or evaluated.\n\nFor any physical level-k occurrence inside H_j, its full adjacent H_k\nchildren imply that its prefix and suffix masses P,T satisfy\n\n    P>=S_k, T>=S_k, P+T=S_j-h_k^lev.                  (3.4)\n\nIts entire actual remaining suffix has the form\n\n    H_k,h_(l_1)^lev,H_(l_1),...,h_(l_t)^lev,H_(l_t),\n    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                              (3.5)\n\nTo obtain it, ascend the binary tree after the right H_k, skip ancestors\nalready entered through a right child, and at each remaining left-child\nancestor take its bridge and entire right child. Each remaining physical\nupdate occurs exactly once. At a final leaf this suffix is empty.\n\n## 4. Monotone segments and one crossing at any physical position\n\nFor a segment actually staying on one ray of curvature p and threshold u,\nlet R,z be its initial/terminal radii and T its physical mass. Radii decrease,\nso all queried gradient magnitudes are at least p*min(z,u). Consequently\n\n    R>=z+p*T*min(z,u).                                (4.1)\n\nIf z<=u this bounds terminal twice-energy by p*R^2/(1+p*T)^2.\nIf z>=u use\n\n    (z+p*T*u)^2-(1+2*p*T)*(2*u*z-u^2)\n                           =(z-(1+p*T)*u)^2.\n\nBoth cases give\n\n    2*f(terminal)<=p*R^2/(1+2*p*T).                   (4.2)\n\nFor a history with exactly one strict crossing, let p,v be source and\ndestination curvatures, h the crossing step and P,T its prefix/suffix\nmasses. At source radius y put t=min(y,u_source). The prefix gives\nR>=y+p*P*t>=(1+p*P)*t, so its landing z and terminal energy satisfy\n\n    z<=[(h*p-1)/(1+p*P)]*R,\n    2*f(terminal)/R^2\n       <=[v/(1+2*v*T)]*[(h*p-1)/(1+p*P)]^2.           (4.3)\n\nA strict crossing requires h*p>1. The two positive factors increase in\ntheir own curvatures: their derivatives are1/(1+2*v*T)^2 and\n(h+P)/(1+p*P)^2. Both can therefore be replaced by K in this UPPER\nEXPRESSION. This is not a claim that actual trajectories are monotone\nin their curvature.\n\nFor a level-k cut in H_j put C=1+K*S_k and e=K*h_k^lev-1. If e<=0\nno curvature at most K crosses. Otherwise\n\n    0<e/C<=d_k/A_k<c-2<1/2.                          (4.4)\n\nThe first comparison follows by increasing K to1. Put\nN=K*(S_j-S_(k+1)) and x=K*(P-S_k); then0<=x<=N and\n\n    (1+K*P)^2*(1+2*K*T)\n      =(C+x)^2*(2*C-1+2*N-2*x).                     (4.5)\n\nIts derivative is2*(C+x)*(C-1+2*N-3*x), so its minimum is at an\nendpoint. The value at N minus its value at0 is\nN*(2*C*(C-1)+N*(2*C-1))>=0. Thus (4.5) is at least\nC^2*(2*C-1+2*N). Also1+2*K*S_j=4*C+2*e-1+2*N, and\n\n    e^2*(4*C+2*e-1)/(C^2*(2*C-1))\n        <(5*C-1)/(4*(2*C-1))<=1.                    (4.6)\n\nThe last inequality is3*(C-1)>=0. Adding the2*N terms preserves the\ncomparison since e^2<C^2. Together these establish, on EVERY H_j\nhistory with at most one strict crossing,\n\n    2*f(Psi_j(s))/s^2<=K/(1+2*K*S_j).                 (4.7)\n\nA zero hit has zero terminal gap. A no-crossing history uses (4.2).\nAlso the FIRST crossing of any H_j history has landing radius z obeying\n\n    z/|s|<=(p*h_k^lev-1)/(1+p*P)\n           <=d_k/(1+P)<=d_k/A_k<c-2.                (4.8)\n\nAll of these statements have been derived here as part of the joint draft.\n\n## 5. Joint complementary proof for q<=q_cut\n\nReflect so q is on the negative ray and K on the positive ray. Put j=m-1.\nEvery step in each H_j child obeys q*h<=q*h_(m-2)^lev<=1. The negative\nradius map r -> r-q*h*min(r,u_minus) is nonnegative, nondecreasing and\nat most r. A child therefore has at most one crossing and obeys (4.7).\n\nWithout a negative-to-positive return, the whole H_m has at most one\ncrossing and (4.7) applies directly. Otherwise the sole such return\nis the root h_j^lev. Its excess e_q=q*h_j^lev-1>0 satisfies\n\n    e_q<=h_j^lev/h_(j-1)^lev-1<c-1.                  (5.1)\n\nIf the initial start is negative, its entire left child stays on q;\n(4.3) at the root bounds its positive landing by(c-2)*|s|.\nIf the start is positive, its first crossing is inside the left child;\n(4.8) bounds that landing by(c-2)*|s|, and the rest of that child\nonly decreases the negative radius. The root multiplies a negative\nquery radius by at most e_q when it crosses, including clipped queries.\nIn both cases its positive landing z satisfies\n\n    z/|s|<(c-1)*(c-2)<30/49.                         (5.2)\n\nThe FULL right H_j starts at this actual z and may cross once again;\nit obeys (4.7). Thus histories with THREE full-word crossings are included.\n\nFor j=m-1>=5, d_j>(2/5)*2^5>9 and (3.2) gives\nS_j>7*d_j/3-1>2*(1+d_j). Therefore S_m=2*S_j+h_j^lev<(5/2)*S_j and\n\n    (1+2*K*S_m)/(1+2*K*S_j)<5/2.\n\nUsing the full right child and (5.2) bounds every returning history by\n\n    2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2\n       <(2250/2401)*K/(1+2*K*S_m).                  (5.3)\n\nSince2250<2401, this and the no-return bound prove the required upper\nprofile throughout q<=q_cut. The next sections cover ALL earlier common\nreversals at once, without retaining monotonicity above J.\n\n## 6. Exact interval maps and no-crossing flow bounds\n\nFor ray sigma with curvature p, cap b, threshold u=b/p, input radii\n0<=r<=R and physical step h, the largest output radii staying on that\nray and crossing to the other ray are\n\n    Stay(R)=[R-h*p*min(R,u)]_+,\n    Cross(R)=(h*p-1)_+*min(R,u).                     (6.1)\n\nThe scalar pre-sign expression has slope1-h*p in the core and slope1\non the tail, so its extrema occur at0, the threshold or the outer endpoint.\nFor an input interval[-R_minus,R_plus], its output on ray sigma is the\nmaximum of Stay_sigma(R_sigma) and Cross_tau(R_tau), tau the other ray.\nThe update is continuous and fixes0; its image is the entire interval\nbetween these attained endpoints. Chronological interval composition is\ntherefore exact. It does not choose incompatible maximizing child starts.\nInterval inclusion also makes the endpoint map nondecreasing in its bounds.\n\nFor a segment actually staying on one ray define\n\n    V_M(R;u)=R*exp(-M),                        R<=u,\n             R-M*u,                          R>=u, M<=R/u-1,\n             u*exp(R/u-1-M),                  R>=u, M>=R/u-1.\n\nThen, for physical mass T,\n\n    terminal radius<=V_(p*T)(R;u).                    (6.2)\n\nThis follows directly even when an effective step exceeds1. In the core,\na noncrossing step has hp<=1 and1-hp<=exp(-hp). A tail step remaining on\nthe tail agrees with the formula. A tail step entering the core without\ncrossing gives u*(1-z)<=u*exp(-z), where z=hp-(R/u-1) in[0,1].\nThe displayed V maps spend the first max(0,R/u-1) units of effective mass\non the linear tail and all later mass in the exponential core. This proves\ntheir additive semigroup property. They are nondecreasing in R, so induction\napplies. Their derivatives with respect to u in the three pieces are0,\n-M, and exp(R/u-1-M)*(1-R/u), respectively, all nonpositive; the pieces\nagree at their boundaries. Hence increasing u decreases V. This comparison\nis for V, not for an actual point map with hp>1.\n\nFor ANY j>=1,p in(0,1], and threshold u>=w/(2*p), every no-crossing\nH_j segment from radius at most d_j*w satisfies\n\n    p*terminal_radius<2*w/5.                         (6.3)\n\nPut P=p*d_j and M=p*S_j. Equations (3.2)-(3.3) give\nM>(7/5)*P and M>(7/3)*P-1. Compare to threshold w/(2*p).\nIf P<=1/2 the normalized radius is at most\nP*exp(-7*P/5)<=exp(-7/10)/2<5/17<2/5, since this comparison\nfunction increases on[0,1/2].\nIf1/2<=P<=1, then M>2*P-1 and (6.2) bounds it by\nexp(3*P/5-1)/2<=exp(-2/5)/2<5/14<2/5.\nIf P>=1, again M>2*P-1 and the bound is\nexp(-P/3)/2<=exp(-1/3)/2<3/8<2/5.\nThe rational comparisons use exp(t)>1+t. Equality at a threshold\nbelongs to both adjacent formulas.\n\nA larger-input counterpart is: if u>=2*w/p, a no-crossing H_j segment\nfrom radius at most c*d_j*w has\n\n    p*terminal_radius<w.                            (6.4)\n\nLet L_j=S_j/d_j>7/5. If c*P<=2, its normalized output is at most\nc*P*exp(-L_j*P), whose maximum is c/(exp(1)*L_j)<85/98<1,\nusing exp(1)>2. If c*P>=2, L_j>7/5>c/2 ensures core entry, and\nthe bound is2*exp(c*P/2-1-L_j*P)<2/exp(1)<1. This proves (6.4).\n\n## 7. Arbitrary-J literal base, all curvature contrasts\n\nAssume q>q_cut. If J>=1, every step in H_J has q*h<=1. The q-ray is\ninvariant; the other ray may have effective steps far above2. There is\nat most one strict crossing in H_J, from K to q. We bound its actual\noutput using its whole physical suffix, not monotonicity of the K map.\n\nSet\n\n    C_return=c*(c-2)^2/(c-1).\n    C_return<765/2401<2/5,\n    c*C_return<13005/16807<1.                        (7.1)\n\nThe comparisons use c<17/7, c-2<3/7 and c-1>7/5.\n\nSuppose the crossing is at level k<J with prefix/suffix masses P,T.\nFrom initial radius at most d_J*w, (4.3) and increasing its source\ncurvature to1 bound its landing radius z by\n\n    z<=d_J*w*d_k/(1+P).                              (7.2)\n\nThe key exact physical cut estimate is\n\n    q*d_J*d_k/[(1+P)*(1+q*T)]<C_return.              (7.3)\n\nTo prove it, put N=S_J-S_(k+1), x=P-S_k, so0<=x<=N and T=S_k+N-x.\nThe denominator (A_k+x)*(1+q*(S_k+N-x)) is concave in x. Its endpoint\nvalue at N minus the value at0 is N*(1-q)>=0. Its minimum is therefore\nat0. Writing t=q*d_J, the left side is at most\n\n    t*(d_k/A_k)/(1+q*(S_J-S_k-h_k^lev)).\n\nEquation (3.2) gives A_J-A_k>=(d_J-d_k)/(c-2). Also q*d_k<=t/c,\nso q*(S_J-S_k-h_k^lev)>=t/[c*(c-2)]-q. The base condition\nq*h_(J-1)^lev<=1 gives1-q>=t/c. Combining these facts and\nd_k/A_k<c-2 bounds the last display strictly by\n\n    t*(c-2)/[t*(c-1)/(c*(c-2))]=C_return.\n\nNow set Z=q*terminal_radius/w and B=b_destination/w. The monotone\nq-ray suffix and (7.2) imply\n\n    Z+q*T*min(Z,B)<=q*z/w.\n\nIf B>=1/2 then (7.3) forces Z<C_return: Z>=B would make the left\nside at least B*(1+q*T), impossible since C_return<B; for Z<B the\nleft side equals Z*(1+q*T). Thus each crossing base history has signed\nlinear radius less than(2/5)*w. Each no-crossing history obeys (6.3).\nThis proves the r=0 base in (1.3) for BOTH choices of w.\n\nFor every r>=0 repeat this argument with w_r=w*theta^r. The fixed caps\nare still at least w_r/2. Equations (6.3),(7.2)-(7.3) give output signed\nlinear radius below(2/5)*w_r<theta*w_r from input d_J*w_r.\nThus all geometric base caps hold directly. No homogeneity across a\nclipping threshold, or convexity of a returning point map, is presumed.\n\nIf J=0 use the literal base H_1=(ell). For input radius\nd_1*w*theta^r=beta*w*theta^r, every cross endpoint from (6.1) has\nsigned linear radius at most\nkappa_destination*a*beta*w*theta^r<=theta*w*theta^r.\nBoth effective leaf steps exceed1. The only additional same-ray endpoint\nis a saturated tail, whose signed linear radius is at most\n\n    kappa_sigma*w*theta^r*(beta-ell/2)_+\n                        <=theta*w*theta^r.           (7.4)\n\nUse b_sigma>=w/2, theta^r<=1, and\ntheta-beta+ell/2=(1+a)/2-beta*(1-a)>(3*a-1)/2>0.\nThis proves the leaf base at every geometric scale, including threshold\nties. H_0 is not treated as a contracting block.\n\nFor the separated-cap case we also need, at the larger base interval,\n\n    kappa_plus*max(0,Psi_(j_base)(s))<=w\n                whenever |s|<=c*d_(j_base)*w.         (7.5)\n\nFor J>=1, a no-crossing positive terminal obeys (6.4). A crossing ending\npositive obeys (7.2)-(7.3) with the input bound multiplied by c; its factor\nis c*C_return<1, while its destination cap is at least2*w. The same\ninversion proves (7.5). For J=0, a negative-to-positive leaf crossing\nhas signed linear radius at most\nkappa_plus*(ell-1/kappa_minus)*w<=a*w<w.\nA same-positive history obeys (6.4); zero contributes nothing.\n\nAs an explicit bounded-contrast check, for J>=1 and K/q<=2 all H_J steps have\nK*h<=2. A noncrossing step decreases radius and objective. A crossing\nis K-to-q and lands at radius at most its input radius; from a core its\nnew twice-energy is at most q times the old radius squared, and from a\nsource tail it lands within u_K while the old twice-energy is at least\nK*u_K^2. Energy therefore also decreases. This literal H_J base is then\nnonincreasing in both physical radius and objective, with at most one\ncrossing. The quantitative proof above also handles K/q>2.\n\n## 8. Source-cap capture through every true ancestor suffix\n\nSuppose a crossing occurs at k<J, its source gradient cap is at most w,\nand its destination cap is at least w/2. Its destination is the q-ray\nand its landing radius is at most d_k*w, because\n\n    (h_k^lev-1/kappa_source)*b_source<=d_k*w.\n\nThe following ACTUAL H_k preserves that ray. Put r=q*d_k, M=q*S_k.\nThen\n\n    r<=1-q,\n    M>=r/(c-2)-q>=(1+1/(c-2))*r-1.                  (8.1)\n\nWrite C_low=1+1/(c-2)>10/3. If r<=1/2, the monotone destination\nflow bound gives normalized signed linear radius at most\nr*exp(-M)<=exp(1)*r*exp(-C_low*r)<=1/C_low<3/10.\nIf r>=1/2, the comparison threshold w/2 gives the bound\nexp(-(C_low-2)*r)/2<exp(-2/3)/2<3/10.\nThese estimates remain valid for k=0 with H_0 empty and M=0.\nIn particular this landing plus its full right H_k gives signed linear\nradius below(2/5)*w<theta*w.\n\nEvery remaining ancestor of level below J preserves the q-ray and\ndecreases radius. At the first ancestor l>=J, if any, its input is in\nthe core because theta*w<min b. The bridge sends it to physical radius\n\n    <=(h_l^lev-1/kappa_source)*theta*w\n    <=d_l*theta*w.                                  (8.2)\n\nIf the H_l plateau is already available, the following full right child\ncaptures it to signed linear radius at most theta*w. Every later\nancestor is treated in the literal increasing order (3.5).\n\nIf the crossing level k>=j_base, its immediate landing is at most d_k*w;\nthe already available H_k plateau gives the same cap, followed by (8.2)\nat each later ancestor. The remaining exceptional case is k=0,J=0:\nits landing signed linear radius is at most a*w. If this is the final\nleaf, this is the actual terminal bound. Otherwise a*w<min b since\na<1/2, and its next ancestor l>=1 sends it to physical radius at most\na*d_l*w<=d_l*w; the H_l plateau captures it.\n\nThus, whenever all needed proper-child plateaus have been established,\n\n    crossing from a ray with b_source<=w,\n    with both fixed caps >=w/2\n        => terminal signed linear radius <=a*w.      (8.3)\n\nThis applies to EVERY crossing in the comparable-cap case. In the\nseparated-cap case it applies to every negative-to-positive return,\nsince b_minus=w and b_plus>=2*w. It is NOT asserted for an arbitrary\ncrossing from the larger gradient cap there.\n\nWhen applied inside H_j, every plateau used by (8.3) has level at most\nj-1. The parent currently being proved is never used as a premise.\nThe argument is a finite induction on the true suffix (3.5), including\nthe original final leaf and actual destination curvature at each step.\n\n## 9. Comparable gradient caps: capture and plateau induction\n\nSuppose max b/min b<=2 and w=max b. Define physical capture radii\n\n    C_(sigma,j)=b_sigma*(S_j+1/kappa_sigma).\n\nFor every j>=0 the exact capture identity is\n\n    Psi_j([-C_(minus,j),C_(plus,j)])=[-u_minus,u_plus]. (9.1)\n\nAn exterior start on ray sigma at radius at least C_(sigma,j) stays\nsaturated throughout H_j: after any prefix mass P its radius is at least\nb_sigma*(S_j-P+1/kappa_sigma)>=u_sigma. Its exact output radius is\nR-S_j*b_sigma.\n\nFor (9.1), H_0 is the identity. If capture holds at j, the first child of\nthe parent capture interval has radii\nb_sigma*(S_j+h_j^lev+1/kappa_sigma), by the exterior formula and central\ncapture. Its bridge same-ray endpoint is exactly C_(sigma,j). All\nopposite excursions are at most\n\n    (h_j^lev-1/kappa_tau)_+*b_tau<=d_j*b_tau\n       <2*(c-2)*A_j*b_sigma\n       <b_sigma*(S_j+1/kappa_sigma)=C_(sigma,j).\n\nHere1/kappa_sigma>=1 and2*(c-2)<1. Thus the bridge image is exactly\nthe child capture interval, and the second child proves (9.1). This\nworks also when just one or neither effective bridge exceeds1.\n\nFor j>=j_base suppose (1.3) holds. From parent input radius c*d_j*w,\ncapture and its exterior branches bound the first-child endpoints by\n\n    R_sigma<=max(u_sigma,c*d_j*w-S_j*b_sigma).        (9.2)\n\nBoth effective bridge steps now exceed1. Its cross endpoints are at most\nd_j*w. Its same-ray tail is bounded by the maximum of a nonpositive\nterm and c*d_j*w-(S_j+h_j^lev)*b_sigma, which is at most d_j*w since\n\n    S_j+h_j^lev=A_j+d_j\n       >(1+1/(c-2))*d_j>=2*(c-1)*d_j,\n    w/b_sigma<=2.                                  (9.3)\n\nThe bridge image is inside[-d_j*w,d_j*w], and the full second child\nproves the parent plateau at r=0.\n\nFor r>=1, c*theta<1 gives\nc*d_j*w*theta^r<=d_j*w*theta^(r-1). The first-child plateau bounds\neach signed linear radius by w*theta^r, within both cores since\ntheta*w<min b. The bridge sends either point to physical radius at most\nd_j*w*theta^r. The second-child plateau at r gives signed linear radius\nat most w*theta^(r+1). This proves (1.3) at every j>=j_base, starting\nfrom the literal bases in Section7. Section8 now also proves (8.3)\nfor every crossing in the full H_m.\n\n## 10. Separated gradient caps: joint plateau and escape induction\n\nReflect so U=b_plus>=2*b_minus=2*w. Either curvature may be smaller.\nWe prove (1.3) and (1.4) together. Section7 supplies the base plateau.\n\nFirst prove base escape. For J>=1, a no-crossing history whose terminal\nsigned linear radius exceeds w has all queried gradients at least w,\nso its radius drops by at least S_J*w. If a crossing occurs it is K-to-q.\nLet h,P,T be its step and masses, y its source radius, z its landing,\nr its terminal radius, and v its source gradient magnitude. The escape\nhypothesis q*r>w gives z-r>=T*w on the monotone q-ray suffix.\nAlso q*h<=1 and z=h*v-y<h*v, whence\n\n    v>z/h>=r/h>w/(q*h)>=w.\n\nThe source prefix gives |s|>=y+P*v and y>=v/K, so\n\n    |s|-z>=2*y+(P-h)*v>=(2/K+P-h)*v,\n    3*(2/K+P-h)-(P+h)\n      =6/K+2*P-4*h>=2*A_k-4*d_k>0.                 (10.1)\n\nUse P>=S_k at the crossing level. The coefficient is positive, and\nadding z-r>=T*w gives |s|-r>=S_J*w/3.\n\nIf J=0 the base is H_1. A no-crossing history is as above. A crossing\nhas landing radius r<=a*|s|. If its terminal signed linear radius exceeds\nw then r>w/kappa_destination>=w, and\n|s|-r>=(1/a-1)*r>w>ell*w/3. Thus base escape holds in this case too.\n\nAt each induction step we also need\n\n    kappa_plus*max(0,Psi_j(s))<=w\n                     for |s|<=c*d_j*w.              (10.2)\n\nFor j=j_base this is (7.5). For j>j_base, a negative-to-positive\nreturn gives signed linear radius at most a*w by Section8 using only\nproper-child plateaus. Without such a return a positive terminal requires\nthe entire child to stay positive. Its root level j-1>=J has\nkappa_plus*h_(j-1)^lev>1, so a positive output there requires a saturated\nroot query. Every earlier positive query is then saturated as well,\nbecause radii decrease. Its initial radius must exceed\n\n    (S_(j-1)+h_(j-1)^lev)*U\n      >=2*(1+1/(c-2))*d_(j-1)*w\n      >c^2*d_(j-1)*w=c*d_j*w,                       (10.3)\n\na contradiction. The strict comparison uses\n2*(1+1/(c-2))>20/3>25/4>c^2. This proves (10.2).\n\nSuppose plateau and escape hold at j. At unscaled parent input radius\nc*d_j*w, the first child's positive endpoint is at most w/kappa_plus.\nIts negative endpoint obeys, by escape,\n\n    R_minus<=max(w/kappa_minus,(c*d_j-S_j/3)*w).     (10.4)\n\nBoth bridge effective steps exceed1. Formula (6.1) bounds both cross\nendpoints by d_j*w. Its positive tail is nonpositive. Its negative\ntail is at most the maximum of a nonpositive term and\n(c*d_j-S_j/3-h_j^lev)*w, which is at most d_j*w because\n\n    (c-2)*d_j<=1+S_j/3.                             (10.5)\n\nIndeed d_j<(c-2)*(1+S_j) and(c-2)^2<1/3. The actual bridge image\nis inside[-d_j*w,d_j*w], so the full second child gives the parent\nplateau at r=0. For r>=1 use the geometric argument following (9.3):\nboth child caps are in their respective cores; each source-to-destination\nbridge gives physical radius at most d_j*w*theta^r. This proves the\nparent plateau FIRST, without the parent escape conclusion.\n\nFor parent escape write y=Psi_j(s), z=y-h_j^lev*g(y), t=Psi_j(z).\nSuppose its terminal signed linear radius exceeds w. If t>0, Section8\nexcludes every negative-to-positive return using plateaus through H_j.\nThe entire parent path is positive and every query gradient exceeds w;\nhence s-t>=S_(j+1)*w, stronger than needed.\n\nLet t=-r<0 with kappa_minus*r>w. Child escape and plateau imply\n\n    |z|>=r+S_j*w/3, |z|>d_j*w.                      (10.6)\n\nThe latter follows because an input radius at most d_j*w would end with\nsigned linear radius at most theta*w. Three cases remain.\n\nA. z>0. A negative y could cross from the small-cap ray to at most\nd_j*w, contrary to (10.6). Hence y>0 remains positive at the bridge.\nSince the effective step exceeds1, this query is saturated and\ny=z+h_j^lev*U>u_plus. The first child cannot have returned from the\nnegative ray, by Section8; all its queries are positive saturated.\nThus s=z+(S_j+h_j^lev)*U. Equation (10.6) gives\ns>=r+(2*S_j+h_j^lev)*w/3.\n\nB. z<0,y<0. The bridge is negative saturated, so\n|y|=|z|+h_j^lev*w>w/kappa_minus. Original problem and accepted interfaces\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum\nof 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite Euclidean\ndimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,\nand x_0!=x_star, using x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). (4.4)\n\nThus no finite pair is omitted or replaced by a feasibility assumption.\nThe total finite-prefix outgoing mass at i, including its optimum edge,\nis N_ii; the incoming prefix mass is N_ii-2alpha_i; the terminal adds\n2alpha_i. Finite-node divergence is zero, terminal divergence is2,\nand optimum divergence is-2, even before any sign claim.\n\nFor prefix indices, including star,\nQ_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>. The remaining prefix quadratic\nis -g^T N y=-<x_0,(Pe-d)^T y>+y^T P y. The terminal contribution\nWITH -2f_n included is\n\n    ||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2\n                                         -2sum_i alpha_i delta_i.\n\nIts value terms cancel the prefix divergence terms. Since\nPe=Ev+Fz and eta E^2=p^2-s^2, the full identity is\n\n    M(q):=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n      =||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n        +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n        +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.           (4.5)\n\nWith ybar_w=sum_i w_i y_i/Ww and e^T zeta=0, this becomes\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n      +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(zeta_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n      +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                      (4.6)\n\nThe initial charge is paid once. Necessity of q>=p^2+J follows by\nsetting scalar x_0=1, y_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i),\nc0=1+sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i E), and g_n=-s+v^T y;\nthen every square vanishes. Equation (4.1) reconstructs all gradients.\nThus full PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J, on the entire original formal\nspace, not just on legal trajectories. On the actual common queried\ngradient direction g_i=G, put X=t^T Kt, Z=t^T z and theta=t^T zeta.\nThe entire prefix remainder is\n\n    (q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>+(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2.\n\nThe common derivative has not been discarded.\n\n## 5. The literal metric, paid column, skew, and all optimum signs\n\nThe fixed common column and incoming optimum load are\n\n    z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, Z=t^T z=0,\n    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload*e_0.\n\nThis z is not proportional to v, since its interior coordinates vanish\nand every odd interior v_i is strictly negative. Choose\n\n    xiM=a(1-a)-a^3/8, sigma=xiM/(1-xiM), omega=p^2,\n    what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega*rho,\n    theta0=2-6p, theta=theta0+ellload,\n    Xreq=theta0/p-4+ellload=2/p-10+ellload,\n    tbase=sum_i what_i t_i/sum_i what_i,\n    Vbase=sum_i what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,\n    scale=Xreq/Vbase, w_i=scale*what_i,\n    zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.                            (5.1)\n\nOne has 0<xiM<1/4 and0<sigma<1/3: positivity follows from\n1-a-a^2/8>1-5/12-25/1152>0, and xiM<a(1-a)<=1/4.\nEvery what_i is positive. The two unit endpoints imply\nVbase>=Delta^2/2 by minimizing their two square terms over the center.\nAlso Xreq>0. Consequently all w_i>0, X=Xreq, e^T zeta=0,\nt^T zeta=theta, and\n\n    J=theta^2/(4Xreq).\n\nUseful exact bounds are\n\n    1<theta0<2, 2<theta<3,\n    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p, Xreq<2/p,\n    1<Xreq/Delta<2, |Xreq/Delta-2|<8p,\n    theta/Xreq>p.                                  (5.2)\n\nFor the first lower bound on Xreq, theta0/p>1024*4 and\nXreq=theta0/p-4+ellload. For the ratio, subtract2 from\n[2-(10-ellload)p]/[1-(b+1)p]; the numerator difference is\n(2b-8+ellload)p, negative in absolute value less than8p after division\nby1-(b+1)p>3/4. The lower ratio1 follows directly from the same formula.\nFinally theta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+4p>0.\n\nGeneral multiplication in (4.2) gives\n\n    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta,\n    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha,\n    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.                (5.3)\n\nThe last two identities use e^T A=-d^T and t^T At=0. Here\nalpha^T t=1/p-3, V=2 and Z=0, so the two moments in (5.3) are both\nellload, precisely matching nu_target. A literal skew realizing both\nAe=d and this nu is as follows:\n\n    rvec=Kt+2v-2alpha-nu_target,\n    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean*e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,\n    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,\n    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,\n    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.               (5.4)\n\nThe two matching moments imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.\nHence e^T fvec=0 and cvec^T fvec=0. Direct multiplication proves\nAe=d and At=rvec; each summand is skew. Every other compatible skew\nis A+C with C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0. Equations (4.3)-(4.4) and (5.4)\ndefine every actual coefficient, with the literal law taking C=0.\n\nAll terminal multipliers are nonnegative. Incoming optimum multipliers\nare exactly ellload at index0 and zero elsewhere. Every outgoing\noptimum multiplier is strictly positive: zeta_i has the sign of\nt_i-tbase, changes sign only once, and has zero sum. Every proper\nsuffix sum is positive, using either the negative preceding prefix or\nthe strictly positive remaining tail. If zeta_i<0, then\nsum_(j>i)zeta_j>=-zeta_i, giving\n\n    (Ltri^T zeta)_i=zeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j\n                                  >=(h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0.\n\nIf zeta_i>=0 the expression is positive as well, including i=l.\nTogether with R_i>0 this proves mu_i>0 for all queries.\n\nFull PSD also passes. By (5.2),\n\n    J<(theta0*p/4)(1+ellload/theta0)^2*(1024/1023)\n      <(4225/8184)p.\n\nSince4225/8184<67/128 and p<1/128, one gets\n\n    p^2+J<(17/32)p<17p/[16(2-p)]=qtar.             (5.5)\n\nThus PSD and every optimum sign are valid for this SIGNED coefficient\nlaw. The remaining finite coefficients must still be nonnegative to\nconstitute an upper-bound certificate; Section7 proves they cannot be.\n\n## 6. Both endpoint row-moment screens pass\n\nAll identities in this section follow from (4.2)-(5.4), not from\nassumed finite multiplier signs. They are unchanged by every C with\nCe=Ct=0.\n\nFor the first row define\n\n    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,\n    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,\n    G0=P0-Delta*S0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-T)N_0j.\n\nThe first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T. Since\nPe-d=2s v+2Fz-zeta and (P+A)t=2Pt-2alpha-nu, direct multiplication gives\n\n    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,\n    P0=b(2sV+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0\n                                  +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0.       (6.1)\n\nIf every finite first-row multiplier is nonnegative, then\n\n    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0,                      (6.2)\n\nbecause b<=t_j-1<=Delta for j>0. These necessary conditions comprise\nthe complete total/first-moment screen for that row; they are not\nindividual coefficient signs.\n\nPut Y=-(Kt)_0=w_0(tbase-1)>0 and dclock=1/Delta. Normalize clocks by\nxclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta in[0,1]. For any base what define\n\n    B=sum what, M=sum what*xclock, M2=sum what*xclock^2,\n    B0=what_0, H=M2-M^2/B,\n    f=B0*M/B, k0=B0(1-B0/B).\n\nHere H>=1/2, B<5/2, f>2/5, H<=B/4<5/8, and\n\n    Y=(Xreq/Delta)f/H>1/2,\n    K_00/Y=dclock*(B-B0)/M.\n\nSince B-B0<3/2 and M>=1, (2.1)-(2.2) give\n aK_00/Y<(5/6)p, while theta/Xreq>p.\nWriting S0=aK_00-theta Y/Xreq+Bsource, the remaining term is\n\n    Bsource=2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0+a(v_0^2+eta z_0^2)<7p^2.\n\nFor this use R_0<2p, v_0<u_0<p^2/64, and0<z_0<2p.\nTherefore\n\n    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16.                           (6.3)\n\n### 6.1 First-row clock, with finite errors and the load retained\n\nWrite K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and Y=Xreq/Delta+eY. Expansion of (6.1)\nusing R_0=bE-a v_0, z_0=ET/Delta and Z=0 yields\n\n    G0=Gendpoint+Epert,\n    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq,\n    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[btheta0-4-(p^2-s^2)T^2]\n       +2bs(2-ET)\n       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-4a]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0.           (6.4)\n\nThe incoming load cancels exactly in Gendpoint: before cancellation its\nfirst bracket is Xreq-Ttheta-(p^2-s^2)T^2 and there is +a nu_0;\nXreq-Ttheta=btheta0-4-Delta*nu_0. This uses the ACTUAL load ellload,\nnot a zero-load substitution.\n\nAs theta0=2-6p and pT=1-bp,\n\n    btheta0-4-(p^2-s^2)T^2\n                  =2b-5-4bp-b^2p^2+s^2T^2.\n\nThe bounds (2.1)-(3.3) now give\n\n    |Gendpoint-a*dclock*(2a-3)|<20p^2.              (6.5)\n\nHere the term a*dclock*(4bp+b^2p^2) is <7p^2; the s^2 term is\n<s^2/p<p^2; 2bs|2-ET|<6s<p^2; the v_0 bracket has absolute value\n<4 and contributes <p^2; aDelta v_0^2<p^2 and2a alpha_0<p^2.\nThe displayed20p^2 safely bounds their sum.\n\nFor an AUXILIARY calculation omit omega*rho only, retaining unit masses\nat0 and1 and mass sigma at1-rclock. Put\n\n    Dclock=1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2.\n\nDirect use of the base moments f,k0,H in (6.4) gives the exact interior\nmetric correction\n\n    Eint=(xiM*rclock/Dclock)\n      [theta(1-a*dclock)(1-2rclock)\n                               -2a Xreq*rclock*dclock].        (6.6)\n\nThis is an auxiliary formula; the positive tail weights are restored\nbelow. One has 1-2p<Dclock<=1 and\n |1/Dclock-1|<rclock. The bracket differs from theta in absolute value\nby less than35p, using a*dclock<p, rclock<4p, theta<3 andXreq*dclock<2.\nThus\n\n    |Eint-xiM*hgap*dclock*(2+ellload)|<106p^2.       (6.7)\n\nIndeed comparison first with xiM*rclock*theta costs <100p^2, and\nreplacing theta by2+ellload costs <6p^2.\n\nAdding omega*rho changes B,M,M2,B0 by at most omega. Both old and\nnew H are >=1/2, and the old f,k0 are <=1. Direct quotient subtraction\nbounds the changes of f,k0,H by2omega,4omega,5omega respectively;\nhence each ratio f/H,k0/H changes by at most32omega. The exact metric\npart of G0 is\n\n    a(Xreq/Delta)(2f/H-k0/H)\n                       +theta(1-a/Delta)f/H-btheta.\n\nUsing a Xreq/Delta<5/2 and theta<3 bounds its change by400omega.\nEach base is rescaled by its OWN variance and the SAME Xreq,theta;\nthere is no neglected change of scale. Combining (6.5)-(6.7),\n\n    |G0-dclock*Cfirst|<526p^2,\n    Cfirst=a(2a-3)+xiM*hgap*(2+ellload).\n\nSubstitution of xiM gives\n\n    Cfirst=a(1-2a)-a^3/2+2ellload*xiM\n                                      -eps*(2+ellload)*xiM\n            >a^3/2-3eps/4>1/32.                   (6.8)\n\nThe last inequality follows from a>2/5, eps<=1/6144:\n4/125-3/24576>1/32. Also |Cfirst|<3. Since p<2^(-40),\n\n    p/64<G0<7p,\n    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<7p-1/32<0.               (6.9)\n\nEquations (6.3),(6.9) prove the entire screen (6.2).\n\n### 6.2 Last-row clock, including the nonzero incoming-load cost\n\nThe last row of Ltri^T is e_l^T, so\n\n    N_ll=P_ll,\n    (Nt)_l=2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l.\n\nFor the last row define\n\n    Slast=sum_(j<l)N_lj=mu_l-P_ll,\n    Jlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-1)N_lj=(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*P_ll,\n    Hlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-t_r)N_lj\n                  =(Nt)_l-t_r*mu_l-hgap*P_ll.\n\nNonpositive off-diagonals require Slast<=0, Jlast<=0 andHlast>=0.\nDefine actual base ratios\n\n    Alast=what_l(1-M/B)/H,\n    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/H.\n\nThen (Kt)_l=(Xreq/Delta)Alast, K_ll=(Xreq/Delta^2)Blast,\nzeta_l=(theta/Delta)Alast. Both ratios are positive and <3.\nFor the auxiliary base without omega*rho,\n\n    Alast=(1-xiM+xiM*rclock)/Dclock>=1-xiM.\n\nRestoring omega*rho changes this ratio by at most32omega: its numerator\nchanges by at most3omega, H by5omega, H>=1/2 and the old numerator<=1.\nThus the actual Alast>=1-xiM-32p^2.\n\nUsing V=2, alpha_l=b-v_l, nu_l=0 and z_l=-E/Delta gives the EXACT\nlast-row invariant\n\n    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast\n       +4v_l+2p(b+hgap)v_l-hgap*v_l^2-2b-hgap*eta*z_l^2,\n    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta]/Delta.              (6.10)\n\nSince T=Delta+1,\n Cclock=2Xreq/Delta-theta+theta*(hgap-1)/Delta.\nBy (5.2),\n\n    2-ellload-20p<Cclock<3,\n    Cclock*Alast>(2-ellload)(1-xiM)-40p.\n\nThe subtracted metric term has magnitude <24p, using Blast<3,\nhgap<2, Xreq/Delta<2 and1/Delta<2p. Also\neta*z_l^2=(p^2-s^2)/Delta^2<4p^4.\nThe function4v-hgap*v^2+2p(b+hgap)v is increasing for a<=v<=v_l<1/2,\nsince its derivative is >2. Consequently (6.10) implies\n\n    Hlast>2a-2a^2-2xiM-ellload(1-xiM)-65p\n          =a^3/4-ellload(1-xiM)-65p\n          >3/8000-65p>1/4000.                      (6.11)\n\nHere the positive eps*a^2 term was dropped. The difference\n(2/5)^3/4-1/64 is3/8000. The load penalty ellload(1-xiM) is therefore\nexplicitly paid; it is not hidden in an asymptotic error.\n\nFinally mu_l=2p v_l+zeta_l<19p and P_ll>=v_l^2>a^2>4/25.\nThus Slast<19p-4/25<-1/8. Since (Nt)_l=2(Kt)_l+4v_l-2alpha_l<14,\n\n    Jlast<14-(3/(4p))*(4/25)<0.\n\nEvery last-row total/first-moment condition passes. Neither endpoint\nscreen, even together with all optimum signs and full PSD, guarantees\nnonnegative entries in the full array.\n\n## 7. Decisive column failure of the prescribed law\n\nThe incoming optimum equation in (4.3) is a COLUMN equation:\n\n    sum_i N_il=nu_l+2alpha_l=2alpha_l.\n\nIf each off-diagonal N_il were nonpositive, it would imply\nN_ll>=2alpha_l. But N_ll=P_ll for every skew A, not just (5.4).\nThe metric scale is small:\n\n    scale<=2Xreq/Delta^2<8p,\n    K_ll<=w_l=scale*(1+omega*rho_l)<12p.\n\nTogether with (3.3) and eta*z_l^2<4p^4, this gives\n\n    P_ll=K_ll+v_l^2+eta*z_l^2<12p+1/4+4p^4<1/3,\n    P_ll-2alpha_l<1/3-19/10=-47/30<-3/2.            (7.1)\n\nThus the putative nonnegative incoming transport masses have negative\ntotal:\n\n    sum_(0<=i<n, i!=l)lambda_(i,l)\n      =sum_(0<=i<n, i!=l)(-N_il)=P_ll-2alpha_l<-3/2.\n\nAt least one original finite edge INTO the last queried node has a\nnegative multiplier. This conclusion is invariant under all Ce=Ct=0\ncorrections and, more generally, under any skew with the same incoming\ncolumn sum. The complete prescribed law fails for every m>=1536.\nThis is not a primal counterexample, an infeasibility proof for all\npossible coefficient architectures, or a conclusion about W itself.\n\n## 8. Second mechanism: transport with diagonal exclusion and the full factor\n\nThis section treats an entire family, rather than adding another metric\natom. First give the exact finite transport interface for any fixed\nP,mu,nu,alpha in the architecture above. Write\n\n    D_i=N_ii, a_ij=-N_ij for i!=j, a_ii=0,\n    r_i=D_i-mu_i,\n    c_i=D_i-nu_i-2alpha_i,\n    b_i^clock=t_i D_i-(Nt)_i.                       (8.1)\n\nHere a_ij denotes transport mass and is unrelated to the physical scalar\na. If the original finite multipliers are nonnegative, then a_ij>=0 and\n\n    sum_j a_ij=r_i, sum_i a_ij=c_j,\n    sum_j t_j a_ij=b_i^clock.                       (8.2)\n\nThus every r_i,c_j is nonnegative, sum r=sum c, and\nsum_i b_i^clock=sum_j t_j c_j. A zero r_i forces b_i^clock=0.\nFor positive r_i the mean b_i^clock/r_i lies in the interval spanned\nby {t_j:j!=i}; retaining the exclusion is essential at either endpoint.\nWith Kmass=sum r=sum c, the forbidden diagonal further implies\n\n    r_i+c_i<=Kmass.                                 (8.3)\n\nIndeed all mass from row i must land outside column i, whose total\ncapacity is Kmass-c_i. This is a true cut inequality.\n\nThere are elementary clock cuts as well. For any real tau,\n\n    sum_i max(0,b_i^clock-tau*r_i)\n                              <=sum_j c_j max(0,t_j-tau).       (8.4)\n\nFor each row, max(0,sum_j a_ij(t_j-tau))<=sum_j a_ij max(0,t_j-tau);\nsumming proves (8.4). More generally for any convex function phi on[1,T],\nfinite convexity gives\n\n    sum_(i:r_i>0) r_i phi(b_i^clock/r_i)\n                                      <=sum_j c_j phi(t_j).    (8.5)\n\nThis is merely the finite convex-combination inequality, provable by\ninduction on the number of nonzero row entries, and imports no transport\nexistence theorem. It is necessary, not sufficient for the original law.\nThe following more general elementary cut keeps the missing diagonal\nvisible: if real Ucut_i,Vcut_i,Zcut_j obey\nUcut_i+Vcut_i*t_j+Zcut_j>=0 for EVERY i!=j, then\n\n    sum_i Ucut_i*r_i+sum_i Vcut_i*b_i^clock+sum_j Zcut_j*c_j>=0. (8.6)\n\nMultiply each allowed coefficient by a_ij>=0 and sum. No condition on\nUcut_i+Vcut_i*t_i+Zcut_i is imposed, because that edge is forbidden.\n\nCrucially, the chronological symmetric-factor constraint remains\n\n    Ltri^(-T)N+N^T Ltri^(-1)=2P.                    (8.7)\n\nA transport coupling satisfying row/column masses and clock conditions\nalone may violate (8.7). This equality cannot be replaced by PSD of P.\nFor precision, given D and an array a with zero diagonal, nonnegative\noff-diagonals, and the row and column sums in (8.2), define\nN=diag(D)-a. If (8.7) holds, then A=Ltri^(-T)N-P is skew.\nFor the fixed architecture mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta, one has\nAe=Ltri^(-T)mu-Pe=d. Moreover\n(P-A)t=N^T e=nu+2alpha, so At=Pt-2alpha-nu.\nThus this system recovers exactly the required skew and all original\ncoefficients; the clock equations follow with\n\n    Nt=Ltri^T(2Pt-2alpha-nu).\n\nThis is an equivalence of constraint descriptions, NOT an asserted\nfeasible unknown matrix or a new certificate law. We use it only to\nprove the explicit family exclusion below.\n\n### 8.1 Last-column cut and a metric concentration inequality\n\nThe last row of Ltri^(-T) is e_l^T. The (l,l) entry of (8.7) therefore\npins D_l=P_ll. The nonnegative column mass c_l gives\n\n    K_ll+eta*z_l^2 >= 2alpha_l+nu_l-(b-alpha_l)^2.   (8.8)\n\nThis combines the transport column cut with an exact original-space\nfactor constraint. Allowing D_l to float freely would lose it.\n\nFor ANY strictly positive metric weights w, set X=t^T Kt and\n\n    Qgap=sum_(i<l) w_i (T-t_i)^2 / sum_(i<l)w_i.\n\nThe weighted variance identity is\n\n    X=(1/Ww)sum_(i<j)w_i w_j(t_i-t_j)^2.\n\nRetaining only pairs involving l and using\nK_ll=w_l(Ww-w_l)/Ww gives the exact necessary estimate\n\n    X>=K_ll*Qgap, so K_ll<=X/Qgap.                  (8.9)\n\nEvery denominator is positive since w_i>0 and the clocks are distinct.\nFor d0>0 define the NONLAST far-clock fraction\n\n    Ffar(d0)=sum_(i<l:T-t_i>=d0)w_i / sum_(i<l)w_i.\n\nThen Qgap>=d0^2*Ffar(d0). If\n Bneed:=2alpha_l+nu_l-(b-alpha_l)^2-eta*z_l^2>0,\n(8.8)-(8.9) imply the quantitative concentration requirement\n\n    Ffar(d0)<=X/[d0^2*Bneed].                       (8.10)\n\nThis is a global statement about ALL nonlast metric weights; it does\nnot assume any sparse support or any special skew completion.\n\n### 8.2 A full family excluded, including arbitrary sparse metric atoms\n\nFix any same-enclosing-m word as in Section2. Consider ALL choices with\n\n (i) alpha_i>=0, sum alpha_i=1-p, alpha_l>=1/2;\n (ii) v=k-alpha, z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, eta=F/E;\n (iii) arbitrary positive w with X=t^T Kt<=3/p;\n (iv) Ffar(Delta/2)>=256p;\n (v) any skew A and any nonnegative incoming optimum vector nu\n     satisfying the original equations (4.2)-(4.3).\n\nThere is no restriction to the literal skew (5.4), to nu=ellload e_0,\nor to the paid minimum-energy zeta. If the divergence and PSD happen\nto pass, they still cannot repair the following finite signs.\n\nFor alpha_l in[1/2,1-p], the function\n2alpha_l-(b-alpha_l)^2 is strictly increasing, because its derivative\nis2+2b-2alpha_l>0. Using b<17/12 gives\n\n    2alpha_l-(b-alpha_l)^2\n                >=1-(b-1/2)^2>23/144.              (8.11)\n\nAlso eta*z_l^2=(p^2-s^2)/Delta^2<4p^4<1/144.\nOn the other hand, (8.9) and condition(iv) yield\n\n    Qgap>=64p*Delta^2,\n    K_ll <=(3/p)/(64p*Delta^2)<1/12,                (8.12)\n\nwhere Delta>3/(4p) was used. Therefore\n\n    c_l=P_ll-nu_l-2alpha_l\n       <1/12+1/144-23/144=-5/72<0.                 (8.13)\n\nThis contradicts a nonnegative column mass. Every member of the entire\nprecisely specified family has some negative original multiplier into\nl. No existence theorem, unknown optimizer, asymptotic coefficient law,\nor numerical evaluation enters this exclusion.\n\nAs a concrete sparse-metric subfamily, take ANY finite subset\nJsubset of {1,...,l-1}, arbitrary coefficients Mlast>0 and b_j>=0,\nand omega0>0, with\n\n    what=e_0+Mlast*e_l+sum_(j in Jsubset)b_j e_j+omega0*rho,\n    1+sum_j b_j+omega0 <= 1/(256p).\n\nScale what by ANY positive scalar for which t^T Kt<=3/p. Universal U and arbitrary-start low-d H2 remain OPEN.", "evidence": "# Actual projection normals and complete-child gluing\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation78 after actual ended baseline77.\nProject gd-exact-profile-20260926.
