Original target OPEN.\nThis is one joint mathematical candidate, not a collection of accepted facts.\n\n## Verdict and exact scope\n\nThe requested universal U_d block inequality is NOT proved or refuted.\nIn particular the low-d, arbitrary-start H2 stress test is NOT closed.\nThere is no full triangle upper bound and no full-function W upper bound.\n\nThis round develops ONE mechanism, retaining projection-normal reserves.\nIt derives an exact identity with all physical and scaled auxiliary points\nin one body, and displays the precise H2 central debt. It pays that debt\nuniformly for all bodies and starts when d>=2/epsilon-2(A+B), and pays\nall depths and every d>=1 on the entire initial unit ball of the centered\ntriangle, including starts outside its quadratic core. It also pays the\nprevious actual strict-switch H3 family using a new terminal-norm bound.\n\nA tempting strengthened child law, the terminal shifted-norm bound, would\nsuffice for the desired U law. It has an explicit two-child gluing identity.\nBut this stronger law is FALSE for general compact bodies on the ACTUAL\noriginal H3, and indeed at every parent depth at least3: one static\nasymmetric interval and one legal complete two-cap trajectory refute it.\nThe same entire interval family satisfies the U law with ratio below3/4.\nThus this counterexample rejects the strengthening, not U itself.\nIt must not be advertised as a counterexample for the centered triangle.\n\nNo second directional storage is introduced. Failure of the stronger norm\nlaw has not supplied a decisive failure of the requested U law itself.\nThe remaining task is an actual low-d H2 debt estimate, followed by a\nuniform same-m all-depth estimate retaining the nonlocal normal coupling.\nA signed telescope or a sufficient-condition reduction is not that estimate.\n\n## 1. Definitions, normalization, and accepted scope\n\nFix ONE integer m>=1536. Set\n\n epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a*c^j,\n S_j=sum H_j, n_j=2^j-1, D_m=1+2S_m.\n\nAll children inherit this same m and these same parameters. For the literal\nH2 write A=1+a, B=1+beta, so H2=(A,B,A), S2=2A+B. The symbol A in this\nH2 abbreviation is a physical step; below Cchild_j=1+S_j denotes a child mass.\nIt is NOT the original benchmark primitive capacity sequence.\n\nFor a nonempty compact convex subset D of a finite-dimensional Euclidean\nspace containing0, define\n\n P_D(x)=the Euclidean projection of x onto D,\n F_D(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),\n U_d(x)=max_(g in D)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2), d>0,\n T_h(x)=x-h P_D(x).\n\nFor a finite nonnegative word h, T_hword is its actual chronological\ncomposition. All uses of U with different d use this SAME body D.\nThe centered-triangle specialization is\n\n v1=(1,0), v2=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), v3=(-1/2,-sqrt(3)/2),\n D_triangle=conv{v1,v2,v3}.\n\nThe original W_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex\nL-smooth functions with a minimizer, under x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i)/L.\nThe original normalization is W=2R. All physical leaves and the actual\nterminal are retained here. Even a universal upper bound within the centered\ntriangle family would not by itself be an upper bound for that full W.\n\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50 and the CONDITIONAL\n799/800 reduction if the full-function bound17/(16D_m) is established.\nIt does not supply that bound. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 fixes the ordered\ninterpolation residual and the actual unqueried-terminal convention.\nOnly those stated accepted scopes are used; no unaccepted general-body\nrepresentation theorem or previous unverified proof is a premise below.\nAll new assertions are derived together in this document.\n\n## 2. Projection facts, legal objectives, and true minimizers\n\nFor g=P_D(x), squared-distance minimization along each segment in D gives\n\n <x-g,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                         (2.1)\n\nConversely, expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2 proves sufficiency of (2.1).\nAdding the inequalities at x and y gives\n||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2<=<P_D(x)-P_D(y),x-y>, proving1-Lipschitzness.\nCompleting the square identifies g as the unique maximizer in F_D.\nThe two maximizing inequalities bound\nF_D(x+t)-F_D(x)-<g,t> between0 and ||t||^2. Thus F_D is differentiable,\nconvex, finite, and1-smooth, with gradient P_D.\n\nIts complete minimum set is the polar cone\n\n K_D={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in D},                     (2.2)\n\nand its minimum is0. Membership in K_D makes every candidate in F_D\nnonpositive, and g=0 attains0. If <z,g>>0 for some g in D, then t*g in D\nfor0<t<=1, and sufficiently small positive t gives positive objective.\nThis proves the converse. The closed convex set K_D contains0, so a closest\ninitial minimizer exists. We do not replace that distance by the origin norm.\n\nFor every d>0,\n\n U_d(x)=||x||^2/d-dist(x,dD)^2/d,\n U_d(x)<=||x-z_star||^2/d for EVERY z_star in K_D.      (2.3)\n\nThe first identity is completion of the square. For the second,\n<z_star,g><=0 implies\n2<x,g>-d||g||^2<=2<x-z_star,g>-d||g||^2\n=||x-z_star||^2/d-d||g-(x-z_star)/d||^2.\nTake the maximum. Consequently a proved U block law at j=m,d=1 would\nhave the true closest-distance consequence in this projection class.\nThis does not require a norm bound about the origin to be the true radius.\n\nFor actual queries and terminal put gi=P_D(xi), fi=F_D(xi). Then every\nordered original interpolation residual is exactly\n\n fi-fj+<gi,xj-xi>+||gi-gj||^2/2\n   =<xj-gj,gi-gj><=0.                                (2.4)\n\nThe same formula holds when either sample is any minimizer, with gradient0\nand value0. Actual Euclidean vectors supply a PSD Gram. Scaling the objective\nby L and using steps h/L preserves every point and the W normalization.\n\n## 3. Exact one-step identity with BOTH normal reserves retained\n\nConsider one actual step xplus=x-h*g, g=P_D(x), with h>0 and d>=1.\nPut\n\n Dmass=d+2h, p=P_D(x/Dmass), q=P_D(xplus/d),\n theta=h/(Dmass-1), z=theta*g+(1-theta)*q,\n e=q-g,\n Nphys=<x-g,g-q>,\n Naux=<x-Dmass*p,p-z>.\n\nBoth normal quantities are nonnegative by (2.1). The mixture z is in D;\nindeed0<theta<=1/2. Define\n\n Bcoef(d,h)=h*((2-h)*(d+2h)-2)/(d+2h-1)^2.\n\nThere is the EXACT identity\n\n U_(d+2h)(x)-U_d(xplus)\n   = Bcoef(d,h)||q-g||^2 +2theta*Nphys\n     +Dmass||p-z||^2+2Naux.                          (3.1)\n\nTo prove it, evaluate 2<x,z>-Dmass||z||^2 and subtract the final maximum\n2<x-hg,q>-d||q||^2. With x=g+(x-g), the coefficient of <g,e> cancels\nbecause theta*(Dmass-1)=h. The remainder is\n-2theta<x-g,e>+[d-Dmass*(1-theta)^2]||e||^2.\nThe square coefficient is Bcoef(d,h). Finally the difference between the\ninitial maximum at p and its value at z is\nDmass||p-z||^2+2<x-Dmass*p,p-z>. This proves (3.1), including boundaries.\n\nFor0<h<=3/2, Bcoef>=0 because d+2h>=1+2h and\n(2-h)(1+2h)-2=h(3-2h)>=0. More generally h<2 is paid when\n(d+2h)(2-h)>=2. At h>2 the coefficient is negative for every d>=1.\nThe physical normal and the auxiliary normal are distinct. The auxiliary\nsquare is also essential data; it is not another projection-normal inequality.\n\nFor a complete word of N steps let\n\n di=d+2 sum_(t=i)^(N-1)h_t,\n xi+1=xi-hi*gi, gi=P_D(xi), pi=P_D(xi/di),\n thetai=hi/(di-1), zi=thetai*gi+(1-thetai)*p_(i+1).\n\nThen the exact whole-word gap is the sum, over EVERY physical i, of\n\n hi*((2-hi)*di-2)/(di-1)^2 *||p_(i+1)-gi||^2\n +2thetai<xi-gi,gi-p_(i+1)>\n +di||pi-zi||^2+2<xi-di*pi,pi-zi>.                   (3.2)\n\nAll pi, gi and zi belong to the same D. The normals at arbitrary other\nphysical or auxiliary points remain available through (2.1).\nEquation (3.2) is an exact interface, not proof that the signed sum is\nnonnegative at every depth.\n\n## 4. The literal H2 stress test and its outstanding debt\n\nTake x1=x0-A*g0, x2=x1-B*g1, x3=x2-A*g2. Define\n\n d0=d+4A+2B, d1=d+2A+2B, d2=d+2A, d3=d,\n gi=P_D(xi) for i=0,1,2; pi=P_D(xi/di) for i=0,1,2,3.\n\nThese are the THREE actual queried projections and FOUR scaled auxiliary\nprojections. No one is substituted by an independent maximizer from another\nrun or another body. The final actual projection P_D(x3) is also legal and\nwill be used in Section5, but p3 need not equal it when d>1.\n\nBoth leaf coefficients in (3.2) are nonnegative, since A<sqrt(2)<3/2.\nThe middle coefficient is\n\n Cmid=B*(epsilon*d1-2)/(d1-1)^2.                       (4.1)\n\nIt follows that, for every compact convex D containing0 and EVERY x0,\n\n d>=max(1,2/epsilon-2(A+B))\n   => U_d(T_H2 x0)<=U_(d+2S2)(x0).                   (4.2)\n\nThis is a fully paid complete-block statement, with an explicit domain in d.\nIt does NOT handle the small trailing masses most relevant to terminal blocks.\n\nWhen epsilon*d1<2 the exact unpaid central debt is\n\n Debt=B*(2-epsilon*d1)/(d1-1)^2 *||p2-g1||^2.         (4.3)\n\nThe required payment is the sum of the two positive leaf squares and ALL\nthree physical normals, three auxiliary mixture squares, and three auxiliary\nnormals in (3.2). Their actual nonlocal correlations may improve that payment.\nNo lower bound on this sum sufficient for EVERY x0 and every remaining\n1<=d<2/epsilon-2(A+B) is proved here. In particular, the presence of these\nnonnegative terms does not permit assuming they exceed (4.3).\n\nThe single long-step core obstruction is also exact: sufficiently small\nnonzero x with x and (1-h)x in D_triangle has\nU_d(T_h x)=(h-1)^2||x||^2/d>||x||^2/(d+2h) for h>2.\nSection8 pays all such small inputs over each COMPLETE actual block.\n\n## 5. A sufficient terminal shifted-norm certificate\n\nFor an arbitrary nonnegative complete word with mass S, input x, endpoint y,\nand actual gradients gi, write y=x-sum_i hi*gi. For d>=1 put\nq=P_D(y/d) and\n\n zbar=[sum_i hi*gi+(d+S)q]/(d+2S) in D.              (5.1)\n\nAll coefficients are nonnegative and sum to1. Evaluating the initial U at\nthis one feasible point and completing the square yields\n\n U_(d+2S)(x)-U_d(y)\n   >= [||x||^2-||y+S*q||^2]/(d+2S).                 (5.2)\n\nFor verification of the algebra, x-(d+2S)zbar=y-(d+S)q; expand both squares\nand subtract2<y,q>-d||q||^2. The numerator is exactly\n||x||^2-||y||^2-2S<y,q>-S^2||q||^2.\nThe omitted difference is the nonnegative initial maximization remainder.\n\nFor a fixed y the two quantities ||P_D(y/d)||^2 and <y,P_D(y/d)> are\nnonincreasing in d>0. Indeed if d2>d1 and qi=P_D(y/di), maximizing at the\nopposite qi gives\n\nd1*(||q1||^2-||q2||^2)\n <=2<y,q1-q2><=d2*(||q1||^2-||q2||^2).\n\nThe difference of squared norms must be nonnegative; the scalar product\ndifference then is nonnegative as well. Hence, for S>=0,\n||y+S P_D(y/d)||^2 is nonincreasing in d.\n\nIn particular the stronger complete-block statement\n\n R_H(x):=||x||^2-||y+S P_D(y)||^2>=0                 (5.3)\n\nwould prove the desired U law for every d>=1, with retained reserve\nR_H(x)/(d+2S). This is a sufficient strengthening, not an equivalence.\nFor F_D, its right-hand square is\n||y||^2+2S F_D(y)+S(S+1)||P_D(y)||^2.\nThis explains its relationship to a primitive/s-composability potential.\nSection7 gives a legal actual counterexample to (5.3) for general bodies.\n\nEven the one-step thresholds show that (5.3) is stronger. For h>0, set\nq=P_D(x-hg), e=g-q, N0=<x-g,g-q>, N1=<x-hg-q,q-g>.\nBoth normals are nonnegative and direct expansion gives\n\n ||x||^2-||x-hg+hq||^2\n   =(2-h^2)||e||^2+2(1+h)N0+2N1.                   (5.4)\n\nFor the derivation, N0+N1=h<g,e>-||e||^2 and\n||x||^2-||x-he||^2=2h<x,e>-h^2||e||^2.\nThus (5.3) holds for h<=sqrt(2). But for a core point and h=3/2 its shifted\nendpoint is (1-h^2)x, of larger norm than x, whereas (3.1) still proves the\nU law. This comparison is a logical warning, not an altered physical step.\n\n## 6. Exact gluing through TWO actual children and their bridge\n\nLet each real child be the same H_j with mass S=S_j, and b=b_j. For one\nactual parent run define\n\n x --H_j--> y --b--> z=y-b*p --H_j--> t,\n p=P_D(y), q=P_D(t), w=p-q,\n e=z-(t+S*q)=sum_(right child i) hi*(gi-q),\n Rleft=||x||^2-||y+S*p||^2,\n Rright=||z||^2-||t+S*q||^2,\n Rparent=||x||^2-||t+(2S+b)q||^2.\n\nKeep the actual nonlocal normals\n\n Ny=<y-p,p-q>, Nt=<t-q,q-p>,\n NR=-<t-q,e>=sum_(right child i)hi*<t-q,q-gi>.\n\nAll three are nonnegative; in particular NR uses the endpoint normal\nagainst EVERY real right-child gradient. They are not independent reserves\nfrom arbitrarily chosen runs.\n\nThe exact parent identity is\n\n Rparent = Rleft +(2S+b+1)/(S+1)*Rright\n   -(S+b)/(S+1)*||e||^2 -2(S+1)<e,w>\n   +[2(S+1)^2-(S+b)^2]||w||^2\n   +2(2S+b+1)Ny +2(S+1)Nt +2(S+b)/(S+1)*NR.        (6.1)\n\nProof by displayed substitutions. First expand the three squared norms:\n\n Rparent=Rleft+Rright+(S+b)*[\n   2<y,p>-2<t,q>+(S-b)||p||^2-(3S+b)||q||^2].\n\nSince t=y-bp-Sq-e, the bracket is\n2<q,e>+2<y+Sp,w>-(S+b)||w||^2. Next,\n\n Rright=||e||^2+2(S+1)<q,e>-2NR,\n Ny+Nt=(S+b)<p,w>-(S+1)||w||^2+<e,w>.\n\nSubstituting these two equalities gives (6.1) term by term.\n\nThe formula contains a negative ||e||^2 coefficient and a mixed term.\nEven replacing both child residues and all three normals by unspecified\nnonnegative numbers does not yield the parent sign. The w-square\ncoefficient itself changes sign at\n\n b-1=(sqrt(2)-1)(S+1).                               (6.2)\n\nThis is a concrete failure of an attempted composition from scalar child\nsigns. It is not a proof that the actual U debt in Section4 is unpaid.\nThe next section makes the stronger-law failure actual, rather than merely\nassigning values to an unconstrained signed quadratic.\n\n## 7. Actual all-depth counterexample to the GENERAL-BODY strengthening\n\nLet r_s=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda. Put Cchild_j=1+S_j and k_j=b_j-1=a*c^j.\nFor j>=2 define the ONE static interval\n\n D_j=[-r_j,1], r_j=k_j/Cchild_j,\n x0=Cchild_j, parent word H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+k_j,H_j).        (7.1)\n\nThis is a compact convex body containing0. It may depend on m and j,\nbut is fixed for the entire actual run. Its F_D has unique minimizer0,\nsince the interval contains a neighborhood of0. The true initial radius\nis exactly Cchild_j.\n\nEvery left-child query is at least1: after physical prefix mass s<=S_j\nit is Cchild_j-s>=1. Thus all its actual gradients equal1 and its endpoint y=1.\nThe central step sends y to z=-k_j. During the right child, at physical\nprefix mass s<=S_j the point is -k_j+s*r_j<=-r_j. Its actual gradient\ntherefore equals-r_j at EVERY right query. At the actual terminal\n\n t=-k_j+S_j*r_j=-r_j.\n\nBoundary projections at y and t are unique; no query is omitted. In the\nnotation of Section6, Rleft=Rright=0, e=0, Ny=Nt=NR=0. The parent shifted\nendpoint has magnitude (2S_j+b_j+1)r_j. Therefore (5.3) fails precisely when\n\n k_j*(2Cchild_j+k_j)>Cchild_j^2,\n equivalently k_j/Cchild_j>r_s.                           (7.2)\n\nThis condition holds for EVERY j>=2 with j+1<=m and EVERY m>=1536.\nTo prove it first use the exact mass solution\n\n Cchild_j=(1-v)2^j+v*c^j, v=a/(c-2), 0<v<1.             (7.3)\n\nThe recurrence Cchild_(j+1)=2Cchild_j+a*c^j and v(c-2)=a prove the formula by\nsubstitution, with Cchild0=1. The inequality0<v<1 follows from\nc(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta.\nThus k_j/Cchild_j=a/[v+(1-v)(2/c)^j] is increasing in j.\nAt j=2, Cchild2=5-epsilon+2r_s*(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and\nk2=beta*c=lambda*(1-epsilon^2). Since lambda=5r_s+2r_s^2,\n\n k2-r_s*Cchild2\n   =epsilon*[r_s+4r_s^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda*epsilon]>0. (7.4)\n\nFor the final sign, epsilon<1/8, r_s>2/5 and lambda<5/2 imply\nlambda*epsilon<5/16<2/5<r_s. If L_j=S_j/d_j>7/5, then\nL_(j+1)=(2L_j+1+1/d_j)/c>(19/5)/(17/7)>7/5.\nNo recurrence was executed to obtain these assertions.\n\nAt any physical occurrence of a level-k bridge in H_j, let P,T be\nthe actual masses before and after that occurrence within H_j.\nIts two full adjacent H_k children imply\n\n    P>=S_k, T>=S_k, P+T=S_j-h_k^lev.                (3.4)\n\nIts actual remaining suffix is\n\n    H_k,h_(l_1)^lev,H_(l_1),...,h_(l_t)^lev,H_(l_t),\n    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                            (3.5)\n\nTo obtain this list, ascend the binary tree after the immediate right H_k,\nskip ancestors entered through a right child, and retain each remaining\nbridge and full right child. This includes the real last leaf; after a\nfinal leaf the list is empty.\n\n## 4. Exact saturated itinerary and the changed tail intercept\n\nChoose\n\n    R_star=2B*S+C_tail/B,\n    r_star=R_star-B*S=B*S+C_tail/B.                 (4.1)\n\nThe complete chronological trajectory is explicitly\n\n    x_i=R_star-B*P_i,\n    P_i=sum_(0<=t<i) h_t, 0<=i<=n.                 (4.2)\n\nIndeed\n\n    r_star-u=B*S+v*(1-kappa/B)>=0.\n\nThus every x_i>=r_star>=u, every gradient equals B, every physical\nupdate in (4.2) is valid, and x_n=r_star after the actual final leaf.\nAll data inherit every ordered row from Section2.\n\nFor any positive saturated start whose terminal is still saturated,\nwrite R>=B*S+u. Its payoff is\n\n    [2B*R-2B^2*S-C_tail]/R^2.\n\nDifferentiation in R, or completing a square in 1/R, gives its unique\nmaximum at (4.1), with value (1.1). Reflection gives the negative ray.\nBecause C_tail>=B^2,\n\n    V_tail<=1/(1+2S).\n\nIf kappa<1, v>0 and 2B-kappa>=kappa>0, so the inequality is strict.\nThis is not a full profile formula: a terminal in the middle/core or\na crossing history has not been optimized by this calculation.\nIn particular, substituting the ordinary Huber intercept B^2 into the\ncurrent tail would give an incorrect score.\n\n## 5. Exact interval update and the outer crossing term\n\nFor a physical step h>=1, put d=h-1 and q_h=|1-h*kappa|.\nThe exact radius of the image of [-R,R] is\n\n    E_h(R)=max{\n       q_h*min(R,1),\n       [d*min(R,u)-h*v]_+,\n       [R-h*B]_+\n    }.                                             (5.1)\n\nHere [z]_+=max(z,0). In the core the map is (1-h*kappa)x.\nIn the middle its positive-ray expression is -d*r+h*v.\nIn the tail it is r-h*B. The extrema occur at0, a knot, or the input\nendpoint; including a formal term from an unavailable piece does not\nincrease the maximum, since that term is then dominated by an available\nendpoint. These observations establish (5.1) also at R=1,u.\n\nThe signed step is continuous and odd and fixes0, so its interval image\nis the entire symmetric interval with this radius. Therefore successive\ncomposition of E_h is exact, rather than a choice of incompatible starts.\nIn particular M_(j+1)=M_j composed with E_(h_j^lev) composed with M_j.\n\nWhen both the source and the destination of a strict crossing are in the\nmiddle, put z=G(r)=r-v. The destination gradient is\n\n    z_new=(h-1)*z-2v.                               (5.2)\n\nThis displays the lost fixed-curvature assumption. The term -2v is\nretained; a child cannot be treated as a homogeneous quadratic in z.\n\nEquation (5.1) alone is NOT the claimed result. Sections6-10 give the\nquantitative deductions obtained from it, and Section11 states what is\nstill missing.\n\n## 6. Complete unit-Huber reference, rederived jointly\n\nLet J_B have derivative sign(x)*min(|x|,B), and write U_j for its interval\nradius map under the same H_j. Scaling x by B reduces this subsection to\nB=1. Its exact single-step map is\n\n    U_h(R)=max(d*min(R,1),R-h).\n\nThe maximum is nonnegative because its first term is. Direct induction\ngives exact capture and the exterior branch:\n\n    U_j(A_j)=1,\n    U_j(R)=R-S_j for R>=A_j,\n    U_j(R)<=max(1,R-S_j) for all R>=0.              (6.1)\n\nFor the induction, start at A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j.\nThe first child gives A_j+d_j+1. The bridge's staying endpoint is A_j,\nand its crossing endpoint is at most d_j<A_j. The second child gives1.\nAn exterior start stays on the unit tail at every actual query.\n\nFor j>=1 and every integer r>=0,\n\n    U_j(d_j*theta^r)<=theta^(r+1).                  (6.2)\n\nThe leaf base is a*beta*theta^r=theta^(r+1).\nAt the unscaled parent input c*d_j, (6.1) bounds the first child by\nmax(1,c*d_j-S_j). The bridge sends this to at most d_j, since\n\n    c*d_j-S_j-h_j^lev<=d_j\n    iff (c-2)*d_j<=A_j,\n\nwhich follows from (3.2). The second child uses (6.2) at r=0.\nFor r>=1, c*theta<1 gives\nc*d_j*theta^r<=d_j*theta^(r-1). The first child gives at most theta^r,\nthe bridge then gives at most d_j*theta^r, and the second child gives\ntheta^(r+1). This proves the full geometric family simultaneously.\n\nFor 0<R<=d_m, choose r with\nd_m*theta^(r+1)<R<=d_m*theta^r. Then\n\n    U_m(R)<=R/d_m.                                 (6.3)\n\nEvery unit-Huber history containing a strict crossing has terminal\nradius at most a. At a level-k crossing the landing is at most d_k.\nFor k>=1 the full following H_k gives at most theta<a. For k=0 the\nlanding is at most a. At every actual later ancestor l>=1, the input\nis at most a<1, the bridge lands at most a*d_l<=d_l, and the full H_l\nagain gives at most theta. This proves the assertion with the exact\nsuffix (3.5), including all returns.\n\nFor R<=d_m, 2J_1(U_m(R))<=U_m(R)^2<=R^2/d_m^2.\nFor R>=d_m and a crossing, it is at most a^2/R^2<=a^2/d_m^2.\nFor sufficiently large m as here, d_m^2>D: by (3.2), D<2d_m/a,\nwhile d_m>2/a. Hence both crossing estimates are below1/D.\nA no-crossing path has terminal radius t with\nR>=t+S*min(t,1). For t<=1 use t^2<=R^2/(1+S)^2<=R^2/D.\nFor t>=1 use\n\n    (t+S)^2-D*(2t-1)=(t-S-1)^2>=0.\n\nThus the ENTIRE unit-Huber all-start profile is at most1/D.\nThe saturated start R=D attains1/D. Rescaling proves the same exact\nprofile for every B>0. This is a self-contained joint reference proof,\nnot an invocation of an earlier unverified report.\n\n## 7. Whole all-start outward-kink comparison region\n\nAlways G(r)<=min(r,B), so F(r)<=J_B(r). A strict crossing at radius r\nhas landing magnitude\n\n    h*G(r)-r <=(h-1)*G(r)<=d*min(r,B).              (7.1)\n\nFor an interior noncrossing query, its largest possible staying output\nis [1-h*kappa]_+*min(R,1). In the middle this follows because\nr-h*(r-v)=1-h*kappa-d*(r-1), which decreases with r.\n\nUnder (1.2), for every physical h>=ell,\n\n    [1-h*kappa]_+<=d,\n    [1-h*kappa]_+<=d*B.                            (7.2)\n\nThe first follows at h=ell from kappa>=kappa_0, and the second at\nh=ell from ell*kappa+aB>=1; increasing h preserves both.\nFor R<=B use the first inequality and min(R,1)<=R.\nFor R>=B use the second inequality.\nTogether with (7.1) and the actual tail stay, this gives\n\n    E_h(R)<=max(d*min(R,B),R-hB)=U_h,B(R).          (7.3)\n\nThe unit-Huber radius maps are nondecreasing. Chronological composition\ntherefore gives M_m(R)<=U_m,B(R). Since F<=J_B, Section6 proves (1.3)\nfor every start, every allowed B in (1.2), and every enclosing m>=1536.\n\nIf kappa>=kappa_1, then\n1-ell*kappa<=a*kappa<=aB; also kappa_1>kappa_0, because the\ndifference in the cross-multiplied numerators is2a^2>0.\nIf B>=1 and kappa>=kappa_0, then\nell*kappa+aB>=1-a+a=1. This proves the two stated convenient regions.\nFor example, kappa>=3/5 is an m-independent sufficient curvature range,\nsince a>2/5 gives kappa_1<5/9<3/5.\n\n## 8. All core starts: literal prefix invariance and objective margin\n\nThis subsection is uniform in ALL 0<kappa<=1. Define P_j as in Section1.\nWe prove jointly\n\n    P_j<=1,\n    kappa*d_j*P_j<=theta for j>=1,                 (8.1)\n\nand that every prefix product of factors |1-kappa*h| in H_j is at most1.\n\nAt j=1, kappa*|1-ell*kappa|<=a.\nOn the nonnegative-factor interval the quadratic kappa*(1-ell*kappa)\nhas maximum1/(4ell)<a. On the negative-factor interval\nkappa*(ell*kappa-1) increases up to a at kappa=1.\nSince d_1=beta, the second bound in (8.1) follows.\n\nSuppose the level-j bridge is a reversal, kappa*(1+d_j)>=1.\nPut t=kappa*d_j. Then |1-kappa*(1+d_j)|<=t, and\n\n    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)\n       <=c*(t*P_j)^2<=c*theta^2=theta*beta^2<theta.\n\nThe bridge applied after the first child has absolute multiplier\nat most t*P_j<=theta<1. Inductively, all prefixes of the second\nchild multiply this by at most1, proving prefix invariance.\n\nIf the level-j bridge is nonreversing, EVERY effective step in\nH_(j+1) lies in [0,1]. Thus\n\n    P_(j+1)<=exp(-kappa*S_(j+1)),\n    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)\n      <=1/(e*(S_(j+1)/d_(j+1)))<5/14<theta,\n\nusing (3.3) and e>2. Prefix products are plainly at most1 in this case.\nThe j=0 bridge has multiplier at most1 directly.\nThese arguments prove all assertions, including equality/zero factors.\n\nStarting with |s|<=1, every resulting quadratic prefix has radius<=1;\ninduction on actual physical indices therefore shows that it is also\nthe trajectory of the current kink function. Hence the payoff is exactly\nkappa*P_m^2, with no radius reset or altered child parameter.\n\nIf kappa*h_(m-1)^lev<=1, then\n\n    kappa*P_m^2<=kappa*exp(-2kappa*S)<=1/(2eS),\n    D*kappa*P_m^2<(1+1/(2S))/2<3/4.\n\nIf kappa*h_(m-1)^lev>1, (8.1) and D<2d_m/a give\n\n    D*kappa*P_m^2\n       <2a*beta^2*(1/d_m+1/c)<31/60<3/4.\n\nHere d_m>10 for m>=1536, a<1/2 and c>12/5 suffice.\nThis proves (1.4), uniformly even when kappa depends arbitrarily on m.\nIt also proves that a complete H_j begun inside the core never increases\nradius or gradient magnitude and remains core at every physical prefix.\n\n## 9. Entire odd family: exact cap capture and unscaled child plateau\n\nThere is an exact cap-level interval capture valid for every kappa,B:\n\n    M_j(u+B*S_j)=u,\n    M_j(R)=R-B*S_j for R>=u+B*S_j,\n    M_j(R)<=max(u,R-B*S_j) for every R>=0.          (9.1)\n\nAn exterior start stays saturated at all queries and gives the exterior\nformula. For the inductive capture input u+B*(2S_j+h_j^lev), the first\nchild gives u+B*(S_j+h_j^lev). The bridge's staying endpoint is\nu+B*S_j. Its largest crossing is at most d_j*B, which is smaller\nthan (1+S_j)*B<=u+B*S_j by u>=B and (3.2). An interior same-side\nendpoint is at most1<=u. The second child therefore gives exactly u.\n\nWe now prove (1.5) by induction, together with the following capture\nconsequence using proper-child plateaus:\n\n    a crossing in a complete H_j\n       => terminal gradient magnitude <=a*B.      (9.2)\n\nAt any level-k crossing, (7.1) bounds its landing radius by d_k*B.\nIf k>=1, the complete following H_k and its plateau give gradient<=aB.\nIf k=0 the landing radius is at most aB, so G of the landing is <=aB.\n\nAt each later ancestor l>=1, suppose the input gradient is <=aB<B.\nThe input cannot be a saturated tail point. If its bridge crosses,\n(7.1) bounds its landing by a*d_l*B<=d_l*B; the full H_l plateau\ngives gradient<=aB. If its bridge does not cross, the resulting point\nis inside the core: this is immediate for a core source, while a middle\nsource r>=1 has staying value\n1-h_l^lev*kappa-d_l*(r-1)<=1. Its gradient has decreased.\nSection8 shows the full following H_l remains core and does not increase\nthat gradient. A zero hit stays zero. This proves (9.2) along (3.5).\nWhen used for H_j it invokes plateaus only at levels <=j-1.\n\nFor the base j=1, input d_1 B=beta B has no positive tail stay.\nEvery crossing lands at radius<=a*beta*B. A positive interior stay\nhas output gradient at most\nkappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+*beta*B<=a*beta*B.\nThe core negative case uses the base inequality in Section8.\nThus G(M_1(d_1B))<=theta B<aB.\n\nSuppose the plateau is known through j>=1 and start the parent at any\nradius R<=c*d_j*B. The first-child image is bounded by (9.1).\nAt the parent bridge:\n\n* A crossing lands within d_jB.\n* A saturated same-side stay has radius at most\n  c*d_jB-(S_j+h_j^lev)B<=d_jB, by (3.2).\n* An interior same-side stay lies inside the core.\n\nThe first two cases are handled by the complete second child's plateau.\nFor the third case, if the first child already contained a crossing,\n(9.2) gives gradient<=aB before the bridge; the bridge and the full\ncore second child cannot increase it.\n\nIt remains to handle a first child with no crossing followed by an\ninterior noncrossing bridge. Let y be the first-child radius and z\nits post-bridge radius. Since all first-child radii decrease,\n\n    R>=y+S_j*G(y)>=(1+kappa*S_j)*y,\n    z=y-h_j^lev*G(y)<=(1-kappa*h_j^lev)*y.\n\nFor z>0 this forces kappa*h_j^lev<1, so every core factor in H_j\nis nonnegative and P_j<=exp(-kappa*S_j). Additional complete trajectory regions\n\nA same-side noncrossing segment of mass T from radius R to terminal\nradius r, with terminal gradient p=G(r), satisfies\n\n    R>=r+T*p,\n    2F(r)<=2r*p-p^2.\n\nThe second inequality is the actual optimum row from Section2.\nHence 2F(r)<=2R*p-(1+2T)*p^2<=R^2/(1+2T).\nThis proves the full-word no-crossing claim for every kappa,B.\n\nFor exactly one strict crossing, let it be a level-k step h=1+d_k,\nwith actual prefix/suffix masses P,T, source radius y and gradient p.\nThe noncrossing prefix gives R>=y+Pp>=(1+P)p.\nIts crossing lands at z=h*p-y<=d_k*p, and the noncrossing suffix gives\n\n    2F(terminal)/R^2\n      <=d_k^2/[(1+P)^2*(1+2T)].                    (10.1)\n\nSet C=A_k, N=S_m-S_(k+1), x=P-S_k, so 0<=x<=N and T=S_k+N-x.\nThe denominator is\n\n    (C+x)^2*(2C-1+2N-2x).\n\nIts derivative is 2(C+x)*(C-1+2N-3x); its minimum on [0,N]\nis at an endpoint. Since\nD=4C+2d_k-1+2N and d_k<C/2, at N=0,\n\n    d_k^2*(4C+2d_k-1)/(C^2*(2C-1))\n       <(5C-1)/(4*(2C-1))<=1.\n\nAdding the matching 2N terms preserves the comparison because d_k^2<C^2.\nThis proves (10.1)<=1/D for every physical cut, including k=0.\n\nFinally consider a trajectory every update of which is either a strict\ncrossing or a same-side stay queried on a saturated tail. Inequality(7.1)\nand the exact tail stay bound each actual radius by the unit-Huber\ninterval step from its preceding radius. Chronological composition and\nF<=J_B therefore give payoff<=1/D.\n\nThis includes every history whose queried AND terminal points all lie\noutside the open core: a middle query r>=1 has positive noncrossing\noutput at most1-h*kappa<1, so such a stay cannot occur in that history.\nIt also includes exterior queries followed by a final crossing into\nthe core. Knot equalities are handled by either adjacent piece.\n\nA final SAME-SIDE middle-to-core entry is a separate case. Its output\nradius is at most1-ell*kappa, but this fact does not imply unit-Huber\ndomination at low curvature. The complete core child is legitimate,\nbut its factor can be near1 at these low effective levels. The proof\nof (9.3) only handles the no-crossing first-child alternative; its\ncrossing alternative currently carries aB rather than aw.\nNo theorem-strength small-radius claim is hidden in that case split.\n\nThe exact remaining WHOLE-family question is\n\n    sup_(s!=0) 2F(|Psi_m(s)|)/s^2 <=17/(16D)\n    for every m>=1536 and every allowed kappa,B,    (11.2)\n\nor the stronger1/D version, or a complete all-m legal counter-witness.\nThis is an unresolved target, not a technical lemma being assumed.\nAfter removing the proved regions one may restrict attention to:\nparameters outside (1.2); |s|>1; at least two strict crossings; and\neither a core query before the terminal, or a final positive middle-to-core\nentry. Problem, normalization, and statements actually claimed\n\nFix any integer m>=1536, and keep the SAME parameters at every child:\n\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j),\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, S=S_m,\n    D=1+2S, n=2^m-1, ell=1+a, theta=a*beta.\n\nThe symbols h_i without a superscript mean the chronological physical\nentries of H_m. The auxiliary d_m is not an additional physical step.\nThe actual last physical step is ell.\n\nFix arbitrary 0<kappa<=1 and finite B>=kappa. Define\n\n    v=1-kappa, u=v+B,\n    G(r)=min(B,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,\n    g(x)=sign(x)*G(|x|), g(0)=0,\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt.\n\nWrite Psi_j(s) for the actual final iterate of the complete H_j from s on\nthis ONE fixed function. Let\n\n    M_j(R)=max_(|s|<=R) |Psi_j(s)|, R>=0.\n\nThe normalized payoff is 2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 for s!=0. The denominator is\nthe true squared distance to the unique minimizer, not a radius of a child.\n\nThe following are the scoped conclusions of this joint candidate:\n\n(A) Exact maximum over trajectories remaining on one saturated ray through\nthe terminal:\n\n    C_tail=B^2+v*(2B-kappa),\n    V_tail=B^2/(2*B^2*S+C_tail).                       (1.1)\n\nIt is attained by an explicit complete real itinerary in Section4.\nFor kappa<1 this is STRICTLY less than 1/D. At kappa=1 it equals 1/D.\nFor B=kappa the expression reduces to kappa/(1+2*kappa*S).\n\n(B) Define\n\n    kappa_0=(1-a)/(1+a), kappa_1=1/(1+2a).\n\nIf\n\n    kappa>=kappa_0 AND ell*kappa+a*B>=1,              (1.2)\n\nthen EVERY nonzero start on the current fixed outward-kink function has\n\n    2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2<=1/D. All subsequent\nreturns and every actual ancestor suffix are included in this statement.\nIt controls an absolute gradient cap; it does not by itself prove (1.3).\n\n(E) For EVERY allowed kappa,B, every full-word trajectory with at most\none strict crossing satisfies (1.3). Neither supplies the missing bound here. Even complete\nclosure of this odd family would not establish that full-class premise.\n\n## 2. Static function, actual integrals, and all ordered rows\n\nThe exact radial objective F(r)=f(r)=f(-r), r>=0, is\n\n    2F(r)=kappa*r^2,                        0<=r<=1,\n    2F(r)=(r-v)^2+kappa*v,                  1<=r<=u,\n    2F(r)=2B*r-C_tail,                      r>=u.    (2.1)\n\nAt r=1, the first two values are kappa. At r=u, r-v=B and\n2F(u)=B^2+kappa*v=2B*u-C_tail. Thus values and first derivatives\nmatch at both knots. The middle interval disappears at B=kappa.\nAt kappa=1, v=0 and the function is the usual unit-curvature Huber\nfunction, irrespective of whether B is greater than1.\n\nThe derivative g is continuous and nondecreasing, with slopes kappa,\n1, and0 on the relevant intervals. Partitioning any interval at its\nfinitely many knots proves |g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Hence f is finite,\nconvex, differentiable, and globally1-smooth. When a piece disappears\nthe same assertions remain valid. Since G(r)>0 for every r>0,\nthe unique minimum is0 at x=0. The true initial distance is |s|.\n\nFor every physical index including the terminal, define\n\n    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i),\n    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i).\n\nAlso include x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For x<y set Delta=g(y)-g(x).\nMonotonicity and the Lipschitz bound give 0<=Delta<=y-x and, on [x,y],\n\n    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),\n    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).\n\nIntegrating gives both directed convexity remainders at least Delta^2/2.\nConsequently all ordered rows, not just adjacent or outgoing terminal\nrows, obey\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.      (2.2)\n\nEqual positions give equality. These are actual samples of one fixed\nfunction, so repeated visits cannot assign different derivatives or\nvalues. The full Gram matrix is PSD because each of its quadratic forms\nis a square of a real linear combination of actual scalar vectors.\nNo formal gradient or zero-step convention is deleted.\n\nFor arbitrary advertised L>0 use L*f with physical step sizes h_i/L.\nThe iterates remain x_i and the payoff remains 2*f(x_n)/s^2.\nIn particular, when the actual Lipschitz constant is smaller than1,\nit is NOT used to rescale the physical word or the performance.\n\n## 3. Same-m geometry and constants\n\nThe following elementary bounds hold throughout this report:\n\n    12/5<c<17/7<5/2, beta>99/100,\n    2/5<a<1/2, 2/5<theta<1/2,\n    c*a=beta<1, c*theta=beta^2<1.                   (3.1)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)>7/5 gives the lower c bound.\nUsing epsilon<1/1000 and sqrt(2)<17/12 gives\nc<(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7. The lower a bound follows from\na>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5; the theta bound follows in the same way.\n\nSet eta=a/(c-2). The strict inequality c(c-2)>1>beta gives 0<eta<1.\nThe exact mass identity and its solution are\n\n    A_(j+1)=2*A_j+d_j,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,\n    d_j/A_j<c-2<3/7<1/2,\n    A_j<c^j for j>=1.                              (3.2)\n\nThese follow by substitution into the literal word recursion.\nAlso\n\n    S_j/d_j>7/5, j>=1.                             (3.3)\n\nThe base is S_1/d_1=ell/beta>7/5. If L_j=S_j/d_j>7/5, then\nL_(j+1)=(2L_j+1+1/d_j)/c>(19/5)/(17/7)>7/5.\nNo recurrence was executed to obtain these assertions.\n\nAt any physical occurrence of a level-k bridge in H_j, let P,T be\nthe actual masses before and after that occurrence within H_j.\nIts two full adjacent H_k children imply\n\n    P>=S_k, T>=S_k, P+T=S_j-h_k^lev.                (3.4)\n\nIts actual remaining suffix is\n\n    H_k,h_(l_1)^lev,H_(l_1),...,h_(l_t)^lev,H_(l_t),\n    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1. This includes the real last leaf; after a\nfinal leaf the list is empty.\n\n## 4. Exact saturated itinerary and the changed tail intercept\n\nChoose\n\n    R_star=2B*S+C_tail/B,\n    r_star=R_star-B*S=B*S+C_tail/B.                 (4.1)\n\nThe complete chronological trajectory is explicitly\n\n    x_i=R_star-B*P_i,\n    P_i=sum_(0<=t<i) h_t, 0<=i<=n.                 (4.2)\n\nIndeed\n\n    r_star-u=B*S+v*(1-kappa/B)>=0.\n\nThus every x_i>=r_star>=u, every gradient equals B, every physical\nupdate in (4.2) is valid, and x_n=r_star after the actual final leaf.\nAll data inherit every ordered row from Section2.\n\nFor any positive saturated start whose terminal is still saturated,\nwrite R>=B*S+u. Its payoff is\n\n    [2B*R-2B^2*S-C_tail]/R^2.\n\nDifferentiation in R, or completing a square in 1/R, gives its unique\nmaximum at (4.1), with value (1.1). Reflection gives the negative ray.\nBecause C_tail>=B^2,\n\n    V_tail<=1/(1+2S).\n\nIf kappa<1, v>0 and 2B-kappa>=kappa>0, so the inequality is strict.\nThis is not a full profile formula: a terminal in the middle/core or\na crossing history has not been optimized by this calculation.\nIn particular, substituting the ordinary Huber intercept B^2 into the\ncurrent tail would give an incorrect score.\n\n## 5. Exact interval update and the outer crossing term\n\nFor a physical step h>=1, put d=h-1 and q_h=|1-h*kappa|.\nThe exact radius of the image of [-R,R] is\n\n    E_h(R)=max{\n       q_h*min(R,1),\n       [d*min(R,u)-h*v]_+,\n       [R-h*B]_+\n    }.                                             (5.1)\n\nHere [z]_+=max(z,0). In the core the map is (1-h*kappa)x.\nIn the middle its positive-ray expression is -d*r+h*v.\nIn the tail it is r-h*B. The extrema occur at0, a knot, or the input\nendpoint; including a formal term from an unavailable piece does not\nincrease the maximum, since that term is then dominated by an available\nendpoint. These observations establish (5.1) also at R=1,u.\n\nThe signed step is continuous and odd and fixes0, so its interval image\nis the entire symmetric interval with this radius. Therefore successive\ncomposition of E_h is exact, rather than a choice of incompatible starts.\nIn particular M_(j+1)=M_j composed with E_(h_j^lev) composed with M_j.\n\nWhen both the source and the destination of a strict crossing are in the\nmiddle, put z=G(r)=r-v. The destination gradient is\n\n    z_new=(h-1)*z-2v.                               (5.2)\n\nThis displays the lost fixed-curvature assumption. The term -2v is\nretained; a child cannot be treated as a homogeneous quadratic in z.\n\nEquation (5.1) alone is NOT the claimed result. Sections6-10 give the\nquantitative deductions obtained from it, and Section11 states what is\nstill missing.\n\n## 6. Complete unit-Huber reference, rederived jointly\n\nLet J_B have derivative sign(x)*min(|x|,B), and write U_j for its interval\nradius map under the same H_j. Scaling x by B reduces this subsection to\nB=1. Its exact single-step map is\n\n    U_h(R)=max(d*min(R,1),R-h).\n\nThe maximum is nonnegative because its first term is. Direct induction\ngives exact capture and the exterior branch:\n\n    U_j(A_j)=1,\n    U_j(R)=R-S_j for R>=A_j,\n    U_j(R)<=max(1,R-S_j) for all R>=0.              (6.1)\n\nFor the induction, start at A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j.\nThe first child gives A_j+d_j+1. The bridge's staying endpoint is A_j,\nand its crossing endpoint is at most d_j<A_j. The second child gives1.\nAn exterior start stays on the unit tail at every actual query.\n\nFor j>=1 and every integer r>=0,\n\n    U_j(d_j*theta^r)<=theta^(r+1).                  (6.2)\n\nThe leaf base is a*beta*theta^r=theta^(r+1).\nAt the unscaled parent input c*d_j, (6.1) bounds the first child by\nmax(1,c*d_j-S_j). The bridge sends this to at most d_j, since\n\n    c*d_j-S_j-h_j^lev<=d_j\n    iff (c-2)*d_j<=A_j,\n\nwhich follows from (3.2). The second child uses (6.2) at r=0.\nFor r>=1, c*theta<1 gives\nc*d_j*theta^r<=d_j*theta^(r-1). The first child gives at most theta^r,\nthe bridge then gives at most d_j*theta^r, and the second child gives\ntheta^(r+1). This proves the full geometric family simultaneously.\n\nFor 0<R<=d_m, choose r with\nd_m*theta^(r+1)<R<=d_m*theta^r. Then\n\n    U_m(R)<=R/d_m.                                 (6.3)\n\nEvery unit-Huber history containing a strict crossing has terminal\nradius at most a. At a level-k crossing the landing is at most d_k.\nFor k>=1 the full following H_k gives at most theta<a. For k=0 the\nlanding is at most a. At every actual later ancestor l>=1, the input\nis at most a<1, the bridge lands at most a*d_l<=d_l, and the full H_l\nagain gives at most theta. This proves the assertion with the exact\nsuffix (3.5), including all returns.\n\nFor R<=d_m, 2J_1(U_m(R))<=U_m(R)^2<=R^2/d_m^2.\nFor R>=d_m and a crossing, it is at most a^2/R^2<=a^2/d_m^2.\nFor sufficiently large m as here, d_m^2>D: by (3.2), D<2d_m/a,\nwhile d_m>2/a. Hence both crossing estimates are below1/D.\nA no-crossing path has terminal radius t with\nR>=t+S*min(t,1). For t<=1 use t^2<=R^2/(1+S)^2<=R^2/D.\nFor t>=1 use\n\n    (t+S)^2-D*(2t-1)=(t-S-1)^2>=0.\n\nThus the ENTIRE unit-Huber all-start profile is at most1/D.\nThe saturated start R=D attains1/D. Rescaling proves the same exact\nprofile for every B>0. This is a self-contained joint reference proof,\nnot an invocation of an earlier unverified report.\n\n## 7. Whole all-start outward-kink comparison region\n\nAlways G(r)<=min(r,B), so F(r)<=J_B(r). A strict crossing at radius r\nhas landing magnitude\n\n    h*G(r)-r <=(h-1)*G(r)<=d*min(r,B).              (7.1)\n\nFor an interior noncrossing query, its largest possible staying output\nis [1-h*kappa]_+*min(R,1). In the middle this follows because\nr-h*(r-v)=1-h*kappa-d*(r-1), which decreases with r.\n\nUnder (1.2), for every physical h>=ell,\n\n    [1-h*kappa]_+<=d,\n    [1-h*kappa]_+<=d*B.                            (7.2)\n\nThe first follows at h=ell from kappa>=kappa_0, and the second at\nh=ell from ell*kappa+aB>=1; increasing h preserves both.\nFor R<=B use the first inequality and min(R,1)<=R.\nFor R>=B use the second inequality.\nTogether with (7.1) and the actual tail stay, this gives\n\n    E_h(R)<=max(d*min(R,B),R-hB)=U_h,B(R).          (7.3)\n\nThe unit-Huber radius maps are nondecreasing. Chronological composition\ntherefore gives M_m(R)<=U_m,B(R). Since F<=J_B, Section6 proves (1.3)\nfor every start, every allowed B in (1.2), and every enclosing m>=1536.\n\nIf kappa>=kappa_1, then\n1-ell*kappa<=a*kappa<=aB; also kappa_1>kappa_0, because the\ndifference in the cross-multiplied numerators is2a^2>0.\nIf B>=1 and kappa>=kappa_0, then\nell*kappa+aB>=1-a+a=1. This proves the two stated convenient regions.\nFor example, kappa>=3/5 is an m-independent sufficient curvature range,\nsince a>2/5 gives kappa_1<5/9<3/5.\n\n## 8. All core starts: literal prefix invariance and objective margin\n\nThis subsection is uniform in ALL 0<kappa<=1. Define P_j as in Section1.\nWe prove jointly\n\n    P_j<=1,\n    kappa*d_j*P_j<=theta for j>=1,                 (8.1)\n\nand that every prefix product of factors |1-kappa*h| in H_j is at most1.\n\nAt j=1, kappa*|1-ell*kappa|<=a.\nOn the nonnegative-factor interval the quadratic kappa*(1-ell*kappa)\nhas maximum1/(4ell)<a. On the negative-factor interval\nkappa*(ell*kappa-1) increases up to a at kappa=1.\nSince d_1=beta, the second bound in (8.1) follows.\n\nSuppose the level-j bridge is a reversal, kappa*(1+d_j)>=1.\nPut t=kappa*d_j. Then |1-kappa*(1+d_j)|<=t, and\n\n    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)\n       <=c*(t*P_j)^2<=c*theta^2=theta*beta^2<theta.\n\nThe bridge applied after the first child has absolute multiplier\nat most t*P_j<=theta<1. Inductively, all prefixes of the second\nchild multiply this by at most1, proving prefix invariance.\n\nIf the level-j bridge is nonreversing, EVERY effective step in\nH_(j+1) lies in [0,1]. Thus\n\n    P_(j+1)<=exp(-kappa*S_(j+1)),\n    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)\n      <=1/(e*(S_(j+1)/d_(j+1)))<5/14<theta,\n\nusing (3.3) and e>2. Prefix products are plainly at most1 in this case.\nThe j=0 bridge has multiplier at most1 directly.\nThese arguments prove all assertions, including equality/zero factors.\n\nStarting with |s|<=1, every resulting quadratic prefix has radius<=1;\ninduction on actual physical indices therefore shows that it is also\nthe trajectory of the current kink function. Hence the payoff is exactly\nkappa*P_m^2, with no radius reset or altered child parameter.\n\nIf kappa*h_(m-1)^lev<=1, then\n\n    kappa*P_m^2<=kappa*exp(-2kappa*S)<=1/(2eS),\n    D*kappa*P_m^2<(1+1/(2S))/2<3/4.\n\nIf kappa*h_(m-1)^lev>1, (8.1) and D<2d_m/a give\n\n    D*kappa*P_m^2\n       <2a*beta^2*(1/d_m+1/c)<31/60<3/4.\n\nHere d_m>10 for m>=1536, a<1/2 and c>12/5 suffice.\nThis proves (1.4), uniformly even when kappa depends arbitrarily on m.\nIt also proves that a complete H_j begun inside the core never increases\nradius or gradient magnitude and remains core at every physical prefix.\n\n## 9. Entire odd family: exact cap capture and unscaled child plateau\n\nThere is an exact cap-level interval capture valid for every kappa,B:\n\n    M_j(u+B*S_j)=u,\n    M_j(R)=R-B*S_j for R>=u+B*S_j,\n    M_j(R)<=max(u,R-B*S_j) for every R>=0.          (9.1)\n\nAn exterior start stays saturated at all queries and gives the exterior\nformula. For the inductive capture input u+B*(2S_j+h_j^lev), the first\nchild gives u+B*(S_j+h_j^lev). The bridge's staying endpoint is\nu+B*S_j. Its largest crossing is at most d_j*B, which is smaller\nthan (1+S_j)*B<=u+B*S_j by u>=B and (3.2). An interior same-side\nendpoint is at most1<=u. The second child therefore gives exactly u.\n\nWe now prove (1.5) by induction, together with the following capture\nconsequence using proper-child plateaus:\n\n    a crossing in a complete H_j\n       => terminal gradient magnitude <=a*B.      (9.2)\n\nAt any level-k crossing, (7.1) bounds its landing radius by d_k*B.\nIf k>=1, the complete following H_k and its plateau give gradient<=aB.\nIf k=0 the landing radius is at most aB, so G of the landing is <=aB.\n\nAt each later ancestor l>=1, suppose the input gradient is <=aB<B.\nThe input cannot be a saturated tail point. If its bridge crosses,\n(7.1) bounds its landing by a*d_l*B<=d_l*B; the full H_l plateau\ngives gradient<=aB. If its bridge does not cross, the resulting point\nis inside the core: this is immediate for a core source, while a middle\nsource r>=1 has staying value\n1-h_l^lev*kappa-d_l*(r-1)<=1. Its gradient has decreased.\nSection8 shows the full following H_l remains core and does not increase\nthat gradient. A zero hit stays zero. This proves (9.2) along (3.5).\nWhen used for H_j it invokes plateaus only at levels <=j-1.\n\nFor the base j=1, input d_1 B=beta B has no positive tail stay.\nEvery crossing lands at radius<=a*beta*B. A positive interior stay\nhas output gradient at most\nkappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+*beta*B<=a*beta*B.\nThe core negative case uses the base inequality in Section8.\nThus G(M_1(d_1B))<=theta B<aB.\n\nSuppose the plateau is known through j>=1 and start the parent at any\nradius R<=c*d_j*B. The first-child image is bounded by (9.1).\nAt the parent bridge:\n\n* A crossing lands within d_jB.\n* A saturated same-side stay has radius at most\n  c*d_jB-(S_j+h_j^lev)B<=d_jB, by (3.2).\n* An interior same-side stay lies inside the core.\n\nFor the saturated case, the first child cannot have contained a crossing:\n(9.2), using only proper-child plateaus, would make its terminal gradient\nat most aB<B. Its first child is therefore noncrossing and ends at radius\ny>=u. Every earlier radius is at least y, so the ENTIRE first child is\nsaturated and y=R-S_jB exactly. This justifies the displayed bound even\nat y=u; the max(u,R-S_jB) estimate alone would not suffice.\n\nThe first two cases are handled by the complete second child's plateau.\nFor the third case, if the first child already contained a crossing,\n(9.2) gives gradient<=aB before the bridge; the bridge and the full\ncore second child cannot increase it.\n\nIt remains to handle a first child with no crossing followed by an\ninterior noncrossing bridge. Let y be the first-child radius and z\nits post-bridge radius. Since all first-child radii decrease,\n\n    R>=y+S_j*G(y)>=(1+kappa*S_j)*y,\n    z=y-h_j^lev*G(y)<=(1-kappa*h_j^lev)*y.\n\nFor z>0 this forces kappa*h_j^lev<1, so every core factor in H_j\nis nonnegative and P_j<=exp(-kappa*S_j). Put\nt=kappa*d_j and L_j=S_j/d_j. The terminal gradient is bounded by\n\n    kappa*P_j*z\n     <= c*t*(1-kappa-t)*exp(-L_j*t)/(1+L_j*t)*B\n     <= c*t*(1-t)/(1+L_j*t)^2*B\n     <= c/[4*(1+L_j)]*B\n     <85/336*B<2/5*B<aB.                           (9.3)\n\nThe penultimate inequality follows from the exact square identity\n\n    (1+L*t)^2-4*(1+L)*t*(1-t)=(1-(L+2)*t)^2.\n\nThis completes the plateau induction for EVERY j and EVERY positive\nkappa,B, with all repeated returns included. It is a quantitative\nall-depth result, not just the interval recurrence.\n\nTo see its limitation, let r_a=G^(-1)(aB), the unique pre-cap radius\nhaving gradient aB. Its true objective is\n\n    2F(r_a)=a^2*B^2/kappa,                  aB<=kappa,\n    2F(r_a)=a^2*B^2+kappa*(1-kappa),        aB>=kappa. (9.4)\n\nConsequently (9.2) gives at most 2F(r_a)/R^2 for a start of radius R.\nThis does NOT control all small R relative to B. The additive middle\narea in (9.4) must not be discarded.\n\n## 10. Additional complete trajectory regions\n\nA same-side noncrossing segment of mass T from radius R to terminal\nradius r, with terminal gradient p=G(r), satisfies\n\n    R>=r+T*p,\n    2F(r)<=2r*p-p^2.\n\nThe second inequality is the actual optimum row from Section2.\nHence 2F(r)<=2R*p-(1+2T)*p^2<=R^2/(1+2T).\nThis proves the full-word no-crossing claim for every kappa,B.\n\nFor exactly one strict crossing, let it be a level-k step h=1+d_k,\nwith actual prefix/suffix masses P,T, source radius y and gradient p.\nThe noncrossing prefix gives R>=y+Pp>=(1+P)p.\nIts crossing lands at z=h*p-y<=d_k*p, and the noncrossing suffix gives\n\n    2F(terminal)/R^2\n      <=d_k^2/[(1+P)^2*(1+2T)].                    (10.1)\n\nSet C=A_k, N=S_m-S_(k+1), x=P-S_k, so 0<=x<=N and T=S_k+N-x.\nThe denominator is\n\n    (C+x)^2*(2C-1+2N-2x).\n\nIts derivative is 2(C+x)*(C-1+2N-3x); its minimum on [0,N]\nis at an endpoint. The endpoint difference, value at N minus value\nat0, is N*(2C(C-1)+N(2C-1))>=0.\nThus its minimum is C^2*(2C-1+2N). Since\nD=4C+2d_k-1+2N and d_k<C/2, at N=0,\n\n    d_k^2*(4C+2d_k-1)/(C^2*(2C-1))\n       <(5C-1)/(4*(2C-1))<=1.\n\nAdding the matching 2N terms preserves the comparison because d_k^2<C^2.\nThis proves (10.1)<=1/D for every physical cut, including k=0.\n\nFinally consider a trajectory every update of which is either a strict\ncrossing or a same-side stay queried on a saturated tail. Inequality(7.1)\nand the exact tail stay bound each actual radius by the unit-Huber\ninterval step from its preceding radius. Chronological composition and\nF<=J_B therefore give payoff<=1/D.\n\nThis includes every history whose queried AND terminal points all lie\noutside the open core: a middle query r>=1 has positive noncrossing\noutput at most1-h*kappa<1, so such a stay cannot occur in that history.\nIt also includes exterior queries followed by a final crossing into\nthe core. Knot equalities are handled by either adjacent piece.\n\nA final SAME-SIDE middle-to-core entry is a separate case. Its output\nradius is at most1-ell*kappa, but this fact does not imply unit-Huber\ndomination at low curvature. No bound is claimed here for that case\nunless another proved region, such as at most one crossing, applies.\n\n## 11. Direct-proof screen, concrete failures, and the remaining target\n\nOne-step unit-Huber domination is FALSE on the entire parameter family.\nFor the single fixed legal law kappa=1/4, B=1 and any 0<R<=1, the\nphysical leaf produces\n\n    E_ell(R)=(3-a)*R/4 > aR=U_ell,1(R),\n\nbecause a<1/2. This is a counterexample to a LOCAL comparison lemma,\nnot a complete-word improving witness. Section8 in fact bounds every\none of these core-start complete-word histories strictly below1/D.\n\nNor can the outward middle simply be replaced by the clipped kappa\nquadratic. For that same law, at R=u=7/4 and any h>=8 the actual\ncrossing radius is h-7/4. The clipped-kappa comparison with derivative\nmin(r/4,1) has interval output (h/4-1)*7/4 there, smaller by9h/16.\nA level with h>=8 exists in every specified H_m (level4 already suffices).\nAgain this refutes a step comparison, not the full-word target.\n\nThe first direct attempt was to propagate unit-Huber domination.\nIt succeeds exactly as a sufficient argument on (1.2), and fails as\na universal argument by the first local example.\n\nThe second direct attempt was to propagate a cap-based state through\nthe kink using the physical interval maps. It proves (1.5) and (9.2)\nfor the ENTIRE family, without assuming fixed curvature in a child.\nIts unresolved extension would need small input radii, not just d_jB.\nA tempting statement is\n\n    G(M_j(d_j*w))<=a*w for every 0<w<=B.             (11.1)\n\nThis report neither proves nor refutes (11.1).\nIt does NOT follow by rescaling (1.5): the kink remains at radius1,\nand replacing B by w changes the function on the entire actual trajectory.\nIn an attempted geometric induction, a bridge that stays on its current\nside lands in the core. The incoming gradient bound w only gives\na physical radius w/kappa. For kappa*h_j^lev<=1 the bridge need not\ncontract it by an extra factor a. The complete core child is legitimate,\nbut its factor can be near1 at these low effective levels. The proof\nof (9.3) only handles the no-crossing first-child alternative; its\ncrossing alternative currently carries aB rather than aw.\nNo theorem-strength small-radius claim is hidden in that case split.\n\nThe exact remaining WHOLE-family question is\n\n    sup_(s!=0) 2F(|Psi_m(s)|)/s^2 <=17/(16D)\n    for every m>=1536 and every allowed kappa,B,    (11.2)\n\nor the stronger1/D version, or a complete all-m legal counter-witness.\nThis is an unresolved target, not a technical lemma being assumed.\nAfter removing the proved regions one may restrict attention to:\nparameters outside (1.2); |s|>1; at least two strict crossings; and\neither a core query before the terminal, or a final positive middle-to-core\nentry. The last-entry exception is part of the remaining region; it is not\nsilently included in the exterior crossing/tail comparison.\n\nThe original unrestricted all-dimension/all-function minimax problem\nremains open irrespective of whether (11.2) ultimately holds.\nThe optional asymmetric family is not entered: no decisive full first-law\nresult authorizes that escalation in this invocation.\n\n## 12. Worker max, invocation87.\nOriginal exact-profile minimax target OPEN. No fact_submit, no new fact_id.\nGuidance136c8e6e0b9c4945; plan d0d3e2892870802d.\n\n## 1. Outcome and exact scope\n\nThe prescribed late common column genuinely repairs the last-column\nshortage. Its full original-space PSD condition, all optimum signs,\nlast adjacent-pair cut, and both endpoint row-moment screens pass for\nevery SAME enclosing m>=1536. The source column nevertheless fails:\n\n    c_0 := N_00 - nu_0 - 2alpha_0 < -1/128.\n\nHere nu_0=1/64 is the specified incoming optimum multiplier. This is\nthe sum of ALL finite incoming multipliers at the first query, not a\nsign test of one arbitrarily selected skew completion. The entire law\nfails for every compatible skew correction.\n\nThe second mechanism is a whole-class obstruction for the genuinely\nenlarged PSD-detail architecture. Even after changing the detail\nmatrix, its clock energy, the paid column, and the common column,\nfull original-space PSD implies the source capacity bound\n\n    N_00 <= b^2 q/a.\n\nConsequently nonnegative finite incoming coefficients require\n\n    nu_0 + 2alpha_0 <= b^2 q/a.                    (1.1)\n\nFor q<=17/(16D), the right side is <6p<1/128, whereas the prescribed\nnu_0 is1/64. No PSD-detail repair retaining that incoming load can\nsucceed in this architecture. No unspecified PSD matrix is assumed\nfeasible: the second mechanism derives a necessary inequality for\nall members and gives literal chronological detail families it covers.\n\nThis is a restriction on coefficients and their paid initial charge.\nIt is not full-dual infeasibility, not a legal primal counterexample,\nnot a lower bound for W, and not a resolution of the original question.\nThe surviving freedom includes nu_0 of order p or zero, with all other\nloads and individual signs then rederived. No third law is attempted.\n\nEvery new result below belongs to this ONE joint unverified draft.\nNo earlier unverified finding is used as an accepted intermediate lemma.\nOnly accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 are used at their\nstated scopes. The latter's799/800 implication remains conditional.\n\n## 2. Original target, coordinates, and uniform scalar bounds\n\nFor a fixed nonnegative length-n word h and L>0, W_n(h) is the supremum\nof 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional\nconvex differentiable L-smooth functions with a minimizer and x_0!=x_star,\nusing x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original problem is\nwhether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, with W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and\nU_n^ZJ the exact all-split Zhang--Jiang benchmark, not a decimal profile.\n\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the physical hierarchy and the scalar\ncomparison B_n/D<47/50, where U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Its separation conclusion\nrequires the still-unproved full bound W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for all m>=1536.\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation orientation\nand exact unqueried-terminal reduction. No queried sample is deleted.\n\nFix an arbitrary integer m>=1536. All children retain these SAME parameters:\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1,\n    S=sum_i h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nAll short notation is LOCAL to this report. In particular rho below is\na probability vector, not the benchmark exponent; T is a scalar clock,\nLtri a matrix, and a superscript T means transpose. Public names ending87\nare aliases for this report's definitions, not project-glossary overrides.\nLet e be the all-ones column on the n queried indices and e_i its coordinate\ncolumns. Define\n\n    Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_ij=h_j for j<i, Ltri_ij=0 for j>i,\n    t=Ltri e, t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j,\n    T=t_l=1/p-b, Delta=T-1, hgap=1+beta, t_r=T-hgap.\n\nThe last three steps are (b,hgap,b). Elementary bounds are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 2/5<a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12,\n    1-2a>a^2, 7/5<b<17/12, 15/8<hgap<2,\n    c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-40), 3/(4p)<Delta<1/p,\n    Delta-b>1/(2p), p<dclock:=1/Delta<4p/3,\n    0<rclock:=hgap/Delta<4p.                       (2.1)\n\nFor the lower a bound use sqrt(2)<10/7 and\n7*6143/(17*6145)>2/5. Its upper bound follows from beta/(1+eps)<1.\nThe quadratic inequality follows from1+a<sqrt(2).\nThe hierarchy mass, also obtained by substitution into\n1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j, is\n\n    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).\n\nThis proves the p bounds; the remaining clock inequalities follow by\nsubstituting Delta=1/p-b-1. These are symbolic all-m inequalities,\nnot evaluated recurrences or asymptotic approximations.\n\nFor integer suffix length Ls in[0,n], let sbit(Ls) be its binary digit\nsum and put\n\n    rtail_Ls=product_(j=n-Ls)^(n-1)(h_j-1)\n             =beta^Ls*c^(-sbit(Ls)), s=rtail_n,\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j), u_i=|k_i|,\n    U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nIndeed h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j), where v2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing j,\nand sum_(j=1)^Ls v2(j)=Ls-sbit(Ls). With Ls=l-i,\n\n    u_i=rtail_Ls+rtail_(Ls+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,\n    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a*hgap, u_(l-2)=b*a*beta.\n\nThe last three u terms exceed4/3,5/8,7/18, so U>7/3.\nAlso 2^m-1>16m^2 for m>=16 by induction, and\nbeta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2. Hence beta^n<2^(-4m), and\n\n    u_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64.\n\nIt follows that\n\n    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, s<p, rho_i>0.                 (2.2)\n\nFinite product telescoping gives\n\n    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S.               (2.3)\n\nFor the terminal pool define\n\n    Vtail=t^T[k-(1-p)rho], Vlast=b(1-p).\n\nHere Vtail>32, as follows without an external estimate. Set\nZtail=sum_(Ls=0)^n rtail_Ls and Mtail=sum_(Ls=0)^n Ls*rtail_Ls.\nExpansion over binary digits gives\n\n    Ztail=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    U=2Ztail-1-s,\n    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)).\n\nChoose j with m/2<2^j<=m; Bernoulli's inequality gives its summand\n>3m/28. An index shift gives\nsum_i rho_i(l-i)=(2Mtail-Ztail+1-ns)/U>(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2,\nusing ns<1 and positivity of the numerator. Each suffix mass\n1/p-t_i is at least b+l-i. Since\nVtail=(1-p)sum_i rho_i(1/p-t_i), this proves Vtail>32 for m>=1536.\n\nKeep the EXACT assigned mixture\n\n    xiT=(2-Vlast)/(Vtail-Vlast),\n    alpha=(1-p)[(1-xiT)e_l+xiT rho], alpha_star=p,\n    v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s, eta=F/E.\n\nSince4/3<Vlast<17/12 and Vtail>32,\n\n    0<xiT<1/45, e^T alpha=1-p, V:=t^T v=2,\n    alpha_l>19/20, a+p<=v_l<1/2,\n    0<alpha_0<u_0, 0<v_0<u_0,\n    e^T v=E, 1-p/32<eta<1.                       (2.4)\n\nFor example alpha_l>44(1-p)/45>19/20 and\nv_l=b-alpha_l<17/12-19/20<1/2. At0,\nalpha_0/u_0=(1-p)xiT/U<1. The eta bound uses2s/E<p/32.\nThe vector R=Ltri^T v satisfies\n\n    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0,\n    R_0=bE-a v_0<2p.                             (2.5)\n\nThe first identity follows by subtracting Ltri^T alpha from (2.3).\n\n## 3. Full original-space identity and literal coefficients\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain the original formal\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n independently. From accepted1252 use\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nPut y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 on every queried index.\nThe actual GD substitutions give\n\n    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g, g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                            (3.1)\n\nThe unit-diagonal triangular Ltri is invertible, so no original\nqueried-gradient direction is removed. The terminal gradient g_n\nis still a separate free vector.\n\nFor positive weights w, define Ww=sum_i w_i and\nK=diag(w)-ww^T/Ww. For e^T z=E and e^T zeta=0 put\n\n    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T, d=zeta+F(v-z),\n    A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).                 (3.2)\n\nThe COMPLETE signed original coefficient array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,l},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct i,j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i.\n\nAll diagonal lambda entries vanish. For every i,j<n,\n\n    N_ij=P_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(P_uj+A_uj). The terminal contributes2alpha_i, so the finite-node\nvalue divergences vanish; terminal divergence is2 and optimum is-2.\n\nFor prefix indices including the optimum,\nQ_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>.\nThe prefix quadratic is -g^T N y=-<x_0,(Pe-d)^T y>+y^T P y.\nAfter including -2f_n, the terminal contribution is\n\n    ||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2\n                                       -2sum_i alpha_i delta_i.\n\nThe values cancel. Using Pe=Ev+Fz and eta E^2=p^2-s^2 gives\n\n    M(q):=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n      =||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.          (3.4)\n\nLet ybar_w=sum w_i y_i/Ww. Completing the weighted squares gives\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+zeta_i*x_0/(2w_i)||^2\n       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                   (3.5)\n\nNecessity is literal: set scalar x_0=1,\ny_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i), c0=1+sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i E),\nand g_n=-s+v^T y. Every displayed square vanishes; (3.1) recovers\nall gradients. Thus full PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J.\nThe charge is paid ONCE. The common queried-gradient direction g_i=G\nleaves prefix remainder\n(q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>+(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2,\nwhere X=t^T Kt, theta=t^T zeta, Z=t^T z. It has not been discarded.\n\n## 4. Mechanism1: prescribed nonzero-clock common column\n\nSet\n\n    z=b e_l+(E-b)e_r, Z=E*t_r+b*hgap,\n    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload e_0,\n    xiM=a(1-a)-a^3/8, sigma=xiM/(1-xiM),\n    omega=p^2, what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega rho,\n    theta0=2-6p, theta=theta0+ellload,\n    Xreq=theta0/p-4-eta Z^2+ellload,\n    tbase=sum what_i t_i/sum what_i,\n    Vbase=sum what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,\n    w=(Xreq/Vbase)what, zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.        (4.1)\n\nThis changes the common column itself. It has e^T z=E, z_0=0,\nz_l=b, and genuinely nonzero clock Z.
