All what_i are strictly positive.\nThe two unit endpoint weights imply Vbase>=Delta^2/2. Further,\n\n    2/p-26<Xreq<2/p,\n    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p>0,\n    1<Xreq/Delta<2, |Xreq/Delta-2|<32p,\n    2<theta<3, theta/Xreq>p.                     (4.3)\n\nFor the second lower bound subtract at most20 from theta0/p,\nwhich is greater than20480. For the ratio subtract2 from\n[2-(10+eta Z^2-ellload)p]/[1-(b+1)p]. Finally\n\ntheta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+p(4+eta Z^2)>0.\n\nThus e^T zeta=0, t^T Kt=Xreq, t^T zeta=theta and\n\n    J=theta^2/(4Xreq), p^2+J<(17/32)p<qtar.        (4.4)\n\nTo justify the strict bound, theta0>1, ellload=1/64 and (4.3) give\nJ<(theta0*p/4)(1+1/64)^2*1024/1023\n <(4225/8184)p<(67/128)p.\nThen use p<1/128 and qtar=17p/[16(2-p)].\n\nFor any skew in (3.2), multiplication gives\n\n    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta,\n    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha,\n    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    t^T nu=Xreq+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.           (4.5)\n\nHere V=2, alpha^T t=1/p-3, so both moments equal ellload,\nmatching nu_target. A literal skew completion is\n\n    rvec=Kt+2v+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target,\n    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,\n    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,\n    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,\n    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.             (4.6)\n\nThe two moments imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.\nConsequently e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0, and direct multiplication\nproves Ae=d and At=rvec. Each term is skew. All other compatible\ncompletions are A+C with C^T=-C, Ce=Ct=0. The displayed law takes C=0;\nthe obstructions below allow all such C.\n\nEvery terminal and incoming optimum sign passes. Every outgoing\noptimum sign also passes. The zeta_i have the signs of t_i-tbase,\nchange sign once, and sum to zero. Every proper suffix sum is positive.\nIf zeta_i<0, the suffix after i is at least -zeta_i, so\n(Ltri^T zeta)_i>= (h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0. If zeta_i>=0 its expression\nzeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j is also positive, including i=l.\nEquation(2.5) then gives mu_i>0 for all queried i.\n\n## 5. Every diagonal/column equation; the decisive first-column failure\n\nFor EVERY queried index define its actual diagonal and finite incoming mass\n\n    Ddiag_i=P_ii+h_i sum_(j>i)(P_ji+A_ji),\n    c_i=Ddiag_i-nu_i-2alpha_i\n       =sum_(j!=i)(-N_ji).                       (5.1)\n\nThese formulas include every query, and (4.6) makes them literal.\nFor another compatible skew C, the diagonal changes by\nh_i sum_(j>i)C_ji. The first and last diagonals, however, are invariant:\n\n    Ddiag_0=b(Pe-d)_0-aP_00,\n    Ddiag_l=P_ll,\n    Pe-d=2s v+2Fz-zeta.                          (5.2)\n\nThe first identity uses the exact first row of Ltri^T,\nb e^T-a e_0^T, and skew diagonals are zero. It is NOT a row-sum\napproximation. Since z_0=0 in Mechanism1,\n\n    Ddiag_0=b(2s v_0-zeta_0)-a(K_00+v_0^2).       (5.3)\n\nTo bound it and audit the endpoint rows, normalize clocks by\nxclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta and set\n\n    B=sum what_i, M=sum what_i*xclock_i,\n    M2=sum what_i*xclock_i^2, B0=what_0,\n    Hvar=M2-M^2/B,\n    fbase=B0*M/B, kbase=B0(1-B0/B),\n    Y=-(Kt)_0=(Xreq/Delta)fbase/Hvar.\n\nHere Hvar>=1/2, B<5/2, M>=1, fbase>2/5,\nHvar<=B/4<5/8, and fbase/Hvar<3. The variance upper bound follows\nfrom xclock in[0,1] and xclock^2<=xclock. Hence\n\n    Y>1/2, a K_00/Y=a*dclock*(B-B0)/M<(5/6)p,\n    -zeta_0=(theta/Delta)fbase/Hvar<12p.           (5.4)\n\nTogether with theta/Xreq>p and (2.2)--(2.4), (5.3) yields\n\n    p/4<Ddiag_0<32p,\n    c_0=Ddiag_0-ellload-2alpha_0<-1/128.          (5.5)\n\nFor the lower bound use\nb(-zeta_0)-aK_00>(b-5/6)pY>(17/60)p,\nand a v_0^2 is smaller than p/100. For the upper bound discard\nboth nonpositive square terms and use2bs v_0<p^2.\nThe last inequality uses32p<1/128. Thus the first diagonal itself\nis positive but cannot pay its incoming optimum load.\n\nThis is a uniform FAILED COLUMN of the full array. It rules out\nsimultaneous nonnegativity of the entire collection (5.1), whatever\nthe other diagonals do. It would be false to mark ALL columns as passed.\nSpecifically at least one original lambda_(j,0), 1<=j<=l, is negative.\nNo compatible skew correction changes that conclusion. Having found\nthis cut, there is no need or justification to search individual signs\nof the enormous array. The remaining tests below distinguish which\nof the proposed repairs really work.\n\n### 5.1 Last column really is repaired\n\nThe scale is at most2Xreq/Delta^2<8p, so K_ll<=w_l<12p.\nBut the new common column gives\n\n    P_ll=K_ll+v_l^2+eta b^2>21/10,\n    c_l=P_ll-2alpha_l>1/10.                      (5.6)\n\nIndeed v_l>a>2/5, eta>1-p/32 and b>7/5 imply\nv_l^2+eta b^2>53/25-(49/800)p>21/10.\nThe incoming optimum mass nu_l is zero. Thus the prior last-column\nobstruction is removed, rather than merely overlooked.\n\n### 5.2 The last adjacent-pair factor cut passes\n\nLet hgap=h_r. Since the last two rows of Ltri^T are e_l^T and\ne_r^T+hgap e_l^T,\n\n    N_lr+N_rl=2P_lr+hgap P_ll.                   (5.7)\n\nThe skew terms cancel exactly. If both off-diagonals are nonpositive,\nthe right side must be nonpositive. For the prescribed law it equals\n\n    2K_lr+hgap K_ll\n     +v_l(2v_r+hgap v_l)\n     +eta b[2E-eps*b].                          (5.8)\n\nHere K_lr<0, v_r=-a*hgap-alpha_r, and v_l<1/2. Thus\nv_l(2v_r+hgap v_l)<-(3/25)hgap<-9/40.\nThe metric term is <24p and the positive part of the common term\nis <4p. Therefore\n\n    2P_lr+hgap P_ll<-1/5.                       (5.9)\n\nThis necessary pair test passes, but does not remedy (5.5).\n\n## 6. Both endpoint row-moment screens for Mechanism1\n\nThese are row tests, distinct from the column equation (5.5). They\nare included to audit the nonzero-clock change jointly, not as a\nsubstitute for the failed column test.\n\nDefine first-row quantities\n\n    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,\n    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,\n    G0=P0-Delta*S0.\n\nNonpositive first-row off-diagonals require\n\n    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0.                   (6.1)\n\nFrom (3.2), (4.5), and the first row b e^T-a e_0^T,\n\n    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,\n    P0=b(4s+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0\n                       +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0. (6.2)\n\nFor the new z_0=0, the nonmetric part of S0 is\n2pR_0-2bs v_0+a v_0^2<7p^2. Hence (5.4) gives\n\n    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16.                        (6.3)\n\nFor the clock, put K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and\nY=Xreq/Delta+eY. Direct expansion of (6.2) gives\n\n    G0=(a/Delta)(b*theta0-4-eta Z^2)\n       +2bFZ-2pT R_0+4bs-4a v_0+2a alpha_0\n       +2bsDelta v_0-aDelta v_0^2+Epert,\n    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq.  (6.4)\n\nThe incoming load cancels exactly in this identity because\nXreq-Ttheta=b*theta0-4-eta Z^2-Delta*ellload,\nand the expansion includes the original +a nu_0=a ellload.\nNo incoming load has been suppressed.\n\nFor an auxiliary base omit only omega rho, keeping its three literal\natoms at0,1,1-rclock with weights1,1,sigma. Put\nDclock=1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2.\nUsing B=2+sigma, M=1+sigma(1-rclock), and\nM2=1+sigma(1-rclock)^2 in the displayed definitions gives exactly\n\n    Eint=(xiM*rclock/Dclock)\n       [theta(1-a*dclock)(1-2rclock)\n                         -2a Xreq*rclock*dclock].              (6.5)\n\nSince1-2p<Dclock<=1, |1/Dclock-1|<rclock,\na*dclock<p, rclock<4p, Xreq*dclock<2 and theta<3,\nthe bracket differs from theta by less than35p. Consequently\n\n    |Eint-xiM*hgap*dclock*(2+ellload)|<106p^2.    (6.6)\n\nRestoring omega rho changes B,M,M2,B0 by at most omega.\nBoth variances are >=1/2. Direct quotient subtraction bounds the\nchanges of fbase,kbase,Hvar by2omega,4omega,5omega, respectively,\nand those of fbase/Hvar,kbase/Hvar by32omega. The exact metric part\nof G0 is\n\na(Xreq/Delta)(2fbase/Hvar-kbase/Hvar)\n       +theta(1-a/Delta)fbase/Hvar-btheta.\n\nIts change is <400p^2. Each base is scaled by its own variance and\nthe same Xreq; hence this includes the change of scale.\n\nIn (6.4), replacing theta0 by2 and eta Z^2 by Zstar^2 costs\nless than42p^2. Replacing2bFZ by2b*dclock*Zstar costs less than63p^2,\nsince |p-dclock|<4p^2 and |Z-Zstar|<4p. Replacing-2pT R_0\nby-2b*dclock costs less than20p^2; use R_0=bE-a v_0 and pT=1-bp.\nThe five remaining s,v_0,alpha_0 terms total less than5p^2.\nTogether with (6.6) and the restored positive tail, this proves\n\n    |G0-dclock*Cfirst|<1000p^2,\n    Cfirst=a(2b-4-Zstar^2)+2b(Zstar-1)\n                                  +xiM*hgap*(2+ellload)\n          =Cold+b*hgap*(2-a*b*hgap),\n    Cold=a(2a-3)+xiM*hgap*(2+ellload).             (6.7)\n\nSubstitute xiM and hgap=2-eps to obtain\n\n    Cold=a(1-2a)-a^3/2+2ellload*xiM\n                                  -eps*(2+ellload)*xiM\n         >a^3/2-3eps/4>1/32.\n\nHere a>2/5 and eps<=1/6144 give4/125-3/24576>1/32.\nAlso a*b*hgap<85/72<6/5 and b*hgap>21/8. Thus the extra term\nin (6.7) exceeds21/10, so Cfirst>2. A coarse direct bound gives\n|Cfirst|<20. Therefore\n\n    p<G0<30p,\n    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<30p-1/32<0.            (6.8)\n\nEquations (6.3),(6.8) establish the entire first-row screen (6.1).\n\nFor the last row put\n\n    Slast=mu_l-P_ll,\n    Jlast=(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*P_ll,\n    Hlast=(Nt)_l-t_r*mu_l-hgap*P_ll.\n\nThese are respectively the row total, its clock relative to1, and its\nclock relative to t_r. Nonpositive off-diagonals require\nSlast<=0, Jlast<=0, Hlast>=0. Set\n\n    Alast=what_l(1-M/B)/Hvar,\n    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/Hvar.\n\nThey are positive and <3, with\n(Kt)_l=(Xreq/Delta)Alast, K_ll=(Xreq/Delta^2)Blast.\nFor the auxiliary three-atom base,\nAlast=(1-xiM+xiM*rclock)/Dclock>=1-xiM.\nRestoring omega rho changes this ratio by at most32p^2, by the same\nquotient estimate. Now expansion gives the exact formula\n\n    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast\n          +4v_l+2p(b+hgap)v_l-hgap*v_l^2-2b\n          +eta*b*(2Z-hgap*b),\n    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta]/Delta.            (6.9)\n\nBy (4.3), Cclock>2-ellload-64p and Cclock<3. Hence\nCclock*Alast>(2-ellload)(1-xiM)-100p.\nThe negative metric term has magnitude <24p. The v_l polynomial is\nincreasing for a<=v_l<1/2, because its derivative is greater than2.\nThe terms in (6.9) before the common-column contribution therefore exceed\n\n    2a-2a^2-2xiM-ellload(1-xiM)-125p\n     =a^3/4-ellload(1-xiM)-125p\n     >3/8000-125p>1/4000.\n\nThe exact incoming-load loss ellload(1-xiM) is retained.\nSince Z>3, hgap*b<17/6, eta>9/10 and b>7/5, the new common\ncontribution in (6.9) exceeds3. Thus Hlast>3.\nFinally mu_l=2p v_l+(theta/Delta)Alast<19p, whereas P_ll>21/10.\nAlso (Nt)_l=2(Kt)_l+4v_l+2eta Zb-2alpha_l<26. It follows that\n\n    Slast<-2, Jlast<26-(21/10)Delta<0, Hlast>3.   (6.10)\n\nBoth endpoint row screens really pass. The first incoming COLUMN,\nnot either endpoint row screen or the last adjacent pair, kills the law.\n\n## 7. Mechanism2: explicit chronological PSD details and their global price\n\nWe now enlarge the detail term itself, instead of changing another\nweight within one weighted-variance metric. The identified constraint\nis the first diagonal's ability to pay a column load under a small\ninitial charge. Both clock-bearing and clock-annihilating details are\nallowed below; the former change the paid column and all load moments.\nTheir price is recomputed, not assumed to vanish.\n\nOn the ORIGINAL queried indices define the literal chronological vectors\n\n    gdetail_i=e_i-e_(i+1), 0<=i<=n-2,\n    ddetail_i=h_(i+1)e_i-(h_i+h_(i+1))e_(i+1)+h_i e_(i+2),\n                                           0<=i<=n-3.\n\nThey obey\n\n    e^T gdetail_i=0, t^T gdetail_i=-h_i,\n    e^T ddetail_i=0, t^T ddetail_i=0.              (7.1)\n\nFor the latter clock identity substitute\n(t_i,t_(i+1),t_(i+2))=(t_i,t_i+h_i,t_i+h_i+h_(i+1));\neach term cancels. Thus zero sum alone does NOT imply zero clock energy.\n\nChoose this ONE explicit enlarged coefficient law:\n\n    gamma_i=Xreq/[2(n-1)h_i^2], 0<=i<=n-2,\n    tau_i=p/[1+h_i+h_(i+1)]^2, 0<=i<=n-3,\n    Hclock=sum_(i=0)^(n-2) gamma_i gdetail_i gdetail_i^T,\n    Hnull=sum_(i=0)^(n-3) tau_i ddetail_i ddetail_i^T,\n    Qdetail=K/2+Hclock+Hnull.                      (7.2)\n\nEvery vector and weight is an original-index formula. No optimizer,\nrecurrence replay, matrix-existence claim, or selected support is used.\nAll sums are finite symbolic definitions, not executed computations.\nThe entries of K/2 correspond to the positive weights w/2. Consequently\nQdetail is PSD and positive definite on e-perp. From (7.1),\n\n    Qdetail e=0,\n    t^T Hclock t=sum_i gamma_i h_i^2=Xreq/2,\n    Hnull t=0, t^T Qdetail t=Xreq.                (7.3)\n\nRetain the same terminal pool and late common column, and REPLACE\n\n    zeta by zeta_new=(theta/Xreq)Qdetail t,\n    P by Pnew=Qdetail+vv^T+eta zz^T,\n    d by dnew=zeta_new+F(v-z).\n\nUse (4.6) with Kt replaced by Qdetail t, d replaced by dnew, and\nrvec=Qdetail t+2v+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target. This again is a literal skew\ncompletion and, with (3.3), specifies EVERY original multiplier.\nEquations(7.3) give e^T zeta_new=0 and t^T zeta_new=theta, so both\nincoming-load compatibility moments remain ellload. The incoming\noptimum vector is exactly nu_target; it is not a free unpriced correction.\n\nThe complete polynomial is (3.4) with K,zeta replaced by\nQdetail,zeta_new. In particular its paid part equals\n\n    (y+(theta/(2Xreq))*t*x_0)^T Qdetail\n                   (y+(theta/(2Xreq))*t*x_0)\n        +(q-p^2-theta^2/(4Xreq))||x_0||^2.         (7.4)\n\nThis equality follows by expanding the square and using (7.3).\nNecessity of this same charge follows with scalar x_0=1,\ny=-(theta/(2Xreq))*t+c0 e, choosing c0 to cancel the common square,\nand choosing the independent g_n to cancel the terminal square.\nThus the exact global charge remains\n\n    q>=p^2+theta^2/(4Xreq),                       (7.5)\n\nwhich passes at qtar by (4.4). There is no extra hidden initial cost,\nno omitted clock energy, and no uncancelled terminal square.\n\nAll optimum signs pass for this second law as well. Hnull t=0, and\nLtri^T Kt has strictly positive entries by the argument following (4.6).\nFor Hclock t write aedge_i=gamma_i h_i>0, with aedge_(-1)=aedge_l=0.\nThe i-th coordinate of Hclock t is aedge_(i-1)-aedge_i; its suffix\nafter i is aedge_i. Therefore\n\n    (Ltri^T Hclock t)_i=aedge_(i-1)+(h_i-1)aedge_i>0\n\nfor every i, interpreting endpoints by the zero conventions. This\nuses h_i>1. Multiplication by theta/Xreq>0, addition of the half-K\nterm and 2pR proves every mu_new,i>0. The incoming and terminal signs\nare the already specified nonnegative ones.\n\nThe source data have changed explicitly:\n\n    Qdetail_00=K_00/2+gamma_0+tau_0*h_1^2,\n    zeta_new,0=zeta_0/2-theta/[2(n-1)b].           (7.6)\n\nThus this mechanism changes the first diagonal and paid source column,\nnot just a row-clock summary. Clock-free details also alter diagonals:\nthe tau_0 term in (7.6) must be included, even though Hnull t=0.\nThe full failure of this enlarged law follows from the next global\ninequality, which covers considerably more freedom than (7.2).\n\n### 7.1 A source-capacity inequality for the entire PSD-detail class\n\nFor this necessary-condition argument allow ANY symmetric PSD Qdetail\nwith Qdetail e=0; no such matrix is asserted to solve a feasibility\nproblem. Allow ANY zero-sum paid vector zeta and ANY common vector z\nwith e^T z=E. Keep v=k-alpha, e^T alpha=1-p, 0<s<p, eta=F/E,\nand the original coefficient formulas\n\n    P=Qdetail+vv^T+eta zz^T,\n    d=zeta+F(v-z), A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).\n\nThe incoming vector may now vary as well. Assume only that the full\noriginal polynomial at level q is PSD. That assumption will be used\nto derive a necessary inequality, not to posit a feasible completion.\nFor any scalar x,u choose\n\n    x_0=x,\n    y=u e_0+[x-(z_0/E)u]e,\n    g_n=-s*x+v^T y.\n\nThese are permissible original formal directions by invertibility\nof Ltri. The common and terminal squares vanish EXACTLY. Because\nQdetail e=0 and e^T zeta=0, full PSD implies, for every x,u,\n\n    Qdetail_00*u^2+zeta_0*x*u+(q-p^2)*x^2>=0.    (7.7)\n\nIn particular the following explicitly evaluated quantity is nonnegative:\n\n    Rsource=Qdetail_00+(b/a)zeta_0\n                         +(b^2/a^2)(q-p^2)>=0.   (7.8)\n\nThe first diagonal formula (5.2) holds unchanged:\n\n    N_00=b(2s v_0+2Fz_0-zeta_0)\n                    -a(Qdetail_00+v_0^2+eta z_0^2).\n\nNow expand these THREE nonnegative terms:\n\n    b^2 q/a-N_00\n       =a Rsource\n         +a(v_0-bs/a)^2\n         +a*eta*(z_0-bE/a)^2 >=0.                (7.9)\n\nTo check the constant exactly, eta E=F and\ns^2+eta E^2=p^2. The constants on the right are therefore\n(b^2/a)[q-p^2+s^2+eta E^2]=b^2 q/a;\nthe linear terms are b*zeta_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,\nand the quadratic terms are a(Qdetail_00+v_0^2+eta z_0^2).\nThis verifies (7.9) term by term without an inverse or external theorem.\n\nIf the actual finite incoming multipliers at the first query are\nnonnegative, their total is N_00-nu_0-2alpha_0>=0. Hence\n\n    nu_0+2alpha_0 <= N_00 <= b^2 q/a.             (7.10)\n\nThis bounds the source load by the ENTIRE paid initial budget.\nIt cannot be repaired by moving clock energy between details,\nusing clock-annihilating details, changing their ranks or supports,\nchanging zeta, changing z (including its clock), or changing skew\ncorrections while keeping the large source load and small q.\nIt even allows a different terminal distribution with nonnegative\nalpha_0, as long as the displayed architecture and p,s apply.\n\nFor the physical parameters, b^2/a<(17/12)^2/(2/5)<6 and qtar<p.\nThus, whenever q<=qtar,\n\n    N_00<6p<1/128,\n    N_00-(1/64)-2alpha_0<-1/128                  (7.11)\n\nif alpha_0>=0. This proves a uniform failed ORIGINAL first column\nfor the explicit enlarged law (7.2), and for the full class in this\nsubsection retaining nu_0=1/64. It is independent of all compatible\nskew corrections. Equivalently that fixed load requires q>1/384,\nwhereas qtar<p<2^(-40).\n\nFor clarity, the obstruction includes arbitrary finite nonnegative\nweights on the literal vectors gdetail_i,ddetail_i, arbitrary positive\nvariance metrics, their sums, and any other zero-sum PSD detail\nconstruction. Only necessary inequalities are claimed for this class;\nthere is no arbitrary unknown PSD object adopted as a certificate.\n\n### 7.2 Moment accounting for the surviving enlargement freedoms\n\nA general sum H=sum gamma_i gdetail_i gdetail_i^T\n+sum tau_i ddetail_i ddetail_i^T, with nonnegative scalar weights,\nhas He=0, t^T Ht=sum gamma_i h_i^2, and its second sum annihilates t.\nIf its clock energy Eclock is less than Xreq, the explicit replacement\nQdetail=(1-Eclock/Xreq)K+H has total clock energy Xreq.\nFor zeta=(theta/Xreq)Qdetail t the exact charge is again (7.5),\nand the incoming total and clock are again ellload. This describes\nthe larger parameter class covered by (7.10); (7.2) is its single\nfully fixed original-index instance used in this report.\n\nIf all details annihilate both e and t and one leaves K,zeta fixed,\nthen the optimum vectors and load moments remain unchanged, but\nN_00 decreases by a H_00, N_ll increases by H_ll, and the last-row\nclock defect changes by -hgap H_ll. The latter follows from\nH t=0, mu unchanged, and the last row of Ltri^T being e_l^T.\nThese diagonal and endpoint changes still have to be paid; null\nclock energy is not permission to ignore them.\n\nIf one changes z, alpha, or the incoming vector beyond the fixed\nlaw, the general compatibility equations are\n\n    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t,\n    V=t^T v, Z=t^T z, X=t^T Qdetail t,\n    theta=t^T zeta.\n\nThus changing nu_0 is a real global-moment change. The current\nnecessary bound requires nu_0+2alpha_0=O(p) at the target scale.\nIt does not assert that such a smaller load is sufficient, nor\nexclude a nonnegative incoming distribution supported elsewhere.\nNo attempt to choose a third coefficient law is made in this round.\n\n## 8. Scope, failed paths, literature, and resource record\n\nMechanism1 fails because its O(p) first diagonal cannot pay the\nfixed1/64 incoming optimum coefficient, despite genuine repair of\nthe last column and success of both endpoint row screens.\nMechanism2 changes the PSD detail term, pays all clock energy, and\nkeeps the exact initial charge; it still fails the uniform source cut.\nThe common cause is the global paid source capacity, not a poor\nchoice of skew completion or an untested transport existence claim.\n\nThe new report also diagnoses the earlier source-loaded laws: any\nmember of this architecture using nu_0=1/64 and q<=qtar fails (7.10),\nregardless of any previously successful endpoint row checks. {"id": "677a3071c5a3b021", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T23:27:57.072015+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4 invocation86 JOINT UNVERIFIED: complete all-dimensional exposed-segment reduction with exact orthogonality/radius payment; exact Q on all |s|<=1 or L=0; all no/one-crossing histories <=1/D; signed forcing product C_m<6sqrt(m) closes all thin segments and the stated large-radius regime. Explicit finite-T benchmark approach has exact score(1+Q)/(D+1). A legal all-m strict plateau-to-upper-wing reactivation refutes replacing future motion by an unbounded Huber trajectory. ENTIRE segment profile remains OPEN; triangle gated, original target OPEN.", "evidence": "# Exposed affine-height segments: reduction, quantitative regimes, and reactivation\n\n**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** Worker max4, project gd-exact-profile-20260926,\ninvocation86 after ended85, controller cap1. Steering: TASK.md and\nmaster_guidance bb8c7f51e22c165f; plan b3ae4588b90dfc98.\n\nNo new fact is asserted. All new mathematical assertions below are parts of\nthis single joint derivation. No fact_submit, mathematical program, numerical\nevaluation, trajectory replay, optimizer, consult, or subagent was used.\nAdministrative text operations and literature/memory retrieval were used.\n\nThe **complete exposed-segment quantitative profile remains OPEN**. This\nreport does not call a scalar recurrence or a list of cases a solution. It\ncloses several uniform regimes and gives a legal all-m obstruction to an\nattempted simplification. Mechanism1 has not achieved its decisive closure,\nso the gated triangle mechanism was not opened. The original target remains\nOPEN and no complete minimax resolution is claimed.\n\n## 1. Fixed parameters, accepted inputs, and scope\n\nFix any integer m>=1536. Throughout this report use the SAME parameters\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, S=S_m, D=1+2S, n=2^m-1.\n\nHere H_j and a,c are local schedule notation, as in the assignment; D is\nthe accepted fact's D_m. Every physical update of H_m, including its last\nleaf 1+a, is retained. There is no child reset of m or eps.\n\nAccepted fact d8b59c7d92425121 says that the original full finite-word\nsupremum W equals the supremum over finite-dimensional affine-height\npolytope envelopes with the stated vertex bound, strict exposing height,\nand nonzero initial point perpendicular to the height vector. Their\ngradient is X-P_K(X-lambda), and their unique minimizer is0. We use that\nfact only for this legality and representation scope. It does not reduce\nthe full supremum to segments and gives no quantitative rate.\n\nAccepted fact 931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the benchmark comparison and its\nconditional 799/800 reduction only after a full-class bound at17/(16D).\nNo converse to that implication is used here.\n\nThe length and mass identities needed below follow directly from the\nliteral concatenation. In particular\n\n    eta=a/(c-2), 0<eta<1,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,\n    d_j/A_j<c-2<1/2, and D<2*c^m.                       (1)\n\nIndeed c>1+sqrt(2)>2 and c(c-2)>1>beta, so0<eta<1.\nThe displayed A_j starts at1 and satisfies A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j.\nSince c^j>2^j for j>0, A_m<c^m. Also A_j>eta*c^j, proving\nthe bound on d_j/A_j, including j=0. For the numerical rational ranges\nused only in symbolic comparisons below,\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1, 0<a<1/2.               (2)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)<17/12 and eps<1/1000 give\nc<(29/12)(1001/1000)<5/2. These are uniform exact inequalities,\nnot computed horizon checks.\n\n## 2. Scalar envelope, boundaries, gradient, and minimum\n\nLet L>=0, K=[0,L]e_1 in R^2, lambda=(1,T) with T>0. Minimizing in\nY=(y,0) gives\n\n    Phi(x,z)=phi_L(x)+z^2/2,\n    phi_L(x)= min_(0<=y<=L) [y+(x-y)^2/2],\n    y(x)=clip(x-1,0,L),\n    g_L(x)=phi_L'(x)=x-y(x).                           (3)\n\nHere L denotes normalized segment length, not the advertised smoothness\nconstant. All calculations use advertised smoothness1. Multiplying Phi\nby any original smoothness constant L_orig>0 and using steps h_i/L_orig\nleaves the trajectory and the normalized W=2R score unchanged.\n\nThe minimizer y(x) follows either from the derivative 1+y-x or from the\nprojection normal. Substitution gives the complete formula\n\n    phi_L(x)=x^2/2,                  x<=1,\n             x-1/2,                1<=x<=1+L,\n             L+(x-L)^2/2,          x>=1+L.             (4)\n\nAt x=1 the adjacent values are1/2 and the adjacent derivatives are1.\nAt x=1+L the adjacent values areL+1/2 and the derivatives again are1.\nFor L=0 the two exterior formulas coincide with x^2/2 and the middle\ninterval is a point. Thus g_L is continuous and nondecreasing, with\nsuccessive slopes1,0,1. Integrating its slope on a partition at the two\nbreakpoints proves global1-Lipschitz continuity. Consequently phi_L is\nconvex, differentiable, and1-smooth. Its unique minimizer is0, its\nminimum is0, and\n\n    0<=phi_L(x)<=x^2/2                                (5)\n\nbecause y=0 is always an available trial in (3).\n\nThe projection statement retains ALL normals: for every x, the selected\ny(x) satisfies (x-1-y(x))(v-y(x))<=0 for every v in[0,L]. The vertical\ncomponent of X-lambda does not affect projection onto this horizontal\nsegment. Hence grad Phi=(g_L(x),z)=X-P_K(X-lambda) at every point,\nincluding every original query, the terminal, and0. One fixed global\nfunction is used throughout, so no separate row-feasibility oracle is\nneeded. The unique minimum of Phi is also0 at(0,0).\n\n## 3. All exposed segments in all dimensions and exact orthogonality\n\nEvery nondegenerate segment allowed by the accepted representation has\nthe form K=[0,ell]e with ell>0 and e a unit vector. In fact0 cannot be an\ninterior point of a nondegenerate segment on which a linear functional is\nstrictly positive away from0: the two opposite directions would have\nopposite heights. Thus0 is an endpoint. Write\n\n    lambda=alpha*e+w, w perpendicular to e, alpha>0,\n    X=alpha*(x*e+z), z perpendicular to e,\n    L=ell/alpha, u=w/alpha.\n\nDirect substitution into the defining minimum, with y=alpha*t, yields\n\n    Phi(X)=alpha^2*(phi_L(x)+||z||^2/2).               (6)\n\nThe normalized coordinates obey exactly the original advertised\nsmoothness1 updates\n\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_L(x_i),\n    z_(i+1)=(1-h_i)*z_i.                             (7)\n\nThe factor alpha^2 cancels in the normalized objective/radius quotient.\nInitial orthogonality is precisely\n\n    x_0+<u,z_0>=0.                                   (8)\n\nThis accounts for all dimensions and all orientations, as follows.\n\n* If u=0, (8) forces x_0=0. The horizontal coordinate stays0 and any\n  admissible nonzero initialization is purely perpendicular quadratic.\n  In dimension1 there is no such nonzero initialization.\n* If u!=0, put T=||u||. All solutions of (8) with x_0=s are\n  z_0=-(s/T^2)u+v, where v is perpendicular to u. Therefore\n  ||z_0||^2=s^2/T^2+||v||^2. The canonical two-dimensional realization\n  is v=0: lambda=(1,T), X_0=(s,-s/T).\n* If s=0, horizontal motion is identically0. Every admissible nonzero\n  z_0, including one perpendicular to u in higher dimension, has the\n  pure quadratic score.\n* For K={0}, the function is ||X||^2/2 regardless of lambda. Every\n  admissible nonzero orthogonal initialization has that same score.\n  If a particular ambient dimension and lambda admit no such point,\n  they simply contribute no instance.\n\nIn particular a one-dimensional nondegenerate interval with scalar\northogonality is not an admissible nonzero start. The extra direction\nand its norm payment cannot be omitted.\n\n## 4. The full-word quadratic factor and the exact weighted score\n\nDefine\n\n    p_j=beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j), p_0=1,\n    Q=p_m^2.\n\nFor j>=1 the SIGNED product of all quadratic factors1-h in H_j is\n-p_j. Indeed the length is odd, the leaf factor is-a, and\np_(j+1)=d_j*p_j^2, which is satisfied by the displayed formula.\nEvery nonempty prefix of H_j has product modulus at most a<1.\nTo prove this, the leaf case is a; a prefix lying in the first child\nuses the induction hypothesis, and one passing its bridge has modulus\nat most\n\n    p_j*d_j = c^(-1)*beta^(2^j) <=a\n\ntimes a prefix modulus at most1 of the last child. Empty prefixes have\nmodulus1. In particular this also proves the weaker all-prefix bound1.\n\nLet x_n(L,s) be the actual scalar output of all H_m. For s!=0 put\n\n    q(L,s)=2*phi_L(x_n(L,s))/s^2.\n\nEquations (6)-(7), the true minimum0, and the full product give exactly\n\n    score = [s^2*q(L,s)+||z_0||^2*Q]/[s^2+||z_0||^2]. (9)\n\nThis is a weighted average with the TRUE squared initial radius. The\ncanonical two-dimensional score is\n\n    q_T(L,s)=[T^2*q(L,s)+Q]/[T^2+1].                 (10)\n\nExtra dimensions only add quadratic weight. Equations (8)-(10), and\nthe s=0 cases above, show that the unrestricted exposed-segment supremum\nis exactly the supremum of q(L,s) over L>=0 and real s!=0. One direction\nuses the weighted average and the fact that L=0 already supplies Q;\nthe other uses finite T tending to infinity for each fixed scalar\ninstance. This equality is only for the segment subclass.\n\nThere is also an explicit FINITE tilt rule for a potential strict\nwitness. If q(L,s)=q_*>b, then\n\n    T^2=1+2*(b-Q)_+/(q_*-b)                          (11)\n\ngives q_T>b. This follows on multiplying by T^2+1 from\nT^2(q_*-b)>b-Q. Also\n|q_T-q_*|=|Q-q_*|/(T^2+1). A limiting scalar score alone is not\npresented as a strict admissible witness.\n\n## 5. Complete small-start and degenerate-plateau regimes\n\nFor every L>=0 and |s|<=1, all pure quadratic candidate iterates have\nabsolute value at most |s| by the prefix product bound. They therefore\nremain in x<=1, where (3) has g_L(x)=x. This verifies the candidate\ntrajectory by induction through EVERY physical step. Its terminal is\n\n    x_n=-p_m*s, q(L,s)=Q for 0<|s|<=1.               (12)\n\nFor L=0 this identity holds for every real s!=0. The zero scalar start\nis handled by Section3 and does not require dividing by s.\n\nEquation (1) also gives\n\n    D*Q <2*c^(-m)<1/4, and p_m<1/(2*sqrt(D)),         (13)\n\nbecause beta<1, c>2 and m>=1536>=3. Thus these complete regimes are\nstrictly below1/D, and (9) preserves this assertion in all dimensions.\n\n## 6. Every no-crossing or one-crossing trajectory, all L and amplitudes\n\nHere a strict crossing means consecutive nonzero iterates with opposite\nsigns; a zero hit remains0 thereafter. The argument is supplied jointly\nand actually applies to any one-dimensional convex1-smooth function\nwith unique minimizing point0 and minimum value0. We apply it only to\nthe present family.\n\nOn either fixed ray let t be the radius and G(t) the positive gradient\nmagnitude. For a ray sign sigma in{-1,1}, write f_sigma(t)=f(sigma*t)\nand G(t)=sigma*f'(sigma*t). Then G is nondecreasing,1-Lipschitz,\nG(0)=0 and G(t)<=t.\nIf q=G(t), one has G(v)<=min(v,q) for0<=v<=t, whence\n\n    2*f_sigma(t)<=2*t*q-q^2.                        (14)\n\nFor a segment of actual updates staying on that ray, with total physical\nmass T, initial radius R and final radius t, radii decrease. All queried\ngradient magnitudes are at least q=G(t), so R>=t+T*q. The identity\n\n    (t+T*q)^2-(1+2T)*(2*t*q-q^2)\n                         =(t-(T+1)*q)^2>=0\n\nproves\n\n    2*f(terminal)<=R^2/(1+2T).                     (15)\n\nThis includes intervals wholly in the outer quadratic region, intervals\nthat traverse either boundary, and arbitrary L. It is not a substitution\nof a clipped-gradient formula for g_L.\n\nSuppose there is exactly one strict crossing, at physical step h=1+d_k.\nLet P,T be the COMPLETE masses before and after that step. If its query\nhas radius y and gradient magnitude v, the no-crossing prefix gives\n|s|>=y+P*v>=(1+P)*v. Its crossing landing has radius\nz=h*v-y<=d_k*v. Applying (15) to its actual no-crossing suffix yields\n\n    q(L,s)<= d_k^2/[(1+P)^2*(1+2T)].                (16)\n\nThe chronological hierarchy pays this denominator at every possible\nphysical occurrence. Put A=A_k, d=d_k,\nN=S_m-S_(k+1)>=0 and x=P-S_k. The full adjacent H_k children imply\n\n    0<=x<=N, T=S_k+N-x,\n    (1+P)^2*(1+2T)=(A+x)^2*(2A-1+2N-2x),\n    D=4A+2d-1+2N.\n\nThe derivative of the denominator expression is\n2(A+x)(A-1+2N-3x). It has no interior minimum. Its value at N minus\nits value at0 is\n\n    N*[2A(A-1)+N*(2A-1)]>=0.\n\nHence the denominator is at least A^2*(2A-1+2N). Since d/A<1/2,\n\n    d^2*(4A+2d-1) <(A^2/4)*(5A-1)\n                            <=A^2*(2A-1),\n\nwhere the last inequality is equivalent to A>=1. Adding the2N terms\npreserves strictness since d^2<A^2. Thus (16) is strictly less than1/D.\nA trajectory with no strict crossing uses (15) on the whole word and\nhas score at most1/D. A zero hit gives zero scalar terminal value.\n\nThis closes ALL finite-L trajectories with at most one strict crossing,\nwithout prescribing where either curvature boundary is visited. It does\nnot cover arbitrary repeated crossings: even the small-start quadratic\ntrajectories in (12) cross at every positive step, so crossing counts\nmust not be silently assumed small.\n\n## 7. Complete forcing identity and uniform thin-segment bounds\n\nFor each physical query let y_i=clip(x_i-1,0,L), so0<=y_i<=L. The\nactual recurrence can be unrolled algebraically, with no branch selected\nor evaluated:\n\n    x_n=-p_m*s+sum_(i<n) w_i*y_i,\n    w_i=h_i*product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t).                  (17)\n\nLet E_j be the sum of absolute values of these forcing coefficients for\nthe complete word H_j. Literal concatenation gives E_0=0 and\n\n    E_(j+1)=(1+d_j*p_j)*E_j+(1+d_j)*p_j.\n\nThis recurrence is SOLVED here, not left as a quantitative answer. Put\n\n    C_j=product_(0<=k<j)[1+c^(-1)*beta^(2^k)].        (18)\n\nC_j in this report is the local segment forcing product, distinct from\nthe project's primitive benchmark capacities C_n. Its shared glossary\nentry is C_seg_j.\n\nUsing p_(j+1)=d_j*p_j^2 shows\n\n    E_(j+1)+p_(j+1)+1\n               =(1+d_j*p_j)*(E_j+p_j+1),\n    E_j=2*C_j-1-p_j.                                (19)\n\nThe signed sum telescopes to1- product(1-h)=1+p_j for j>=1.\nConsequently the total POSITIVE coefficient mass in (17) is C_m,\nand the total magnitude of its NEGATIVE coefficients is C_m-1-p_m.\nKeeping these signs gives, for every actual scalar trajectory,\n\n    -p_m*s-L*(C_m-1-p_m) <=x_n<= -p_m*s+L*C_m,\n    |x_n|<=p_m*|s|+L*C_m.                            (20)\n\nThe y_i were not independently optimized to claim realizability: (20)\nis only an upper bound on the actual coupled sequence.\n\nThe product (18) has the following completely evaluated uniform bound:\n\n    C_m<6*sqrt(m).                                   (21)\n\nTo prove it take K=ceil(log_2(4m)). Since2^m>=4m for m>=4, K<=m.\nAlso2^K<8m. For k<K, each factor in (18) is at most1+1/c<sqrt(2),\nso their product is less than sqrt(8m). For k=K+t,\n\n    c^(-1)*beta^(2^k) <(1/2)*exp(-2^t),\n\nusing log(1-eps)<=-eps and2^K>=4m. The tail product is at most\nexp((1/2)*sum_(t>=0)exp(-2^t)). Since2^t>=t+1, the sum is at most\n1/(exp(1)-1)<1. Its exponential is therefore less than exp(1/2)<2.\nThese elementary exponential bounds follow for instance from the series\n2<exp(1)<3<4. The full product is less than2*sqrt(8m)<6*sqrt(m).\nIf the tail is empty the same bound holds.\n\nCombining (5), (12), and (20) proves the all-start bound\n\n    sup_(s!=0) q(L,s) <= [p_m+L*C_m]^2.              (22)\n\nFor |s|>=1 use (20) divided by |s|; for |s|<1 use (12).\nThis is a quantitative bound for EVERY L, but it is not the desired\nprofile when L is large. In particular it closes the entire thin-segment\nregime\n\n    0<=L<=1/(12*sqrt(m*D)):\n                 sup_(s!=0) q(L,s)<1/D.             (23)\n\nIndeed (21) bounds L*C_m by1/(2sqrt(D)), and (13) gives the other\nstrict half. Equations (9) and (13) transfer (23) to all exposed-segment\ndimensions with this normalized length.\n\nFor any L, the additional complete large-radius regime\n\n    |s|>=1 and |s|>=2*L*C_m*sqrt(D)                 (24)\n\nalso has q(L,s)<1/D. One may replace the second threshold by the\nexplicit larger threshold12*L*sqrt(m*D). These conclusions allow L\nand s to depend arbitrarily on the enclosing m.\n\n## 8. Exact benchmark approach with a finite orthogonal tilt\n\nThe segment class is not uniformly below the scalar Huber benchmark.\nFor each enclosing m choose\n\n    L=2S, s=D, T=sqrt(D),\n    K=[0,2S]e_1, lambda=(1,sqrt(D)), X_0=(D,-sqrt(D)).\n\nEvery scalar query and the terminal lie in[1,1+L]. In fact at prefix\nmass P_i the scalar coordinate is D-P_i, which lies in[S+1,D].\nThus the entire horizontal run is linear with gradient1,\n\n    x_n=S+1, 2*phi_L(x_n)=D, q(L,s)=1/D.\n\nInitial orthogonality is D-sqrt(D)*sqrt(D)=0 and the true initial\nradius squared is D^2+D. The vertical terminal is p_m*sqrt(D), with\nthe sign obtained from z_0=-sqrt(D) and full product-p_m. Therefore\n\n    2*Phi(X_n)/||X_0||^2=(1+Q)/(D+1)<1/D.            (25)\n\nThe relative deficit from1/D is (1-DQ)/(D+1), a positive number\nless than1/(D+1). More generally increasing finite T in (10) makes\nthis score arbitrarily close to1/D. This is a legal lower approach\nfor the segment supremum, not a strict improvement over1/D or17/(16D).\n\n## 9. Actual all-m upper-wing reactivation after a strict plateau query\n\nThis construction refutes the proposed shortcut:\n\"After an actual iterate enters the finite plateau, replace all future\ngradients by the one-sided unbounded Huber gradient min(x,1).\"\nThe static finite plateau can be left through its upper boundary again.\nIt does not refute the desired segment objective bound.\n\nFor every enclosing m>=1536 choose explicitly\n\n    L=1/10, y=1+L/2=21/20,\n    M=a*(1+beta)+a^2*beta*(1+d_2)\n                      +a^3*beta*d_2*(1+beta),\n    r=(y+L*M)/p_3, s=-r,\n    T=1, lambda=(1,1), X_0=(-r,r).                  (26)\n\nAll quantities are finite and positive, and this is one legal segment\ninstance with initial squared radius2r^2. The scalar first seven steps\nare the literal H_3 prefix of H_m. Its exact queried coordinates can be\nspecified in reverse order solely to prove the explicit forward law:\n\n    x_7=y, x_6=-y/a,\n    x_5=L+(L+y/a)/beta, x_4=-x_5/a,\n    x_3=L+(L+x_5/a)/d_2, x_2=-x_3/a,\n    x_1=L+(L+x_3/a)/beta, x_0=-x_1/a.                (27)\n\nSubstitution or multiplication of the three negative forcing\ncoefficients gives x_1/a=(y+L*M)/p_3=r, so (27) agrees exactly with\nthe completely explicit initial choice (26). Each negative query uses\ngradient x. Every positive query among x_1,x_3,x_5 is STRICTLY above\n1+L, hence uses gradient x-L:\n\n* x_5>L+y/a>2, because y>1, a<1/2 and beta<1.\n* a*d_2<5/4 and x_5>2 give\n  x_3>L+x_5/(a*d_2)>L+8/5>L+1.\n* x_3>1, a<1/2 and beta<1 give x_1>L+2>L+1.\n\nThese guards verify every update in (27), without a feasibility oracle.\nThe next actual query x_7=y lies STRICTLY inside[1,1+L]. If zeta_i>=0, its diagonal plus\npositive suffix expression is positive as well. Thus(3.2) proves mu_i>0\nfor EVERY queried index. All terminal multipliers and the assigned nu\nare nonnegative. This argument concerns optimum signs only.\n\n## 6. Law I: exactly zero incoming load\n\nFreeze the first law as follows:\n\n    nu=0, theta=theta0=2-6p,\n    Xreq=theta0/p-4-eta Z^2,\n    xiM=a(1-a)-a^3/8, sigma=xiM/(1-xiM),\n    what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+p^2*rho.\n\nUse(5.3),(5.4),(4.2),(4.3) for every other coefficient, with C=0.\nSince0<xiM<1/4, one has0<sigma<1/3 and every what_i>0. The unit\nsource and last atoms imply Vbase>=Delta^2/2: for any scalar c0,\n(1-c0)^2+(T-c0)^2>=Delta^2/2, and the other terms are nonnegative.\nThe new zero-load estimates are\n\n    2/p-26<Xreq<2/p,\n    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p>0,\n    1<Xreq/Delta<2, |Xreq/Delta-2|<32p,\n    1<theta0<2, theta0/Xreq>p.                         (6.1)\n\nFor example Xreq=2/p-10-eta Z^2 and eta Z^2<16. Also\nXreq-2Delta=2b-8-eta Z^2<0. Finally\n\n    theta0-pXreq=p(4+eta Z^2)>0.\n\nThere is no residual1/64 in either incoming moment: equations(5.1)\ngive e^T nu=t^T nu=0 exactly, and(5.4) produces nu_i=0 entrywise.\nThe paid charge obeys\n\n    J=theta0^2/(4Xreq)<(65/128)p,\n    qmin=p^2+J<(66/128)p<(17/32)p<qtar.                (6.2)\n\nThe first inequality follows from Xreq>(1023/1024)theta0/p and\n(1024/1023)/2<65/128; p<1/128 proves the next one. Thus the entire\noriginal-space polynomial(4.5) is PSD at qtar.\n\n### 6.1 Every column total, including the failed penultimate column\n\nTo give every diagonal and column without a matrix-existence placeholder,\nuse the suffix sums\n\n    Wtail_i=sum_(u>i)w_u, Dtail_i=sum_(u>i)d_u,\n    Ftail_i=sum_(u>i)fvec_u, Ctail_i=sum_(u>i)cvec_u.\n\nFor ALL i=0,...,l the canonical diagonal and finite incoming mass are\n\n    Ddiag_i=N_ii\n       =w_i[1-(w_i+h_i Wtail_i)/Ww]+v_i R_i\n        +eta*z_i*(Ltri^T z)_i\n        +h_i[(Dtail_i-(l-i)d_i)/n\n             +(cvec_i Ftail_i-fvec_i Ctail_i)/Ve],\n    c_i=Ddiag_i-nu_i-2alpha_i\n          =sum_(u!=i, u<n)lambda_(u,i).                (6.3)\n\nThis includes all queried columns and their actual terminal loads.\nFor a correction C the diagonal changes by h_i sum_(u>i)C_ui.\nThe first and last diagonals are invariant under such corrections:\n\n    Ddiag_0=b(Pe-d)_0-aP_00\n           =b(2s v_0-zeta_0)-a(K_00+v_0^2),\n    Ddiag_l=P_ll.                                    (6.4)\n\nHere the first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T and z_0=0.\n\nFor explicit source bounds normalize xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta and put\n\n    B=sum what_i, M=sum what_i*xclock_i,\n    M2=sum what_i*xclock_i^2, B0=what_0,\n    Hvar=M2-M^2/B,\n    fbase=B0*M/B, kbase=B0(1-B0/B),\n    Y=-(Kt)_0=(Xreq/Delta)*fbase/Hvar.\n\nOne has Hvar>=1/2, B<5/2, M>=1, fbase>2/5,\nHvar<=B/4<5/8 and fbase/Hvar<3. The upper variance bound uses\nxclock in[0,1] and xclock^2<=xclock. Hence\n\n    Y>1/2,\n    aK_00/Y=a*dclock*(B-B0)/M<(5/6)p,\n    -zeta_0=(theta0/Delta)*fbase/Hvar<12p.              (6.5)\n\nFor the middle bound B-B0<3/2, M>=1 and(2.1) suffice.\nUsing theta0/Xreq>p in(6.4) gives\n\n    p/4<Ddiag_0<32p,   c_0=Ddiag_0-2alpha_0>p/8.       (6.6)\n\nFor the lower bound the positive metric difference is greater than\n(b-5/6)pY>17p/60, and the subtracted a v_0^2 is less than p/100.\nThe source terminal mass2alpha_0<p^2/32 cannot destroy the margin.\nThus the old constant-load shortage really has disappeared.\n\nFor reference the whole paid source-capacity identity also holds.\nDefine Rsource=K_00+(b/a)zeta_0+(b^2/a^2)(q-p^2). Setting\nx_0=x, y=u e_0+[x-(z_0/E)u]e and g_n=-s*x+v^T y in(4.4) shows\nK_00*u^2+zeta_0*x*u+(q-p^2)*x^2>=0 when the full form is PSD, so\nRsource>=0. Expansion gives\n\n    b^2*q/a-N_00\n       =a Rsource+a(v_0-bs/a)^2+a eta(z_0-bE/a)^2>=0.  (6.7)\n\nThe constants use s^2+eta E^2=p^2. Thus N_00<=b^2 q/a, which is\ncompatible with(6.6), since nu_0=0 rather than a fixed positive load.\n\nThe metric scale is less than8p, so K_ll<=w_l<12p. Since\nv_l>a>2/5 and eta>1-p/32,\n\n    P_ll=K_ll+v_l^2+eta b^2>21/10,\n    c_l=P_ll-2alpha_l>1/10.                           (6.8)\n\nThe last adjacent pair also passes its necessary symmetric-factor test:\n\n    N_lr+N_rl=2P_lr+hgap P_ll\n       =2K_lr+hgap K_ll+v_l(2v_r+hgap v_l)\n                    +eta*b*(2E-eps*b)<-1/5.          (6.9)\n\nHere v_r=-a*hgap-alpha_r, v_l<1/2, so the v term is less than\n-(3/25)hgap<-9/40. The positive parts of the metric and common terms\nare less than24p and4p. This does not assert the separate two signs.\n\nIn fact the canonical completion fails at the penultimate query.\nFor i=r,l, w_i<12p, |(Kt)_i|<12, |zeta_i|<24p and |d_i|<28p.\nAlso |rvec_i|<24, tmean<1/p, |d^T t|<3, and hence |fvec_i|<55.\nAs |cvec_i|<=Delta and Ve>=Delta^2/2, formula(5.4) yields\n\n    |A_lr|<400p.                                     (6.10)\n\nAll bounds use only the two endpoint clocks, not an assumption that\nan exponentially long array was evaluated. Since R_r>0, v_r<0 and\nz_r=E-b, equations(4.3) give\n\n    N_rr=K_rr+hgap K_lr+v_r R_r\n                 +eta(E-b)(E+beta*b)+hgap A_lr<-3/2,\n    N_rl=K_rl+hgap K_ll+v_l R_r\n                 +eta*b*(E+beta*b)-A_lr>3/2.         (6.11)\n\nIndeed K_rr+hgap K_lr<=w_r<12p and K_rl>=-w_l>-12p;\neta(b-E)(E+beta*b)>8/5 and eta*b*(E+beta*b)>8/5 by(3.3).\nThe remaining error is bounded by812p in the first line and412p\nin the second. Consequently c_r=N_rr-2alpha_r<-3/2 and the literal\noriginal entry lambda_(r,l)=-N_rl<-3/2.\n\nThis is an explicit failed OTHER column as well as an explicit failed\nentry. Formula(6.3) specifies every remaining column. They are not all\ncertified nonnegative; a complete all-column passing claim is false.\nThe next section proves failure for every skew completion, so no\nexhaustive classification of the remaining canonical columns is needed.\n\n### 6.2 Both endpoint row-moment screens, rederived for nu=0\n\nLet\n\n    S0=sum_(u>0)N_0u,\n    P0=sum_(u>0)(t_u-1)N_0u, G0=P0-Delta*S0.\n\nNonpositive first-row off-diagonals require\nS0<=0 and Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0. Directly from(5.1),(6.4), with nu_0=0,\n\n    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0,\n    P0=b(4s+2FZ-theta0)-2a(Pt)_0+2a alpha_0-mu_0.\n\nThe metric part of S0 is less than-p/12 by(6.5); the rest is less\nthan7p^2. Hence S0<-p/16. The detailed finite clock calculation in\nAppendix A proves\n\n    p<G0<30p,\n    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<30p-1/32<0.                 (6.12)\n\nThus this ENTIRE first-row moment screen passes, notwithstanding(6.11).\n\nFor the last row define\n\n    Slast=mu_l-P_ll,\n    Jlast=(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*P_ll,\n    Hlast=(Nt)_l-t_r*mu_l-hgap*P_ll.\n\nNonpositive off-diagonals require Slast<=0, Jlast<=0, Hlast>=0.\nLet\n\n    Alast=what_l(1-M/B)/Hvar,\n    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/Hvar.\n\nBoth are positive and less than3. Formula(5.1) gives exactly\n\n    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast\n            +4v_l+2p(b+hgap)v_l-hgap*v_l^2-2b\n            +eta*b*(2Z-hgap*b),\n    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta0]/Delta.                (6.13)\n\nFor the three-atom base without p^2*rho,\nAlast=(1-xiM+xiM*rclock)/(1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2)>=1-xiM.\nRestoring the positive tail changes this ratio by at most32p^2;\nAppendix A supplies the quotient estimate. Also\n2-64p<Cclock<3. The metric negative term is bounded by24p.\nThe v_l polynomial is increasing on[a,1/2], with derivative greater\nthan2. The terms preceding the common-column term in(6.13) therefore\nexceed\n\n    2a-2a^2-2xiM-125p=a^3/4-125p>0.\n\nThere is no incoming-load loss in this zero-load formula. By(3.3),\nthe common-column term is greater than3. Furthermore mu_l<19p,\nP_ll>21/10 and (Nt)_l<26. It follows that\n\n    Slast<-2, Jlast<26-(21/10)Delta<0, Hlast>3.        (6.14)\n\nBoth endpoint screens really pass. They do not imply sign feasibility.\n\n## 7. An exact final-three-query cut for every skew completion\n\nThis part is a direct finite-sign argument in the original coefficient\narray. It does not require the canonical formula or a flow-existence\noracle. Keep j=l-2,r=l-1 and the physical steps(b,hgap,b).\nThe last rows of Ltri^T imply, for EVERY skew A,\n\n    N_lr+N_rl=2P_rl+hgap P_ll,\n    N_jl+N_lj=2P_jl+b N_rl-b*beta*P_ll.                (7.1)\n\nFor the second identity, N_lj=P_lj+A_lj and\nN_jl=P_jl+A_jl+b(P_rl+A_rl+P_ll); substitute\nP_rl+A_rl=N_rl-hgap P_ll and hgap-1=beta.\nThus no untested symmetry or omission of a queried index is involved.\n\nThe exact row and column sums at l give\n\n    mu_l+nu_l+2alpha_l-2P_ll\n       =N_lj+N_jl+N_lr+N_rl\n                       +sum_(i<j)(N_li+N_il).\n\nInsert(7.1) and use2+hgap-b*beta=3-a*beta. With F3 as in Section1,\nthis proves the exact identity\n\n    F3=-b N_rl-sum_(i<j)(N_li+N_il)\n       =b*lambda_(r,l)+sum_(i<j)[lambda_(l,i)+lambda_(i,l)].\n\nIf every finite coefficient is nonnegative, F3>=0. This necessity\nholds regardless of the signs or values of the other canonical columns.\n\nNow keep the fixed terminal and common columns of Section3, and allow\nany weighted-variance K. Since k_j=b*a*beta and k_r=-a*hgap,\n\n    v_j+v_r=a(a*beta-1)-alpha_j-alpha_r\n                                      <=a(a*beta-1).\n\nThe common part in F3 is\neta[(1-a*beta)b^2+2bE]; the bracket is positive, so it is at most the\nsame bracket without eta. Off-diagonal entries of K are negative.\nAssuming only the necessary optimum signs mu_l,nu_l>=0, we obtain\n\n    F3<=G(v_l)+3K_ll+2bE,\n    G(v)=2a(a*beta-1)v+(3-a*beta)v^2\n                               +2v-2b+(1-a*beta)b^2.\n\nFor a<=v<=1/2 the derivative of G is positive: it is\n2a(a*beta-1)+2(3-a*beta)v+2>2-2a>0. Since v_l<1/2,\n\n    G(v_l)<G(1/2)\n       =g(a,beta):=3/4-a+a^2-a*beta*(5/4+a+a^2).\n\nBoth partial derivatives of g are strictly negative for\n2/5<=a<1/2 and beta>0. In fact\npartial_a g=-1+2a-beta*(5/4+2a+3a^2)<0 and\npartial_beta g=-a*(5/4+a+a^2)<0. Therefore\n\n    G(v_l)<g(2/5,99/100)=-5169/25000<-1/5.\n\nAlso2bE<4p. This proves the useful exact-scope estimate\n\n    F3<-1/5+3K_ll+4p.                                (7.2)\n\nFor Law I, K_ll<12p, so F3<-1/5+40p<-1/6. This uniformly excludes\nEVERY compatible completion of Law I for every SAME enclosing m>=1536.\nIn particular repairing the isolated coefficient(6.11) cannot repair\nall the other coefficients appearing in(1.1).\n\n### 7.1 Literal clock-neutral four-index circulations and their effects\n\nNo circulation is needed to prove the obstruction. To make the surviving\nskew freedom concrete, take four distinct queried indices i0<i1<i2<i3,\nwrite t_a=t_(ia) just in this paragraph, and define original-index columns\n\n    u=(t_1-t_2)e_(i0)+(t_2-t_0)e_(i1)+(t_0-t_1)e_(i2),\n    vcir=(t_1-t_3)e_(i0)+(t_3-t_0)e_(i1)+(t_0-t_1)e_(i3),\n    C=kappa*(u*vcir^T-vcir*u^T), kappa any real scalar.\n\nSumming each column gives zero. Taking its clock inner product gives\n(t_1-t_2)t_0+(t_2-t_0)t_1+(t_0-t_1)t_2=0, and the identical formula\nwith2 replaced by3. Consequently C^T=-C, Ce=Ct=0, and C is supported\non these four original queried indices. Its EXACT array effect is\n\n    DeltaN_iu=kappa[(Ltri^T u)_i*vcir_u\n                                 -(Ltri^T vcir)_i*u_u].\n\nThus DeltaN e=0, (DeltaN)^T e=0 and DeltaN t=0. It preserves mu,nu,\nall row clocks, P, the charge, and F3. Its source and last diagonal\neffects are zero, using the first and last rows of Ltri^T. It can move\nindividual signs but cannot alter the negative total(1.1).\n\nThese formulas can generate the entire neutral skew space using fixed\nanchors i0=0,i1=l. More explicitly, for each interior index i set\nq_i=e_i-[(T-t_i)/Delta]e_0-[(t_i-1)/Delta]e_l. Each q_i annihilates e\nand t and has its unique interior coordinate1. For any neutral skew C,\n\n    C=sum_(0<i<u<l) C_iu*(q_i q_u^T-q_u q_i^T).\n\nThe right side has the same interior entries and annihilates e,t.\nTheir difference has all interior/interior entries zero; its interior\nrows then have only columns0,l, which must vanish by the two distinct\nclock constraints. Its remaining two-by-two skew block also vanishes.\nThis proves the identity without an existence oracle. Every summand\nis a four-index original-coordinate formula. For three indices the\nspace annihilating e,t is one-dimensional, and a skew map on that\nspace is zero; therefore no nonzero three-index skew has both kernels.\nNone of these formulas is presented as a successful sign repair.\n\n## 8. Law II: late incoming mass and one polynomial tail weight\n\nOnly AFTER the decisive all-completion result in Section7, freeze this\nsecond and final law:\n\n    ellload=1/64, nu=ellload*e_r,\n    omega=m^(-2), what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega*rho,\n    theta=theta0+ellload,\n    Xreq=theta0/p-4-eta Z^2+ellload*t_r.\n\nKeep the same alpha,z and sigma. Use(5.3),(5.4),(4.2),(4.3) literally,\nincluding-nu in rvec. This is a genuinely different paid allocation\nand an explicit polynomial, rather than exponentially tiny, positive\ntail metric. There is no third law.\n\nThe incoming moments are NOW\n\n    e^T nu=ellload, t^T nu=ellload*t_r,\n\nexactly. In particular, inserting a bare ellload instead of\nellload*t_r into Xreq would be wrong. As t_r=1/p-b-hgap,\n\n    Xreq=theta/p-Gamma,\n    Gamma=4+eta Z^2+ellload*(b+hgap), 4<Gamma<21.\n\nThus1<theta<129/64, Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta/p>0 and\nXreq<theta/p<9/(4p). The paid vector has both moments in(5.3), and\n\n    J=theta^2/(4Xreq)<(516/1023)p<(65/128)p,\n    p^2+J<(66/128)p<qtar.                             (8.1)\n\nThe factor516/1023 is exactly (129/64)*(1024/1023)/4.\nEvery optimum coefficient still passes by Section5. The full original\npolynomial(4.5), with the new metric and paid vector, is PSD at qtar.\nNo order-p term was substituted for a constant or unweighted mass.\n\nThe base retains the two unit endpoint atoms, so Vbase>=Delta^2/2.\nSince0<omega<1,\n\n    Xreq/Vbase<8p, w_l<16p, K_ll<16p.\n\nSection7 therefore gives F3<-1/5+52p<-1/6 for every compatible skew.\nThis disposes of the full second law at a finite original-coefficient\ncut. Endpoint success is not asserted for this modified metric: the\np^2-tail perturbation estimates in Section6 are not silently reused\nwith omega=m^(-2). Equations(5.1),(6.3) remain the literal row, column\nand clock formulas with the new nu,w; regardless of their other signs,\n(1.1) prevents simultaneous finite-sign feasibility.\n\n### 8.1 Scope beyond the two literal laws\n\nThis is a necessary-condition consequence, not a third design. Retain\nthe terminal pool and common column of Section3. Let\nwhat=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega*rho with0<omega<=1 (even any sigma>=0),\nand let w be a positive scalar multiple of this base. Allow ANY\nnonnegative incoming vector nu, ANY zero-sum paid vector zeta, and\nANY compatible skew. Suppose the complete original polynomial is PSD\nat some q<=qtar and all optimum signs are nonnegative.\n\nSet Mnu=e^T nu, Cnu=t^T nu, theta=t^T zeta, X=t^T Kt>0.\nThe same exact moment identities imply\n\n    theta=theta0+Mnu>0,\n    X=theta0/p-4-eta Z^2+Cnu,\n    Cnu<=T*Mnu=(1/p-b)Mnu,\n    X<theta/p.                                      (8.2)\n\nFrom(4.5) and weighted Cauchy--Schwarz,\n\n    theta^2=[sum_i zeta_i(t_i-tbase)]^2\n        <=[sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i]*[sum_i w_i(t_i-tbase)^2]\n        =4JX,\n    q>=p^2+J>=theta^2/(4X)>theta*p/4.\n\nSince qtar=17p/[16(2-p)]<9p/16, theta<9/4 and X<9/(4p).\nThe metric scale is X/Vbase, so again K_ll<=w_l<16p.\nEquation(7.2) gives F3<-1/6, contradicting(1.1) if every original\nfinite coefficient is nonnegative.\n\nThus arbitrary reallocation of incoming mass, including moving it\nto very late clocks, cannot fix THIS fixed terminal/common architecture\nwith a bounded additive tail in an endpoint-dominated variance metric.\nThis argument even permits a non-minimizing paid vector; its extra\ncharge cannot help the displayed necessary inequalities.\n\nMore precisely, finite-sign feasibility would require from(7.2)\n\n    K_ll>1/15-(4/3)p.\n\nFor a general PSD detail matrix the cut(1.1) remains exact, but the\nweighted-variance simplification2K_jl+2K_rl<=0 may fail. The precise\nremaining local requirement is the unsimplified nonnegative F3, with\n2K_jl+2K_rl+(3-a*beta)K_ll retained. A different common column or\nterminal pool also changes the scalar obstruction. None of those\nsurviving freedoms is ruled out, solved, or instantiated as a third law.\n\n## Appendix A. Finite first-endpoint clock calculation for Law I\n\nThis appendix applies ONLY to the zero-load,p^2-tail first law.\nSet K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and Y=Xreq/Delta+eY. Since z_0=0,\n(Pt)_0=-Y+2v_0 and mu_0=2pR_0-a zeta_0. Expanding the formulas\nin Section6.2 gives exactly\n\n    G0=(a/Delta)(b*theta0-4-eta Z^2)\n         +2bFZ-2pT R_0+4bs-4a v_0+2a alpha_0\n         +2bsDelta v_0-aDelta v_0^2+Epert,\n    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta0(Delta-a)eY/Xreq.       (A.1)\n\nIn particular Xreq-Ttheta0=b*theta0-4-eta Z^2. No source incoming\nload, weighted or unweighted, occurs in this identity.\n\nTemporarily omit only p^2*rho from what. The remaining atoms are at\nnormalized clocks0,1,1-rclock with weights1,1,sigma. Let\nDclock=1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2. Substituting B=2+sigma,\nM=1+sigma(1-rclock), M2=1+sigma(1-rclock)^2 yields\n\n    fbase/Hvar=(1-xiM*rclock)/Dclock,\n    kbase/Hvar=1/Dclock,\n    Epert=Eint=(xiM*rclock/Dclock)\n         [theta0(1-a*dclock)(1-2rclock)\n                            -2a Xreq*rclock*dclock]. (A.2)\n\nThese are exact scalar identities. The first two follow from\nB*Hvar=(1+sigma)Dclock. One has1-2p<Dclock<=1,\n|1/Dclock-1|<rclock, a*dclock<p, rclock<4p,\nXreq*dclock<2 and theta0<2. Subtracting theta0 from the bracket in\n(A.2) bounds its absolute error by35p. Consequently\n\n    |Eint-2xiM*hgap*dclock|<106p^2.                  (A.3)\n\nRestoring p^2*rho changes each of B,M,M2,B0 by at most p^2.\nBoth variances are at least1/2. Direct quotient subtraction bounds\nthe changes of fbase,kbase,Hvar by2p^2,4p^2,5p^2, and hence the\nchanges of fbase/Hvar and kbase/Hvar by32p^2. For clarity, in\nHvar=M2-M^2/B the three contributions are bounded by p^2,\n(2M p^2+p^4)/B, and M^2 p^2/(B_old B_new); B>=2 and M<=B bound\ntheir sum by5p^2. The other two numerator bounds follow by the same\ndisplayed product/quotient subtraction, with B0_old=1.\nThe exact metric part of G0 is\n\n    a(Xreq/Delta)(2fbase/Hvar-kbase/Hvar)\n                   +theta0(1-a/Delta)fbase/Hvar-b*theta0.\n\nIts change is less than400p^2. Each base uses its own variance\nnormalization, so this includes the change in w. The same estimate\nbounds the change in Alast in Section6.2 by32p^2.\n\nIn(A.1), replacing theta0 by2 and eta Z^2 by Zstar^2 costs less\nthan42p^2. Replacing2bFZ by2b*dclock*Zstar costs less than63p^2:\nuse |p-dclock|<4p^2 and |Z-Zstar|<4p. Replacing-2pT R_0\nby-2b*dclock costs less than20p^2, using R_0=bE-a v_0 and\npT=1-bp. The remaining five terms involving s,v_0,alpha_0 have total\nabsolute value less than5p^2 by(2.2),(3.1). Together with(A.3),\n\n    |G0-dclock*Cfirst|<1000p^2,\n    Cfirst=a(2b-4-Zstar^2)+2b(Zstar-1)+2xiM*hgap\n          =Cold+b*hgap*(2-a*b*hgap),\n    Cold=a(2a-3)+2xiM*hgap\n         =a(1-2a)-a^3/2-2eps*xiM.                    (A.4)\n\nUsing1-2a>a^2, a>2/5, xiM<1/4, eps<=1/6144 gives\nCold>a^3/2-eps/2>1/32. Also a*b*hgap<85/72<6/5 and\nb*hgap>21/8, so the extra term exceeds21/10. Hence Cfirst>2;\na direct triangle bound in the first expression gives |Cfirst|<20.\nSince p<dclock<4p/3 and p<2^(-40), this proves p<G0<30p as claimed.\n\n## 9. Failure synthesis, literature and resource record\n\nLaw I does what the latest guidance intended at the source: it removes\nthe impossible constant incoming load and recomputes both moments and\nthe full initial price. The fixed late common column still pays the\nlast diagonal and both endpoint moments. It nevertheless places an\nunpayable joint burden on original edges involving the final three\nqueried indices. The canonical negative penultimate column is one\nmanifestation; the exact positive weighted sum(1.1) is the reason no\nbalanced completion can repair it.\n\nLaw II changes the actual incoming clock and uses a polynomial tail\nweight. Its global charge and both incoming moments are correct, but\nits last metric diagonal stays O(p), and the same finite cut fails.\nSection8.1 proves why varying the incoming allocation within this\nbounded-tail architecture cannot change that conclusion. Every new assertion below\nbelongs to this ONE joint candidate proof, not to the accepted fact graph.\n\n## Verdict and scope\n\nThe literal same-m H2=(A,B,A) has an explicit candidate full original-space\nprimitive certificate: eight nonnegative ordered interpolation multipliers\nand three positive adjacent-gradient squares. It proves, within this joint\ndraft, V_H2 >= (epsilon^2/2) sum_(i=0)^2 ||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.\nNo gradient is identified or deleted. This closes the previously unproved\nlow-d H2 stress test analytically, subject to the unavailable verifier gate.\n\nFor every compact convex body D containing0, every start, and every d>=1,\nthe same candidate gives U_d(T_H2 x)<=U_(d+2S2)(x), with the explicit\nreserve above divided by d+2S2, plus further nonnegative terms. The\nprimitive certificate also gives the full smooth-convex three-step result\nW_3(H2)=1/(1+2S2). This is a finite-block assertion, not an asymptotic result.\n\nThe full all-depth U law remains OPEN. A general bridge identity displays\nthe precise signed defect, which turns negative beginning at the H3 join.\nIts magnitude is bounded by 27 epsilon C_j^2 times an actual gradient-norm\ndrop. The initial-maximization remainder is retained exactly. It pays the\ndefect on a complete, fixed-interval cap/cap family with U ratio below1/4,\neven though both child primitive reserves vanish. No uniform payment for\nall bodies and starts is proved, and no uniform 1+C/m loss is proved.\n\nOne substantive mechanism is used: primitive energy with its actual\nprojection-normal and maximization reserves. The optional second H2\nmechanism is not opened because the first H2 certificate succeeds.\nThe original unrestricted minimax target is OPEN. No new fact_id exists.\n\n## 1. Parameters, accepted inputs, and original rows\n\nFix ONE integer m>=1536 and set\n\n epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, s=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j.\n\nThe symbol s in this report is the constant sqrt(2)-1, not a word mass.\nFor the physical H2 use A=1+a, B=1+beta, S2=2A+B.\nFor a general child use C_j=1+S_j and k_j=a*c^j.\nAll children inherit the SAME enclosing m. No function or radius resets.\n\nFor a convex differentiable 1-smooth function f, actual GD points satisfy\nx_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i). Retain the actual\nterminal gradient as a mathematical sample, even though no extra step is\ntaken there. Use the accepted residual orientation\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.       (1.1)\n\nThis orientation and its necessity are in accepted1252b8823bb5a290,\nproof paragraph1, which was read. For a word h with mass S define\n\n V_h=2 sum_(i<N) h_i(f_i-f_N)\n      +sum_(i<N) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S(S+1)||g_N||^2. (1.2)\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750, Sections10-11, supplies this definition, the\nfull original-space certificate criterion, the exact-root primitive join,\nand the dominant identity J_(h,(h,S+1))=V_h. Those applicable sections,\nincluding the coefficient audits and original-coordinate qualifications,\nwere read completely. Their exact-root join is NOT treated as a theorem\nabout shortening physical steps. The missing variant is derived below.\n\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201 has only the previously assigned scalar comparison\nand conditional negative-reduction scope. No rate is inferred from the\naffine-height representation d8b59c7d92425121. This report needs neither\nas a new mathematical premise. The original normalization remains W=2R.\n\n## 2. Exact elementary row identities\n\nFor one physical step x_v=x_u-h g_u, direct substitution in (1.1) gives\n\n V_(h)=-2Q_uv-2(1+h)Q_vu+(2-h^2)||g_u-g_v||^2.       (2.1)\n\nTo check every coefficient, the two rows are\n\n Q_uv=f_u-f_v+(1/2-h)||g_u||^2\n             -<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2,\n Q_vu=f_v-f_u+||g_u||^2/2\n             +(h-1)<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2.\n\nAdding2Q_uv+2(1+h)Q_vu to V_(h) cancels both function values and\nleaves diagonal coefficients2-h^2 and mixed coefficient-2(2-h^2).\nConsequently the leaf is primitive for0<=h<=sqrt(2), without a product\nequality assumption.\n\nFor h>0, adding the same two interpolation rows gives\nQ_uv+Q_vu=||g_u-g_v||^2-h<g_u,g_u-g_v>. Therefore\n\n ||g_u||^2-||g_v||^2\n   =(2/h-1)||g_u-g_v||^2-(2/h)(Q_uv+Q_vu).           (2.2)\n\nThis norm drop is nonnegative for0<h<=2, with its complete original-row\ncertificate retained. Its sign is NOT asserted for an arbitrary long step.\n\n## 3. General off-root primitive join: the missing term\n\nLet l,r be any nonnegative words, with capacities\nP=1+sum l and C=1+sum r. They need not already be primitive for the\nidentity in this section. Insert the actual bridge1+t, where0<=t<C.\nWrite p for the bridge query index, z for the terminal index, and j for\nthe nonterminal right-child indices, with physical steps r_j.\nThus x_z=x_p-(1+t)g_p-sum_j r_j g_j.\nPut\n\n T=P+C+t, K=(2P+C+t)/(C-t),\n Delta=PC-t(P+C+t), R=(P+t)Delta/(C-t),\n E=sum_j r_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+t Q_(p,z)\n                                      +sum_j r_j Q_(j,z).       (3.1)\n\nThen the exact identity, on the actual original formal space, is\n\n V_(l,1+t,r)=V_l+K V_r-(K-1)E\n                              +R(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2).         (3.2)\n\nNo changed trajectory at an exact-root bridge appears here. All child\nenergies use their actual positions in the shortened or lengthened word.\n\nHere is the complete coefficient audit. Writing G=sum_j r_j g_j, expansion\nof E cancels all mixed gradient terms. Its value part is\n\n 2sum_j r_j f_j+(t-C)f_p+(2-C-t)f_z,\n\nand its gradient part is\n\n sum_j r_j(1-r_j)||g_j||^2\n       +[(C-t)/2-t^2]||g_p||^2+(C+t)||g_z||^2/2.    (3.3)\n\nFor example, in sum r_j Q_(j,p) the position and squared-difference\nterms combine to t<G,g_p>+||G||^2/2\n+sum r_j(1-r_j)||g_j||^2/2+(C-1)||g_p||^2/2.\nThe analogous sum r_j Q_(j,z) contributes -||G||^2/2\n+sum r_j(1-r_j)||g_j||^2/2-<G,g_z>+(C-1)||g_z||^2/2.\nQ_(z,p) contributes t<g_z,g_p>+<G,g_z>\n+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2, and t Q_(p,z) contributes\n-t(t+1/2)||g_p||^2-t<g_p,G>+t||g_z||^2/2-t<g_p,g_z>.\nThese four displayed expressions sum to (3.3).\n\nThe choice (K-1)(C-t)=2(P+t) matches every value coefficient in (3.2).\nAfter that cancellation the two remaining gradient coefficients in\nV_parent-V_l-K V_r+(K-1)E are\n\n P^2-Kt^2=R,\n KC^2-T^2=-R.                                      (3.4)\n\nThe first equality follows by multiplying by C-t and factoring\n(P+t)[PC-t(P+C+t)]; the second follows since the two expressions sum to0.\nThis proves (3.2) with every value, gradient, and mass term accounted for.\n\nAt Delta=0, one recovers the accepted exact-root identity, including\nK=(T/C)^2. Off that root, discarding R is unjustified. If Delta>=0\nand the actual bridge-plus-right-child history has a certified gradient\nnorm drop, (3.2) does give a valid shortened-bridge primitive certificate.\nThe following application proves that drop from two actual short steps.\n\n## 4. The literal H2: signs and complete multiplier array\n\nSet C=1+A=2+a in this section, t=beta, and define\n\n K=(3C+beta)/(C-beta),\n Delta=C^2-beta(2C+beta), R=(C+beta)Delta/(C-beta),\n delta_A=2-A^2.                                    (4.1)\n\nAll these parameters refer to the ACTUAL H2=(A,B,A).\nOne has1<A<sqrt(2), 1<B<2, C>beta, K>1, delta_A>0.\nThe sign of Delta needs the actual bridge comparison, not just B<2.\nUsing a=s(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and2s+s^2=1 gives\n\n s C-beta=epsilon(1+epsilon-2s^2)/(1+epsilon)>0,\n Delta=(s C-beta)(lambda C+beta)>0.                 (4.2)\n\nThis proves beta<s C without changing either leaf step.\n\nFor original indices0,1,2,3, equation (3.2) becomes\n\n V_H2=V_(A;0,1)+K V_(A;2,3)\n       -(K-1)(A Q_21+Q_31+beta Q_13+A Q_23)\n       +R(||g_1||^2-||g_3||^2).                    (4.3)\n\nApply (2.2) to the actual bridge B between1,2 and to the actual last\nleaf A between2,3, then use (2.1) on the two leaves. The result is\n\n V_H2=-sum_(i!=j) Lambda_ij Q_ij\n        +d0||g_0-g_1||^2+d1||g_1-g_2||^2\n                              +d2||g_2-g_3||^2,    (4.4)\n\nwhere\n\n d0=delta_A,\n d1=R(2/B-1)=R epsilon/B,\n d2=K delta_A+R(2/A-1),                             (4.5)\n\nand the complete nonzero multiplier list is\n\n Lambda_01=2,\n Lambda_10=2(1+A),\n Lambda_12=2R/B,\n Lambda_21=(K-1)A+2R/B,\n Lambda_13=(K-1)beta,\n Lambda_31=K-1,\n Lambda_23=2K+(K-1)A+2R/A,\n Lambda_32=2K(1+A)+2R/A.                            (4.6)\n\nAll other entries, including every diagonal entry, are zero. Every entry\nin (4.6) and every coefficient in (4.5) is positive. The full remainder\nis the four-gradient path quadratic, with matrix\n\n [ d0,    -d0,       0,     0;\n  -d0, d0+d1,     -d1,     0;\n    0,    -d1,  d1+d2,   -d2;\n    0,      0,    -d2,    d2 ].                     (4.7)\n\nIt is PSD on the entire original four-gradient space because its\nquadratic form is exactly the three squared differences in (4.4).\nIts kernel is the constant-gradient subspace; no equality among those\nformal gradients has been imposed. The original initial-position row is\nzero, consistently with the primitive criterion in the accepted fact.\n\nAs an independent flow audit, the function coefficients in\n-sum Lambda_ij Q_ij are (2A,2B,2A,-2S2). For the bridge coefficient,\n-2A+(K-1)(C-beta)=-2A+2(C+beta)=2B. The last nonterminal\ncoefficient is -2(K-1)A+2KA=2A. Thus no function-value term is hidden.\n\nThere is a simple uniform reserve. Since epsilon<1, s>1/4, and\nA=sqrt(2)-2s epsilon/(1+epsilon),\n\n delta_A=[2s epsilon/(1+epsilon)](sqrt(2)+A)>epsilon/2.\n\nSince s<1/2, (4.2) gives sC-beta>epsilon/4 and lambda C+beta>4.\nHence Delta>epsilon and R>epsilon. With B<2, this proves\n\n d0>=epsilon^2/2, d1>=epsilon^2/2, d2>=epsilon^2/2,\n V_H2 >= (epsilon^2/2)sum_(i=0)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2. (4.8)\n\nThe bounds are deliberately conservative; they are symbolic inequalities,\nnot arithmetic or numerical evaluations.\n\n## 5. Projection bodies, optimum normalization, and V exactly\n\nLet D be any nonempty compact convex subset of a finite-dimensional\nEuclidean space containing0. Define\n\n P_D(x)=argmin_(g in D)||x-g||^2,\n F_D(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),\n U_d(x)=max_(g in D)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2), d>0,\n T_h(x)=x-h P_D(x).                                (5.1)\n\nSquared-distance minimization along every segment in D gives\n<x-P_D(x),g-P_D(x)><=0 for every g in D; expansion proves sufficiency.\nAdding this condition for two projections gives\n||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2<=<x-y,P_D(x)-P_D(y)>.\nThus P_D is1-Lipschitz. Completing the square gives the unique maximizer\nP_D(x) in F_D. The maximizing inequalities bound the first-order remainder\nby ||t||||P_D(x+t)-P_D(x)||<=||t||^2, proving grad F_D=P_D.\nTherefore F_D is finite, convex, differentiable, and1-smooth.\n\nThe minimum is0 and the complete minimizer set is\n\n K_D={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in D}.                  (5.2)\n\nIf z is in K_D, all candidates for F_D(z) are nonpositive and g=0 attains0.\nIf <z,g>>0 for some g, sufficiently small positive multiples of g belong\nto D and give positive objective. This proves both directions.\nFor every z_star in K_D and every M>0, completion of the square gives\n\n U_M(x)<=||x-z_star||^2/M.                           (5.3)\n\nIndeed2<x,g>-M||g||^2<=2<x-z_star,g>-M||g||^2\n<=||x-z_star||^2/M for each g in D. This bound uses the true minimizer\ndistance, including when0 is not a closest minimizer.\n\nFor an actual word trajectory, write x=x_0, y=x_N, g_i=P_D(x_i).\nHere f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, so telescoping the actual squared\ndistances in (1.2) gives the EXACT identity\n\n V_h=||x||^2-||y+S P_D(y)||^2.                      (5.4)\n\nIn detail, ||x||^2-||y||^2=2sum h_i<x_i,g_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2.\nSubstitution in V_h leaves this expression minus2S f_N\nminus S(S+1)||g_N||^2, which is (5.4). No s-composability definition\nor equality between mass and product is invoked.\n\nAlso every original finite row becomes\nQ_ij=<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><=0. All rows in (4.6) are therefore actual\nsame-body projection normals, including the two nonlocal terminal rows.\n\n## 6. Exact U gap with the actual maximizer remainder\n\nFor any complete word of mass S and any d>=1, put\n\n M=d+2S, q=P_D(y/d), g=P_D(y), p0=P_D(x/M),\n zbar=[x-y+(d+S)q]/M,\n Rmax=M||p0-zbar||^2+2<x-Mp0,p0-zbar>.               (6.1)\n\nThe point zbar is in D: x-y=sum h_i g_i, and the nonnegative weights\nh_i and d+S sum to M. All these points use the same fixed body.\nProjection optimality at p0 gives Rmax>=0, term by term.\n\nThere is the exact identity requested in the assignment:\n\n U_M(x)-U_d(y)\n   =[||x||^2-||y+S q||^2]/M+Rmax.                  (6.2)\n\nTo verify it, the difference between the initial maximum at p0 and its\nvalue at zbar is exactly Rmax. Moreover x-M zbar=y-(d+S)q. Expanding\nthe value at zbar and subtracting2<y,q>-d||q||^2 gives the numerator\n||x||^2-||y||^2-2S<y,q>-S^2||q||^2, divided by M.\n\nFor1<=d1<d2, let q_i=P_D(y/d_i). Comparing each maximizer with the\nother feasible point gives\n\n d1(||q_1||^2-||q_2||^2)\n <=2<y,q_1-q_2><=d2(||q_1||^2-||q_2||^2).\n\nThe squared-norm difference must be nonnegative, since otherwise the\ndisplayed lower bound would exceed its upper bound. The inner-product\ndifference is then nonnegative too. Consequently\n\n Lrad=2S<y,g-q>+S^2(||g||^2-||q||^2)>=0,\n U_M(x)-U_d(y)=(V_h+Lrad)/M+Rmax.                   (6.3)\n\nCombining the explicit original-row identity (4.4) with (6.3) gives\nthe complete candidate H2 equality and lower bound\n\n U_(d+2S2)(x)-U_d(T_H2 x)\n   =[-sum Lambda_ij Q_ij+sum_(i=0)^2 d_i||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2\n                                                    +Lrad]/(d+2S2)+Rmax\n   >= [epsilon^2/(2(d+2S2))]sum_(i=0)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.       (6.4)\n\nThis holds at arbitrary starts, arbitrary finite dimension, and EVERY\nd>=1, including the low-d regime left unpaid previously. It also retains\nthe actual maximization and radial reserves rather than silently spending\nthem twice. At d=1, U_1=2F_D, so (5.3) gives\n\n 2F_D(T_H2 x)<=dist(x,K_D)^2/(1+2S2).                (6.5)\n\nScaling f=L F_D and using steps h/L preserves the trajectory and the W=2R\nnormalization. The projection-class implication is correctly normalized.\n\n## 7. Finite H2 full-function consequence, without a class restriction\n\nThe certificate (4.4) is valid for all convex1-smooth f, not only F_D.\nTranslate a selected minimizer to0 and subtract its value. Let\nu=(h_0,...,h_(N-1),S+1), whose sum is Dmass=1+2S.\nThe accepted identity from04219554e41d9750 is\n\n V_h=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2Dmass f_N\n                                      -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.       (7.1)\n\nIts direct audit is also short: the last vector is y-(S+1)g_N;\nexpanding the terminal optimum row cancels its mixed term, and the\nremaining expression is (1.2) after telescoping squared distances.\nThe weights and -Q_(i,star) are nonnegative. Thus V_H2>=0 proves\nW_3(H2)<=1/(1+2S2), uniformly over the full original function class.\n\nThe matching lower example is the scalar Huber function with threshold\ntau=1/(1+2S2): f(t)=t^2/2 for |t|<=tau and\nf(t)=tau|t|-tau^2/2 outside. It is convex,1-smooth, and uniquely minimized\nat0. Start at1. Every query is in the positive linear region since every\nprefix mass is at most S2 and1-S2*tau=(1+S2)/(1+2S2)>=tau.\nThus x_3=1-S2*tau and2f(x_3)=2tau-(2S2+1)tau^2=tau.\nThis proves the finite equality claimed in the verdict, within this draft.\nIt supplies no all-depth schedule bound.\n\n## 8. Higher joins: explicit signed debt and its scale\n\nKeep the SAME enclosing m. For the join forming H_(j+1), put\nC=C_j=1+S_j, k=k_j=a*c^j, and T=2C+k. Its mass is T-1.\nThe general identity (3.2) has\n\n K_j=(3C+k)/(C-k),\n Delta_j=C^2-k(2C+k), R_j=(C+k)Delta_j/(C-k),\n V_parent=V_left+K_j V_right-(K_j-1)E_j\n                             +R_j(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2).       (8.1)\n\nHere E_j is precisely (3.1), on the actual two child histories and bridge.\nAll its weights are nonnegative. No radius or child parameters reset.\n\nThe needed scalar mass facts can be derived symbolically. From the word\ndefinition C_(j+1)=2C_j+a*c^j and C_0=1. With v=a/(c-2),\n\n C_j=(1-v)2^j+v c^j, 0<v<1,\n k_j/C_j=a/[v+(1-v)(2/c)^j] <c-2.                  (8.2)\n\nSubstitution proves the displayed closed form. Since c>lambda>2 and\nc(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta, one has0<a<c-2 and hence0<v<1.\nThe ratio in (8.2) increases with j. Also lambda<49/20 and\nepsilon<1/100 give c<5/2, so k_j/C_j<1/2 and K_j<7.\n\nAt j=1 the H2 comparison is Delta_1>0 from (4.2). At j=2,\nC_2=5-epsilon+2s(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and k_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),\nso, using lambda=5s+2s^2,\n\n k_2-s C_2=epsilon[s+4s^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda epsilon]>0.       (8.3)\n\nIndeed epsilon<1/8, s>2/5, and lambda<5/2 imply\nlambda epsilon<5/16<2/5<s. Monotonicity in (8.2) then gives\nDelta_j<0 for EVERY j>=2. Thus the primitive sign genuinely changes\nat the H3 join, not at H2.\n\nThe magnitude is uniformly controlled. The current assignment expressly gives zero fact_submit credit.\nEvery new lemma used below is proved within this report; no old unverified\nfinding is adopted as an accepted predecessor.\n\nThere are exactly TWO substantive mechanisms. Mechanism1 derives the exact\nsigned interval map and the tube, then proves a uniform geometric capture\nlaw for complete children.
